# POLCO TOURS — Decision & Change Log

Mirror of the governance record in the master design package. **Living-document
mandate (DR-007):** every structural change (data model, module boundary,
permission, business rule) and every integration change (add/remove/reconfigure
an external service) is recorded here with a dated entry, the affected volume(s)
updated, and the DR id referenced in the PR — before merge. Enforced by the PR
template and the Definition of Done (Vol. 10 §10.3).

## Approved decisions

| ID | Date | Decision | Affects |
|----|------|----------|---------|
| DR-001 | 2026-07-07 | Stack: Next.js full-stack (App Router, TS) + Neon PostgreSQL + Prisma, modular monolith. | V5, V6, V9, V11 |
| DR-002 | 2026-07-07 | Payment gateway locked to DPO Pay (hosted page, v6, SAQ-A). Commercial terms pending (OI-01). | V3, V7, V8, V11 |
| DR-003 | 2026-07-07 | Brand confirmed: `polcotours` for website + all social handles; polcotours.com primary. | V1, V11 |
| DR-004 | 2026-07-07 | Deployment on Vercel for all environments; GitHub → Vercel CI/CD. | V9 |
| DR-005 | 2026-07-07 | Launch tenancy: single operator "Lam" (Namibia + DRC); Lam holds SUPERADMIN. Multi-tenant RLS retained. | V4, V6 |
| DR-006 | 2026-07-07 | Per-country effective-dated tax (DRC 16% / Namibia 15%), superseding flat 16%. | V1, V2, V3 |
| DR-007 | 2026-07-07 | Living-document mandate adopted (this policy). | All |
| DR-008 | 2026-07-07 | Phase 0 foundation scaffolded: repo, CI (GitHub→Vercel), Prisma schema + RLS, Better Auth + RBAC skeleton, design tokens, tax table, observability baseline. Implements DR-001/004/005/006. | V5, V6, V9, V10 |
| DR-009 | 2026-07-08 | Security-driven dependency bump on the DR-001 stack: Next.js 15.1.6 → 15.5.20, better-auth → 1.6.23, zod → 4.4.3, Playwright → 1.61.1. No stack change, patched releases of already-approved majors. | V9 |
| DR-010 | 2026-07-08 | OI-04 resolved: object storage on **Vercel Blob**, region `fra1` (matches DR-004 hosting region). Chosen over Cloudflare R2 / AWS S3 to avoid a second infra vendor/credential model alongside the existing Vercel deployment. Passport/visa documents (NFR: encryption + short-lived signed URLs + access logging, DB stores references only) will use this store starting when document upload lands (Phase 2). | V8, V9 |
| DR-011 | 2026-07-08 | Phase 1 Increment 1 (booking core, no payments): `TourPackage` extended with real catalog fields; new `Departure` (scheduled instance, own capacity) and `Booking` models — a "hold" is `Booking.status=HELD` + `holdExpiresAt`, not a separate table, using DB lazy expiry (no Redis/QStash for this increment). New permissions `booking.confirm`/`booking.cancel`. New tourists auto-join the primary org (Lam) at signup via a better-auth hook. DPO payments/invoicing/notifications/i18n explicitly deferred to later increments (OI-01 still open). | V1, V4, V6, V7 |
| DR-012 | 2026-07-09 | Phase 1 Increment 2 (invoicing + stubbed DPO payments): new `invoicing` module — `Invoice` (1:1 with `Booking`) + `Payment` (sub-concept, per DR-011's fold-into-one-module precedent, not a sibling module) — snapshots tax jurisdiction/rate at invoice-issue time from the platform-wide `TaxRate` table (DR-006) via new `src/lib/tax.ts` (its first reader). New business rule: 40%/60% deposit/balance split on the post-tax total, half-up rounding, `balance = total - deposit` (no independent rounding, avoids drift). DPO integration stubbed behind a `PaymentGateway` interface (charter rule 8) — a staff-only route manually resolves a `PENDING` payment to `SUCCEEDED`/`FAILED`, standing in for DPO's future webhook; only the adapter changes when OI-01's commercial terms land, no caller does. Booking confirmation remains deliberately uncoupled from invoice/payment status this increment. New permissions `invoice.read`/`payment.initiate`/`payment.resolve` (tourist gets the first two, only staff get `payment.resolve` — anti-fraud). `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo; "Affects" below only tags ids per prior-DR convention. | V1, V4, V6, V7 |
| DR-013 | 2026-07-09 | Phase 1 Increment 3 (notifications + notification-content i18n): new `notifications` module (`domain`/`gateway`/`service`/`index`, deliberately no `repository.ts` -- owns no table, delivery outcomes recorded via the existing `audit()` lib, not a bespoke log model) wired into booking confirm/cancel and invoicing payment-resolve. Real (not permanently-stubbed) HTTP adapters for Resend (email), WhatsApp Cloud API, and Africa's Talking (SMS) behind a shared `NotificationChannelGateway` interface (charter rule 8) -- each gated by its own env var(s), throwing before any network attempt when unconfigured (true in every environment today), so the WhatsApp→SMS→email fallback chain always degrades gracefully and a channel outage never fails a booking. New `User.phone`/`preferredLocale` (`Locale` enum EN/FR) columns + a `profile.write` self-service `PATCH /users/me` (every role except `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER`, an interpretive reading of BR-10). i18n scope this increment is notification templates only (EN/FR) -- existing `Errors.*` API error messages stay English-only, deferred (would require threading locale through `AuthContext`). | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-014 | 2026-07-09 | First authenticated frontend: staff-only pilot dashboard (`src/app/staff/...`) for Lam to manage bookings/invoices/payments, sequenced ahead of a future tourist-facing site. Mounted Better Auth's own HTTP route for the first time (`src/app/api/auth/[...all]/route.ts`, `toNextJsHandler`) -- previously configured but never wired up. New `authService.getUserByEmail` (thin wrapper, no new DB capability) so staff can book on behalf of an existing tourist found by email. Business rule: staff may only book for tourists who already have an account this increment -- creating an account for a brand-new, never-signed-up client ("lead" capture) is explicitly deferred, since `Booking.touristUserId` is a hard FK and no such capability exists yet. Real payment collection during the pilot is still manual (`payment.resolve`) -- the DPO gateway remains stubbed (OI-01 still open); a "payment link" in the UI is real, but doesn't yet move real money. New CI job runs Playwright e2e (previously installed but never invoked in CI) against its own Postgres bootstrap, mirroring the `quality` job's setup. | V4, V5, V7, V9 |
| DR-015 | 2026-07-09 | Staff booking-setup wizard: extends the DR-014 "new booking" staff flow into a multi-step wizard (still staff-only; a tourist self-serve equivalent is a separate future increment). New tenant-scoped tables: `Traveler` (one per seat; exactly one `isTourLead=true` per booking regardless of group size; captures name/age/sex/nationality/ID-or-passport-number/phone/disabilities/allergies/drinkPreference), `Document` (generic passport-metadata store, reusable by Phase 2 visa documents -- `kind` is a plain string, not an enum, so adding `VISA` later needs no migration), `AddonService` (new org-scoped catalog concept in the `catalog` module -- priced photography/videography/translator/visa-assistance), `BookingAddon` (selection, price/currency snapshotted at selection time, same pattern as `Booking.priceMinor`). New `Booking.addonsFinalizedAt`, stamped by the add-ons wizard step (including choosing none) -- gates `bookingService.getBillableTotal`, the new cross-module entry point `invoicing` now calls instead of reading `Booking.priceMinor` directly, so an invoice's subtotal always includes finalized add-ons and can never be created before the traveler manifest + tour-lead passport + add-ons steps are all complete (a behavior change to `GET /bookings/{bookingId}/invoice`, now 409 until then). First real exercise of DR-010's object-storage decision: new `documents` module wraps `@vercel/blob` (`access: 'private'` -- no public URL ever exists for a passport; retrieval uses the SDK's authenticated `get()`, not a bare fetch) behind a `BlobGateway` interface (charter rule 8); passport bytes are only ever served back through an authenticated, audited `GET /bookings/{bookingId}/travelers/{travelerId}/passport` route -- the Blob pathname itself never reaches a client. RBAC change: `TOUR_OPERATOR` gains `documents.write` (previously read-only) so staff can upload a passport on a tourist's behalf. New static `src/lib/country-codes.ts` (no new dependency, charter rule 4) powers the traveler form's phone-country-code/flag and nationality pickers. `docs/design-package/` still doesn't exist in-repo to actually update -- flagged again, not fabricated. See OI-08 below: this repo's CI/e2e environments have no `BLOB_READ_WRITE_TOKEN`, so real end-to-end passport upload is only verified with the Blob gateway mocked at the test boundary (`tests/api/booking-setup.api.test.ts`); the Playwright e2e coverage deliberately stops at the passport step's upload form rather than submitting a real file. | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |

| DR-016 | 2026-07-09 | Tourist self-serve site: guest checkout, no accounts. Confirmed directly: clients never sign up -- only operational roles (admins, guides, drivers, vehicle owners, visa facilitators) get real accounts. Public browse (`/packages`, `/quiz`) has no session at all -- new `catalogService.listPublicPackages`/`getPublicPackageWithDepartures`/`getPublicDepartureDetail`/`getQuizResults` resolve a new `src/lib/primary-org.ts` (`getPrimaryOrgId`, single-tenant launch, DR-005) themselves rather than deriving org from `ctx`; the `databaseHooks.user.create.before` auto-join hook (DR-011) now calls the same helper instead of its own inline query. New `TourPackage.tags PackageTag[]` (`WILDLIFE/ADVENTURE/RELAXATION/FAMILY/CULTURE/LUXURY/BUDGET`) powers the quiz's `scorePackagesForQuiz` (tag-overlap ranking, alphabetical-by-title tiebreak -- deliberately never price, since packages span 4 currencies with no FX conversion anywhere in this app). Booking itself uses better-auth's bundled `anonymous` plugin (already part of the approved DR-001 stack, not a new dependency) -- `authClient.signIn.anonymous()` mints a real, cookie-backed session with zero password/email UX, so the entire existing `bookingService`/`invoicingService`/`documentsService` stack (already correct for a TOURIST-shaped `AuthContext`, confirmed by inspection) works for a guest completely unchanged; `disableDeleteAnonymousUser: true` because these guests never "convert" to a real account. New `User.isAnonymous` (better-auth-managed column) and `UpdateProfileInput.name` (so the guest contact-info step can set a name via the existing `PATCH /users/me`, DR-013). New `Booking.confirmationCode` (short, unique, generated at hold-creation for every booking) is revealed to a guest once the setup wizard is complete and a payment has been initiated -- the public "find my booking" page (`bookingService.lookupByConfirmationCode`, code + tour lead's last name, two factors standing in for session auth) is deliberately read-only, no cancel/mutate action reachable from it, and rate-limited via a new `countRecentAuditEvents` helper (`src/lib/audit.ts`) counting recent `booking.lookup_failed` entries per IP -- a crude, infra-free stand-in until real rate-limiting exists. An earlier draft of this design used a bare `bookingId` embedded in wizard URLs as the guest's whole "session"; an adversarial review flagged that as a real leak vector (Referer headers, browser history, shared devices) for data this sensitive (passport numbers, disability/allergy info, payment status), which is why the anonymous-session approach replaced it before implementation. Deliberately not built this increment: new public `/api/v1` REST routes (Server Components call catalog/booking services directly, same convention as the staff dashboard); a staff-facing package-management UI (packages/departures are still only creatable via the existing `/api/v1/catalog/*` routes -- a pre-existing gap, more visible now that there's a real consumer, `prisma/seed.ts` seeds a demo catalog to cover it for now); a real DPO redirect (still stubbed, OI-01) -- "Pay deposit" ends at an honest PENDING state, same as the staff flow. | V1, V4, V5, V6, V7, V8 |

| DR-017 | 2026-07-10 | Phase 2 Increment 1 (fleet + compliance): new `Vehicle` (plate/make/model/year/vehicleType -- open-ended string, not an enum, since types vary too much across NA/DRC/seatCapacity/status, optional `ownerId` FK to a `VEHICLE_OWNER` user, null = operator-owned) and `DriverProfile` (1:1 with a `DRIVER`-role `User`; compliance data doesn't belong on the `auth`-owned `User` model per the module-boundary rule) tables, plus new `Document.expiresAt`/`vehicleId`/`driverProfileId` columns (nullable, unused by existing `PASSPORT` rows) for vehicle registration/insurance/inspection and driver-license compliance documents. New `src/modules/fleet/` (`domain`/`repository`/`service`/`index`, same shape as `catalog`/`documents`) with a pure `complianceStatus(expiresAt, now)` rule (`MISSING`/`VALID`/`EXPIRING_SOON` within 30 days/`EXPIRED`) -- the only actual business rule this increment. `documentsService` generalized: `uploadPassport` is now a thin wrapper over a new `uploadDocument(ctx, { kind, ... })`, with a per-`kind` content-type/size validation table in `domain.ts` (compliance kinds allow a PDF or a phone-photo JPEG/PNG, unlike passport's PDF-only); confirmed safe by auditing all 3 existing `uploadPassport` call sites, none of which touch the module's constants/types directly. New permissions `fleet.read`/`fleet.write`: `TOUR_OPERATOR` gets both; `VEHICLE_OWNER`/`DRIVER` get `fleet.read` only, scoped in `fleet/service.ts` to their own vehicle(s)/profile (404, not 403, on a mismatch -- anti-BOLA, same convention as `invoicing/service.ts`). New `/api/v1/fleet/{vehicles,drivers}` routes (+ nested `.../documents` upload/stream) and a staff dashboard `src/app/staff/(dashboard)/fleet/...` (list/create/edit vehicles and drivers, compliance-doc upload with a status badge), following the DR-014 `page.tsx`+`actions.ts` convention. Deliberately deferred to later Phase 2 increments: linking a vehicle/driver to a `Departure`/`Booking` (that's "Assignments" -- `rbac.ts`'s pre-existing, previously-unused `assignment.write` permission anticipated this sequencing), GPS tracking, automated compliance-expiry notifications (the data model only makes expiry computable, no alert wiring), and guide compliance records (`TOUR_GUIDE` isn't a fleet concept). `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- "Affects" below only tags volume ids per the established convention, flagged rather than fabricated. | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |

| DR-018 | 2026-07-10 | Phase 2 Increment 2 (assignments): new `Assignment` table linking a `Departure` to the vehicle/driver/guide staffing it -- `@@unique([departureId, vehicleId])` so a departure can have several assignments (one per vehicle) once its capacity exceeds one vehicle's `seatCapacity`, per this session's confirmed scope. New `src/modules/assignment/` (`domain`/`repository`/`service`/`index`) with the actual business rule this increment: a pure `departuresOverlap(a, b)` check (a departure with no `endDate` is treated as a same-day trip) prevents double-booking -- a vehicle or driver cannot be assigned to two departures with overlapping dates. `createAssignment` also rejects an inactive (`MAINTENANCE`/`RETIRED`/`SUSPENDED`) vehicle/driver and a `guideUserId` not referencing a `TOUR_GUIDE` in the same organization (checked explicitly here since `authService.getUser` is a raw, org-unscoped lookup, same convention as `getUserByEmail`). New `fleet/service.ts` addition: `getMyDriverProfile` (resolves the caller's own `DriverProfile` by `userId`, needed because a `DRIVER`'s assignments are keyed by `driverProfileId`, not `userId`). New permission `assignment.read` (`assignment.write` already existed, unused, since DR-011) -- `TOUR_OPERATOR` gets both; `TOUR_GUIDE`/`DRIVER`/`VEHICLE_OWNER` get `assignment.read` only, scoped in `assignment/service.ts`'s `listMyAssignments` to their own guide/driver-profile/vehicle records. Confirmed with the user this session: staff-managed only -- those three roles have never had staff-dashboard access (the layout's baseline gate requires `booking.confirm`, which only `TOUR_OPERATOR`/`SUPERADMIN` hold), so `listMyAssignments` + `GET /api/v1/assignments/mine` exist API-only; a real self-service "my schedule" portal (which would need to widen that shared gate) is deliberately deferred to its own future increment. New `/api/v1/departures/{id}/assignments` (list/create) and `/api/v1/assignments/{id}` (delete) routes, plus the first staff-facing departures UI in the repo (`src/app/staff/(dashboard)/departures/...` -- packages/departures were API-only until now, a gap DR-016 flagged): a package → departure browse page (reusing `catalogService.listPackages`/`listDepartures`, no new catalog methods) and a departure-detail page showing current assignments, a "seats covered" tally (informational only, not enforced), and a form to add an assignment (vehicle/driver pickers filtered to `ACTIVE`, guide by email). Deliberately not built: in-place reassignment (swap vehicle/driver via delete + recreate, which re-runs all validation cleanly), a global cross-departure assignments dashboard, and (as above) the self-service portal. `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |

| DR-019 | 2026-07-10 | Phase 2 Increment 3 (visa documents): `visa.process`/`immigration.read` had existed in `rbac.ts` since Phase 0 and were never used anywhere -- fully greenfield, unlike fleet/assignments' partial scaffolding. Two scope decisions confirmed with the user: (1) a **full status workflow**, not a bare document attach -- new `VisaApplication` per `Traveler` (`SUBMITTED -> APPROVED/REJECTED`, no resubmission after a decision this increment) rather than a bare `visaDocumentId` mirroring `passportDocumentId`; (2) **real country-scoping** for `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER` (BR-10 says "country-scoped" but nothing tied an officer to Namibia vs. DRC before this) -- new `User.assignedCountry`, set via new admin-only `authService.assignOfficerCountry` (first real use of `admin.all`, also previously unused). Key design finding: `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER` holds no `booking.read`, so `VisaApplication` **snapshots** the traveler's identity (first/last name, nationality, idOrPassportNumber) and the destination `country` at submission time, rather than joining live into the `booking` module -- granting `booking.read` just to read traveler names would violate BR-10's "strictly read-only, single-permission footprint" already documented in `rbac.ts`, and would expose every booking, not just visa-relevant travelers. New `src/modules/visa/` (`domain`/`repository`/`service`/`index`) with the one status-transition rule this increment, `canDecide` (`SUBMITTED` only), and an `OfficerVisaView` projection that deliberately excludes disabilities/allergies/phone/drinkPreference (data minimization for an immigration audience). `documentsService` gains the `'VISA'` kind (PDF or a phone-photo JPEG/PNG, same allowance as the DR-017 fleet compliance kinds) -- no other change, `uploadDocument`/`streamDocument` are already generic. New routes: `POST/GET .../bookings/{id}/travelers/{id}/visa(/submit|/decide|/document)`, `GET /immigration/visa-applications` (country-scoped; `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER` is forced to their own `assignedCountry`, any `?country=` they pass is ignored -- confirmed by a dedicated anti-BOLA test), and admin-only `PATCH /users/{id}/assign-country`. `VISA_FACILITATOR`/`IMMIGRATION_OFFICER` get no staff UI this increment (same discovery as Assignments' DR-018: the dashboard layout's baseline gate requires `booking.confirm`, which neither role holds) -- API-only, consistent with that precedent. One small addition for `TOUR_OPERATOR` (who does have dashboard access): a read-only "Visa" line per traveler on the existing booking-detail page. Deliberately deferred: resubmission after `REJECTED`, a staff UI for `VISA_FACILITATOR`/`IMMIGRATION_OFFICER`, and an officer-management UI (assigning a country is API-only, single current superadmin). `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |

| DR-020 | 2026-07-11 | Officer-management UI: closes the gap DR-019 explicitly deferred ("assigning a country is API-only, single current superadmin"). Two real screens, both reusing existing DR-019 capabilities with no new permission and no schema change: (1) `/staff/immigration` -- an `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER`'s own country-scoped visa queue, strictly read-only (BR-10 -- confirmed no decide/approve/reject action belongs here; that's `VISA_FACILITATOR`'s `visa.process` workflow via the existing booking-nested visa routes, untouched by this increment); an admin may pass `?country=` to filter or see every country. (2) `/staff/admin/officers` (`admin.all`) -- lists `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER` accounts in the org with an inline form to assign/reassign `assignedCountry` from the org's own `countries`, calling the existing `authService.assignOfficerCountry` directly. Creating a brand-new officer account stays CLI-only (`scripts/create-staff-user.ts`), same as DR-014's "staff can only book for an already-registered tourist" precedent -- deliberately out of scope. The one blocker requiring a real design decision: the `(dashboard)` layout's baseline gate hardcoded `booking.confirm`, which `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER` has never held -- it would have been redirected to `/staff/forbidden` before its own page's `immigration.read` check ever ran, the same gap DR-018/019 flagged and deferred for `TOUR_GUIDE`/`DRIVER`/`VEHICLE_OWNER`/`VISA_FACILITATOR`. Fixed by widening the gate to a new `rbac.ts` `isStaffRole(role)` ("any role except TOURIST") -- `requireStaffContext()` called with no permission now means "any staff role", while every nested page still gates on its own specific permission unchanged; `StaffNav` became permission-aware (`can(role, permission)` per link) since not every staff role can now open every link. Also closed two pre-existing compliance/audit gaps surfaced by giving these code paths a real UI that will actually be clicked repeatedly: `visaService.listForCountry`'s `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER` branch never audited its own reads despite `audit.ts`'s own docstring requiring it (BR-10); `authService.assignOfficerCountry` (an `admin.all` action) was never audited either, despite the security posture's STRIDE "Elevation" control saying `admin.all` actions are audited. Both now call `audit()`. No new `/api/v1` routes for the dashboard pages themselves (Server Components/Actions call `authService`/`visaService` directly, the established convention) except one small new read endpoint, `GET /api/v1/immigration/officers` (`admin.all`), added for API-level testability and consistency with `visa-applications`' sibling route -- backed by a new `authService.listOfficers`/`authRepository.listByRole`. `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-021 | 2026-07-11 | Guide/driver/vehicle-owner self-service "my schedule" portal: closes the gap DR-018/019/020 each independently deferred. No new permission (reuses `assignment.read`, already granted to `TOUR_GUIDE`/`DRIVER`/`VEHICLE_OWNER` since DR-018) and no schema change -- the dashboard-gate blocker DR-018 originally cited was already removed by DR-020's `isStaffRole` widening, so this increment is purely: enrich `GET /api/v1/assignments/mine`'s bare foreign keys into something displayable, and wire up a page. New `/staff/schedule` (Server Component, no `actions.ts` -- read-only), composing `assignmentService.listMyAssignments` + `catalogService.getDepartureDetail` + `authService.getUser` + two new `fleet` module methods directly in the page, same pattern the manager-side departure-detail page already established (no shared cross-module service method introduced). The one real design decision: `fleetService.getVehicle`/`getDriverProfile`'s existing anti-BOLA check (`src/modules/fleet/service.ts`) only allows a "fleet manager" role or the record's own `ownerId`/`userId` -- a `DRIVER` assigned to a vehicle they don't own would 404 trying to view it. New `fleetService.listVehiclesByIds`/`listDriverProfilesByIds` (public-interface addition) deliberately skip that filter, org-scoped only, mirroring `authService.getUser`'s existing "no internal permission check; the caller already gates" convention -- safe here because the caller only ever supplies IDs drawn from their own `listMyAssignments` result, not arbitrary input. New `StaffNav` entry ("My schedule", gated on `assignment.read`) also surfaces for `TOUR_OPERATOR`/`SUPERADMIN`/`PLATFORM_ADMIN` (who hold that permission too) -- harmless, renders the same empty state `listMyAssignments` already returns for any role outside the three target ones. Two real bugs caught in code review (this sandbox's Neon connectivity blocked running the test suite live): `catalogService.getDepartureDetail` 404s for a non-operator role once a departure leaves `SCHEDULED` status, which a `COMPLETED` trip in anyone's own assignment history would trigger routinely -- a plain `Promise.all` would have 500'd the whole page the first time; switched to `Promise.allSettled` and drop unresolvable rows. Separately, `TOUR_GUIDE` holds `assignment.read` but not `fleet.read` (only `DRIVER`/`VEHICLE_OWNER` do) -- the page now checks `can(ctx.role, 'fleet.read')` before calling the two new fleet methods and falls back to the existing "Unknown vehicle/driver" text otherwise, rather than granting `TOUR_GUIDE` a permission it's never needed. `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-022 | 2026-07-11 | Homepage interactive Africa/Namibia/DRC map (Phase B of a 10-item guest-site redesign batch): new dependencies `@visx/geo`, `@visx/responsive`, `@visx/tooltip`, `@visx/event`, `topojson-client`, `world-atlas` (+`@types/topojson-client` dev). Originally scoped as `react-simple-maps` + `topojson-client`, but `react-simple-maps@3.0.0` (and its abandoned 2023 `4.0.0-beta.6`) only declares React 16-18 in `peerDependencies` -- `npm install` fails outright with `ERESOLVE` against this repo's React 19. Switched to the `@visx` ecosystem instead (same underlying approach: D3-geo projections + topojson, rendered as SVG), since every needed `@visx/*` package cleanly declares `react: "^18.0.0 || ^19.0.0"` with zero install conflicts -- confirmed via `npm view ... peerDependencies` before installing, not asserted. New `src/components/AfricaMap.tsx` (client component -- needs hover/zoom interaction state): converts `world-atlas`'s `countries-110m.json` topojson to GeoJSON client-side via `topojson-client`'s `feature()` (kept as compact topojson in the shipped bundle rather than pre-expanding server-side, which would ship a larger already-expanded payload -- the whole reason topojson format exists). New `src/lib/africa-country-ids.ts` (ISO 3166-1 numeric-3 codes for the African Union's 55 members/territories, used only for map coloring, not a business rule) and `src/lib/country-facts.ts` (capital/language/currency/population/area reference data for Namibia and DR Congo only, labeled as approximate estimates, same "don't hardcode as ground truth" spirit as the tax/visa figures elsewhere in this app). Interaction: Africa (including Namibia/DRC) renders one highlight color at world-zoom; a "Zoom into Namibia & DR Congo" toggle button animates a `<g>` transform (CSS-transitioned scale+translate centered on the two countries' `@visx/geo`-computed centroids) and switches Namibia/DRC to a second, distinct fill color once zoomed; hovering either then shows a `@visx/tooltip` populated from `country-facts.ts`. Deliberately not built: real drag/scroll/pinch zoom (`@visx/zoom` was installed then removed -- a simple click-triggered CSS-transform zoom is simpler, avoids the common bad-UX scroll-jacking problem on an embedded decorative map, and needed no extra dependency once available); keyboard-accessible hover-equivalent for the tooltip. No new npm vulnerabilities introduced (`npm audit` before/after this increment is unchanged at 12, all pre-existing in dev-only tooling). No schema/permission change. `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V9 |

| DR-023 | 2026-07-12 | Real EN/FR i18n infrastructure + language switcher (Phase C of the same 10-item guest-site batch). New dependency `next-intl@4.13.2` -- confirmed React 19/Next 15 support via `npm view` before installing (learned from DR-022's `react-simple-maps` surprise). **Cookie-based locale, no `/en`/`/fr` URL-prefixed routing** -- this matches scaffolding already sitting in `src/middleware.ts`, which resolved a candidate locale (`locale` cookie, else `Accept-Language`) but only ever wrote it to an `x-locale` response header nothing read; fixed to instead seed the actual `locale` cookie on a visitor's first request. `User.preferredLocale` (the Prisma `Locale` enum, EN/FR) is a separate, unrelated concept used only to pick notification-template language (DR-013) -- not touched, not unified with the new guest-UI locale cookie. New `src/i18n/request.ts` (`getRequestConfig` reads the cookie, defaults `en`), `src/messages/{en,fr}.json` (namespaced `Nav`/`Footer`/`HomePage` keys only -- every other guest page stays plain hardcoded English, an explicitly incremental translation scope, not an oversight), `next.config.mjs` wrapped with `createNextIntlPlugin()`. New `src/app/(guest)/language-switcher.tsx` (hover-opens, not click) + `set-locale-action.ts` (a Server Action writing the cookie, then `router.refresh()`) -- once a visitor picks a language explicitly, that cookie wins over middleware's Accept-Language seed on every later request. `NextIntlClientProvider` wraps only `src/app/(guest)/layout.tsx`'s tree, not the true root layout -- the staff dashboard has never been in i18n scope and stays completely unaffected. Verified via dev-server curl requests with different `Cookie`/`Accept-Language` combinations (no browser tool exists in this sandbox to click the actual hover-menu) -- all three scenarios (no cookie/no header → English; explicit `locale=fr` cookie → French; `Accept-Language: fr` with no cookie → middleware seeds `locale=fr` and that same response already renders French) behaved correctly, no runtime errors. | V1, V9 |

| DR-024 | 2026-07-12 | Guided trip-planning journey, sites dropdown, quote-request flow, pay-in-full (Phase D, final phase of the 10-item guest-site batch). Schema: `BookingStatus` gains `QUOTE_REQUESTED`, `PaymentKind` gains `FULL` -- both purely additive `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE`, applied directly to the shared dev/production Neon database (no separate staging environment exists per DR-005) with explicit user confirmation, using the `neondb_owner` credential ephemerally (never written to `.env`). **Key design insight**: "request a quotation" needed no new booking-creation path -- by the time a guest reaches the payment step a `HELD` booking already exists and already passed its capacity check, so requesting a quote is just transitioning that same booking to `QUOTE_REQUESTED` (`bookingService.requestQuotation`, reusing `booking.cancel`'s permission/ownership shape rather than adding a new permission), which releases the seat via the existing `updateStatus`/capacity-allow-list behavior with zero new capacity-tracking logic. Per explicit user decision, staff may confirm a `QUOTE_REQUESTED` booking directly with no automatic seat re-check (accepted risk over a safer "must convert to hold first" design) -- mitigated only by a one-line UI caution next to Confirm, not a hard gate. New `PaymentKind.FULL` ("pay in full") is mutually exclusive with the deposit/balance split (`canInitiatePayment`), and a new `amountForPaymentKind` lookup replaces a fragile `kind === 'DEPOSIT' ? ... : ...` binary ternary that would have silently charged a `FULL` payment the balance amount. Both guest and staff booking-detail pages gained a "first payment decision" fork (Pay deposit / Pay in full / (guest only) Request a quotation, shown together only while `payments.length === 0`) -- a real bug caught in review before shipping: the deposit/balance buttons had no `booking.status === 'HELD'` gate at all (pre-existing on the deposit/balance path, newly relevant for `QUOTE_REQUESTED`), so a quote-requested booking would have still shown "Pay deposit"; fixed on both pages. New staff `/staff/quote-requests` queue page (same `Table`/`Badge` pattern as the bookings list, filtered client-side given DR-005's small org-wide booking volume). Guided journey: `BOOKING_WIZARD_STEPS`/`StepIndicator` (previously scoped to the 4-page booking wizard only) now spans the whole journey from `/quiz` through payment as one continuous progress bar, per explicit user choice of "one merged wizard" over a lighter-touch alternative -- implemented as extending the existing step-indicator/page-based flow rather than rewriting the already-tested traveler/passport/payment logic into a single new component; `/packages/[packageId]` (departure-picking) deliberately stays indicator-free since it's shared with plain browse-without-quiz traffic. New sites-to-visit quiz question (`src/lib/destination-sites.ts`, a static curated list -- no `Site`/`Destination` entity exists or was added) scores a substring match against package title/description additively on top of the existing tag score, same lightweight approach, no schema change. `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-025 | 2026-07-14 | Visa resubmission after rejection: closes the dead end DR-019 explicitly deferred ("no resubmission after a decision this increment"). **Design decision**: mutate the SAME `VisaApplication` row back to `SUBMITTED` on resubmit rather than adding a history/versioning table -- `travelerId` is DB-enforced `@unique` (strict 1:1 with `Traveler`), so a history table would mean either breaking that 1:1 or bolting on a child table joined to the same row, real complexity for a 3-state enum with one resubmission path. The durable "this was rejected once, for reason X, on date Y" record already lives forever in the append-only `audit_logs` table (INSERT-only RLS policy, no UPDATE/DELETE ever exists on it) via `audit('visa.decided'/'visa.resubmitted', ...)` -- the schema itself only needs to reflect current state. Two new additive columns on `visa_applications` (nullable/defaulted, no existing column touched): `rejectionReason String? @db.VarChar(500)` (new, set only on a REJECTED decide via a new optional `DecideVisaInput.reason`; without this, resubmission would be nearly meaningless since nobody could see what to fix) and `resubmissionCount Int @default(0)` (display-only counter). New `visaService.resubmitApplication`/`domain.canResubmit` (mirrors `canDecide` exactly: true only for `REJECTED`) resets `status: SUBMITTED`, `decidedAt: null`, `rejectionReason: null`, and **also nulls `documentId`** (a stale rejected document must stop 200'ing from `GET .../visa/document` after resubmission) and **bumps `submittedAt: new Date()`** (both `listForCountry`/`listAll` order by `submittedAt desc`, so without this a freshly-resubmitted application would sort as if it were old, defeating the point of surfacing it for re-review). New `POST .../visa/resubmit` route, same `visa.process` permission and `findTraveler` anti-BOLA check as `submit`/`decide` -- no RBAC change. `resubmissionCount` is added to `OfficerVisaView` too (bare count, enough signal that "this bounced before"), but `rejectionReason` is deliberately kept off that view to preserve its existing BR-10 data-minimization posture (same spirit as it already excluding disabilities/allergies/phone) -- the strictly-read-only officer role has no `visa.process` stake in *why* it was rejected. No `VisaStatus` enum change (still SUBMITTED/APPROVED/REJECTED, now cycling), so `status-tones.ts`'s exhaustive `VISA_STATUS_TONE` record needed no edit; no `rls.sql` change (its `visa_applications` policy is row-level via `organizationId` only, column-agnostic). Two new nullable/defaulted columns applied directly via `db push` to the shared dev/production Neon database (no staging environment per DR-005), with explicit user confirmation, using the `neondb_owner` credential ephemerally (never written to `.env`) -- same precedent as DR-024. Small addition to the existing read-only `TOUR_OPERATOR` "Visa" line on the staff booking-detail page to surface the reason/count; no new UI for `VISA_FACILITATOR` (still has no staff dashboard at all, same gap DR-019/020/021 already flagged -- `submit`/`decide`/`resubmit` all stay API-only for it). `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-026 | 2026-07-15 | Real superadmin user management + multi-role RBAC: replaces CLI-only staff account creation (`scripts/create-staff-user.ts`) with a real in-app flow, and lets a single user hold several simultaneous roles for the first time. **Multi-role design**: repurposes the `Membership` model (existed since Phase 0, never queried anywhere in `src/`) as the real role-set source of truth for staff accounts -- widened its unique constraint from `[userId, organizationId]` to `[userId, organizationId, role]` so multiple roles per user per org are possible. `User.role` stays a single "primary" role, unchanged, for tourist/guest self-signup (which never gets a `Membership` row). `AuthContext.role: Role` is renamed to `AuthContext.roles: Role[]` (the union of a user's `Membership.role` values plus `User.role`, deduped, always non-empty) -- a genuinely large mechanical refactor across `rbac.ts` (`can`/`assertCan`/`isStaffRole` now take `Role[]`, using union/"any role grants it" semantics), `route-guard.ts`, `staff-guard.ts`, and every service (`booking`, `catalog`, `fleet`, `assignment`, `documents`, `invoicing`, `visa`) plus three staff-page `actions.ts` files that validate a target user's role before assigning them as guide/driver/vehicle-owner (now checking the target's role set, not just their primary role) and `StaffNav`. `assignment/service.ts`'s `listMyAssignments` now unions results across every role a caller holds (e.g. both `TOUR_GUIDE` and `DRIVER`) instead of returning only the first matching role's list, since that's now a real possibility. New `authService.listUsers`/`createUser`/`deactivateUser` (`admin.all`, no new permission). **Soft delete**: `User.deletedAt` already existed and `authRepository`'s reads (`findUserByEmail`/`findUserById`/`listByRole`) and `resolveSession` already treated a `deletedAt`-set user as unauthenticated -- only the write side was missing; `deactivateUser` just sets it, and blocks self-deactivation. No explicit session-revocation step needed since `resolveSession` re-reads the DB on every request. **Generated passwords**: `createUser` generates a one-time password via `better-auth/crypto`'s `generateRandomString`, returned exactly once in the API/UI response and never persisted in plaintext; new `User.mustChangePassword` forces a redirect to a new `/staff/change-password` page (`staff-guard.ts`, sitting outside `(dashboard)` the same way `/staff/login` does, to avoid a redirect loop) until the user changes it via better-auth's own `changePassword` API. New `/staff/admin/users` page (list/create/deactivate), kept separate from the existing narrowly-scoped `/staff/admin/officers` (country assignment only). New `GET/POST /api/v1/users` + `DELETE /api/v1/users/{userId}` routes, all `admin.all`. New RLS policy for `organization_members` (the `Membership` table's real name) -- it existed since Phase 0 but, being unused, never got one either; now load-bearing for real authorization data, it needs the same tenant isolation every other org-scoped table has, plus a new `tests/rls.cross-tenant.memberships.test.ts`. Separately, per explicit user instruction: every existing `User` row was deleted (`scripts/reset-all-users.ts`) -- cascading, per `schema.prisma`'s `onDelete: Cascade`, to every `Membership`/`Session`/`Account`/`Booking` (and everything hanging off a booking)/`DriverProfile`; `Assignment.guideUserId`/`Vehicle.ownerId` survived `SET NULL`'d; `Document.uploadedByUserId`/`AuditLog.actorUserId` aren't real FKs and were left holding stale UUIDs (acceptable -- `AuditLog` is explicitly append-only and meant to outlive its actors) -- then a single bootstrap `SUPERADMIN` (`cyberpolco@gmail.com`) was created with a real, operator-chosen password (not a generated one, so no `mustChangePassword`). `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V5, V6, V7, V8 |

| DR-027 | 2026-07-15 | Bookings module v2 -- reconciles a new external spec against the existing booking system (DR-011/012/016/024). **`BookingStatus` replaced wholesale** (not additive, unlike every prior status change): `HELD/CONFIRMED/CANCELLED/EXPIRED/QUOTE_REQUESTED` -> `DRAFT/AWAITING_QUOTATION/QUOTATION_SENT/AWAITING_DEPOSIT/DEPOSIT_PAID/FULLY_PAID/CONFIRMED/IN_PROGRESS/COMPLETED/CANCELLED/REFUNDED`. `AWAITING_DEPOSIT` + `holdExpiresAt` is the new hold (was `HELD`); an expired hold lazily sweeps straight to `CANCELLED` -- there is no dedicated `EXPIRED` value in the new set, so that distinction survives only in the audit log (explicit user decision). Safe to do as a hard replace (vs. the usual `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE`) because the live DB had zero real `Booking` rows at the time (re-verified immediately before migrating) -- a `DROP TYPE`/recreate, not a value-by-value migration. **New booking origin**: `BookingOrigin` (`PREDEFINED_PACKAGE` | `TAILOR_MADE`) -- a bespoke trip request with no pre-existing `Departure` (`Booking.departureId` is now nullable), priced by staff afterward via a new `sendQuotation` (`AWAITING_QUOTATION -> QUOTATION_SENT`, sets `priceMinor`/`currency`, both now nullable on `Booking` until quoted) and accepted by the client via new `acceptQuotation` (`QUOTATION_SENT -> AWAITING_DEPOSIT`, same no-capacity-recheck accepted-risk posture as DR-024's existing quote flow -- there's no shared capacity to protect for a bespoke trip anyway). New `customCountry`/`customTravelStart`/`customTravelEnd`/`customDescription` fields (`TAILOR_MADE`-only) -- `customCountry` substitutes for a `Departure`'s package country when resolving the tax rate (`invoicingService.getOrCreateInvoiceForBooking`) and a visa application's destination (`visaService.submitApplication`), both of which previously assumed `departureId` was always present. New guest entry point `/tailor-made` (mirrors `/book/[departureId]`'s anonymous-session pattern) plus a staff `?tailorMade=1` branch on the existing manual-booking wizard. **New business-facing reference**: `Booking.bookingReference` (e.g. `POL-2026-000154`, from a plain Postgres sequence `booking_reference_seq`) -- coexists with, does not replace, the existing non-guessable `confirmationCode` (still the guest "find my booking" secret; a sequential id would be enumerable). **New `Booking.specialRequests`** (free text, addresses a spec gap -- no such field existed at any level before). **Payment status now drives booking status**: new `bookingService.recordPaymentReceived`, called from `invoicingService.resolvePayment`'s `SUCCEEDED` path (module-boundary-respecting, through booking's public interface only) -- `DEPOSIT` -> `DEPOSIT_PAID`, `BALANCE`/`FULL` -> `FULLY_PAID`. `confirm` now requires `DEPOSIT_PAID`/`FULLY_PAID` (previously confirmable straight off a hold/quote with no payment precondition at all). New staff-only `refund` (`CANCELLED -> REFUNDED`) -- status-only, no real payment-reversal mechanism exists yet (no `PaymentStatus.REFUNDED`/reversal `Payment` row); actual refund processing is flagged as future Payments-module territory. **Deposit split stays 40%/60%** (`splitDeposit`, DR-012) -- the new spec's "60% deposit" was explicit user-confirmed imprecise wording, not a real business-rule change. **RBAC**: `booking.create` stays as-is (`SUPERADMIN`/`PLATFORM_ADMIN`/`TOUR_OPERATOR`/`TOURIST`) rather than narrowing to the spec's literal "Super Admin or Platform Admin" -- per explicit user framing ("Tour operator = platform admin" for booking purposes in this single-operator launch), narrowing would have blocked Lam's actual walk-in/phone-booking workflow. New `sendQuotation`/`refund` reuse the existing staff-only `booking.confirm` permission (no new permission). New routes: `POST /bookings/tailor-made`, `POST /bookings/{id}/quotation`, `POST /bookings/{id}/quotation/accept`, `POST /bookings/{id}/refund`. Real bug caught mid-implementation: `prisma/seed.ts`'s `Membership` upsert (added by DR-026) used the raw unscoped `prisma` client, which now 403s against DR-026's own `organization_members` RLS policy -- fixed by routing it through `withOrg` like every other tenant-scoped write in that file. `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V5, V6, V7, V8 |

| DR-028 | 2026-07-15 | Packages Module: closes two related gaps against an external spec -- (1) the first-ever staff package-management UI, and (2) bridging an approved TAILOR_MADE booking (DR-027) into a real operational itinerary with resources assigned. **Package management**: new `TourPackage.packageReference` (`PKG-00034`-style, from a plain Postgres sequence `package_reference_seq`, coexists with the UUID `id` -- same pattern as `Booking.bookingReference`, DR-027). New `catalogService.deletePackage` (soft delete via the already-scaffolded-but-unused `deletedAt` column -- confirmed by explicit user choice over a real hard DELETE, since that would cascade to any real `Departure`/`Booking`/`Invoice` ever created from it) and `duplicatePackage` (clones the package definition only -- title/description/country/price/currency/durationDays/tags -- as a new DRAFT package with a fresh reference; deliberately no departures, per explicit user choice: old dates wouldn't make sense on a copy). New `/staff/packages` (list/create/edit) + `/staff/packages/[id]` (edit form + archive/delete/duplicate actions) -- CLAUDE.md had flagged since DR-016 that package creation/editing was only ever reachable via the raw `/api/v1/catalog/*` routes; this closes that gap. No RBAC change -- `catalog.write` already covers `TOUR_OPERATOR` + admins, matching this session's "Tour operator = platform admin" precedent from DR-027 rather than the spec's literal "Super Admin" wording. **Tailor-made -> operational itinerary**: per explicit user confirmation, this is the SAME client-facing concept as DR-027's `TAILOR_MADE` booking origin, not a separate package-level entity -- the new spec's "Pending Review until approved" maps to `AWAITING_QUOTATION`/`QUOTATION_SENT`, and per explicit user choice, "approved" = the moment staff sends the quotation (not full payment) -- operators can start planning logistics as soon as a booking is priced. Confirmed via research that this was a genuine, unsolved gap: `Assignment.departureId` is a required FK (needs a real `Departure`), and `Departure.tourPackageId` was itself a required FK (needed a real `TourPackage`) -- a `TAILOR_MADE` booking (DR-027) has neither, so it had zero path to resource assignment before this. **Design**: `Departure.tourPackageId` made nullable (mirroring `Booking.departureId`'s DR-027 precedent) for a new "bespoke" departure kind; new `Departure.currency`/`customCountry` columns snapshot what a `TourPackage` join would otherwise supply, since the catalog module cannot depend on the booking module (module boundary) -- new `catalogService.createBespokeDeparture(ctx, params)` takes plain params, never a `Booking`. New `bookingService.convertToItinerary` (staff-only, reuses `booking.confirm`, no new permission) creates the bespoke departure from the booking's own `customCountry`/`customTravelStart`/`customTravelEnd`/`seats`/`priceMinor`/`currency`, then sets `Booking.departureId` to point at it -- from that moment on, the booking behaves like any other departure-having booking for every downstream purpose (invoicing tax lookup, visa destination, and critically `assignmentService.createAssignment`, all completely unchanged). `catalogService.getDepartureDetail` (the one cross-module entry point booking/assignment already call) branches on `tourPackageId` being null to read the bespoke departure's own snapshotted fields instead of joining a package; `bookable` is always false for one (never for public sale, capacity is exactly its owning booking's seat count). New routes: `DELETE /catalog/packages/{id}`, `POST .../duplicate`, `POST /bookings/{id}/convert-to-itinerary`. New staff UI: a "Convert to operational itinerary" button on a priced `TAILOR_MADE` booking, and once converted, a link straight into the existing (unmodified) `/staff/departures/{id}` assignment page. `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V5, V6, V7, V8 |

| DR-029 | 2026-07-15 | Fleet Management expansion -- new Starlink kit resource, vehicle VIN + maintenance history, driver languages, and a rules-based assignment recommendation engine, against an external spec. **Scope decisions, all explicit user choices**: (1) the full recommendation engine is built now, ahead of this project's own roadmap listing "AI assignment engine" as Phase 3 -- it is honestly a simple, transparent, equal-weighted scorer, not real AI, and the code/docs say so throughout to avoid overclaiming; (2) Starlink "last location" is staff-entered lat/long for now, no live API integration -- same stubbed-until-real-credentials posture as Resend/WhatsApp/Africa's Talking (new **OI-09** tracks real Starlink API access, mirroring OI-05/06/07); (3) driver rating is deliberately NOT built -- no reviews system exists yet, and a staff-typed number with nothing backing it would be actively misleading, so it's excluded from both the driver profile and the recommendation scoring entirely; (4) driver "availability" needed no new field -- it's computed from the existing `departuresOverlap` double-booking check (DR-018), same hard gate `createAssignment` already enforced. **New schema**: `Vehicle.vin` (nullable); `DriverProfile.languages String[]`; new `MaintenanceRecord` model (append-only log, `performedAt`/`description`/`costMinor`+`currency` paired per BR-02, no scheduled/due-date concept -- that would need a maintenance-interval policy this spec doesn't define); new `StarlinkKit` model (`kitId`, `status`, 1:1 optional `vehicleId`, `lastLatitude`/`lastLongitude`/`lastLocationAt`); new `Departure.pickupLatitude`/`pickupLongitude` (staff-entered, optional -- most departures won't have it, and the engine degrades gracefully when it's missing). **Recommendation engine** (`assignmentService.recommendAssignment`): vehicles are hard-filtered to ACTIVE + capacity-fits-the-departure + not date-conflicting (reusing `departuresOverlap`), then scored as an equal-weighted average of capacity-fit (tighter fit = higher), maintenance recency (`fleetService.maintenanceRecencyScore`, a neutral 0.5 with no logged history -- not a penalty, since most vehicles won't have any yet), and distance-from-pickup (haversine, only computed when both a `Departure` pickup point and a vehicle's Starlink location exist; otherwise excluded from the average, not penalized). New `src/lib/geo.ts` (`haversineDistanceKm`) is a pure formula -- no new external dependency or approved-stack entry, deliberately staying within the current stack. Drivers are filtered the same way (ACTIVE + not conflicting) but never ranked (see rating decision above) -- the staff UI still pre-selects the top vehicle pick and reorders eligible-first, but the Platform Admin can freely override from the full active list (the dropdown is never narrowed to only recommended candidates, since `createAssignment`'s actual hard checks don't require the capacity fit the scorer prefers). New routes: `GET /departures/{id}/recommend-assignment`, vehicle maintenance CRUD, Starlink kit CRUD + location-update, `PATCH /departures/{id}` (pickup location -- the first mutable field on an existing departure). No RBAC change -- everything reuses `fleet.read`/`fleet.write`/`assignment.write`/`catalog.write`. `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V5, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-030 | 2026-07-15 | Guides Module -- against an external spec's fourth section ("Guides are managed independently. Guide profile includes: Languages, Certifications, Specialties, Rating, Availability. Assignments are linked to the Booking ID. The guide dashboard only displays: Assigned bookings, Daily itinerary, Client list, Pickup points, Emergency contacts"). Closes the last of the "TOUR_GUIDE isn't a fleet concept" gap DR-017 explicitly parked. **Module placement**: per explicit user choice, folded into the existing `fleet` module (not a new standalone `guides` module) to reuse its compliance-document/anti-BOLA plumbing directly -- `Assignment.guideUserId` still points at `User` unchanged (a pre-existing FK, not touched), `GuideProfile` is a satellite skills/compliance profile looked up by `userId`, same relationship `DriverProfile` has to `DRIVER`-role `User`s. **New schema**: `GuideProfile` (1:1 with a `TOUR_GUIDE`-role `User`, mirrors `DriverProfile`: `languages String[]`, `specialties String[]` (freeform, no fixed taxonomy specified), `status GuideStatus` (`ACTIVE`/`SUSPENDED`)); new `Document.guideProfileId` FK + `GUIDE_CERTIFICATION` document kind (reuses the existing kind-based compliance-document upload path with expiry tracking, same as `DRIVER_LICENSE` -- per explicit user choice over a plain unstructured text field, since real certifications expire); new `Traveler.emergencyContactName`/`emergencyContactPhone`/`emergencyContactRelation` (genuinely new -- nothing captured this before). **Explicit user choices matching DR-029's precedent, applied again for consistency**: rating deferred entirely (no reviews system exists to back one); availability computed from the existing `departuresOverlap` assignment data, no new field. **RBAC**: `TOUR_GUIDE` gains `fleet.read` (self-view of their own `GuideProfile`, scoped in `fleet/service.ts` the same anti-BOLA way as `DRIVER`/`VEHICLE_OWNER`) -- previously deliberately absent (a DR-021 choice, since guides had nothing fleet-related to read yet); `fleet.write` for `GuideProfile` stays staff-only, matching `DriverProfile`. **Closed a pre-existing asymmetry while it was fresh**: `assignmentService.createAssignment`'s `guideUserId` branch previously did only a role+org check, with no ACTIVE-status gate and no double-booking/overlap check at all (unlike vehicle/driver, which both had both) -- now, if a `GuideProfile` exists for the target user, its `status` must be `ACTIVE` (a guide with no profile at all is still assignable, since profiles are new and shouldn't retroactively block anyone), and the same date-overlap check vehicles/drivers get is now applied to `guideUserId` too (via the already-existing but previously-unused-here `assignmentRepository.listForGuide`). **Guide dashboard**: extends the existing DR-021 `/staff/schedule` self-service page (not a new route) with a `TOUR_GUIDE`-only "Daily itinerary & clients" section, rather than fragmenting the self-service portal in two -- "Pickup points" reuses `Departure.pickupLatitude`/`pickupLongitude` verbatim (DR-029, previously staff-only, now also surfaced here); "Daily itinerary" is the existing assigned-departure list read chronologically, not a new day-by-day activity-authoring feature (no itinerary-detail entity exists anywhere in this schema, and nothing in the spec asked for one); "Client list" is a new, deliberately data-minimized `bookingService.listTravelersForDeparture` + `TravelerDutyView` (excludes `idOrPassportNumber`/`passportDocumentId` -- a guide needs to know who's on their tour and how to help them, not passport numbers) -- **the real security finding this increment surfaced**: `bookingService.list`'s existing `isStaff()` check treats `TOUR_GUIDE` identically to `TOUR_OPERATOR` and would return the *entire org's* booking manifest if a guide-facing UI ever called it directly, so this new method is intentionally NOT exposed as a public `/api/v1` route (unlike almost every other new capability this session) -- it follows the same "caller already gates" convention as `fleetService.listVehiclesByIds`/`authService.getUser` (DR-021), safe only because its one caller (the schedule page) only ever passes a `departureId` drawn from the caller's own `listMyAssignments` result, never an arbitrary one. Per the data-minimization choice made explicitly this session, the client list shows full duty-relevant detail (disabilities/allergies/drink preference/emergency contact), not a stripped-down subset -- still strictly scoped to the guide's own assigned departures, never the org manifest. New `/staff/fleet/guides` + `/staff/fleet/guides/new` + `/staff/fleet/guides/[id]` pages and `GET/POST /fleet/guides` + `GET/PATCH /fleet/guides/{id}` + document routes, all mirroring the driver equivalents exactly. `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V5, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-031 | 2026-07-15 | My Schedule -- against an external spec's fifth section, personalizing the self-service dashboard per operational role (DRIVER: assigned trips/vehicle/pickup locations/route/client details/itinerary; TOUR_GUIDE: assigned tours/daily itinerary/tour notes/client information; VISA_FACILITATOR: immigration tasks/missing documents/visa deadlines). **Driver client details -- pure reuse, no new code beyond a gate widen**: the DR-030 guide-only "client list"/"daily itinerary"/"pickup points" section on `/staff/schedule` is now shown to `DRIVER` too (`showClientDetails = ctx.roles.includes('TOUR_GUIDE') || ctx.roles.includes('DRIVER')`) -- `DRIVER` already held `booking.read` (required by `bookingService.listTravelersForDeparture`), so no RBAC/schema change was needed; `VEHICLE_OWNER` deliberately excluded, since the spec doesn't ask for client details for that role and it has no operational reason to see a manifest. **"Tour notes"** reuses `Booking.specialRequests` (already rendered in the DR-030 client-list booking header, just relabeled) rather than adding a parallel field with overlapping meaning -- explicit user choice. **VISA_FACILITATOR dashboard -- genuinely new on every axis**: this role previously had zero staff UI at all (a gap DR-019/020/021/025 each independently re-flagged) despite already passing the `isStaffRole` baseline gate since DR-020 -- confirmed via research that only per-traveler actions existed (submit/decide/resubmit/upload), no "list mine to process" query. New `visaService.listForFacilitator` (new `FacilitatorVisaView`, richer than the BR-10-minimized `OfficerVisaView` since this role already holds unscoped `visa.process` -- includes `rejectionReason` and a resolved `travelStartDate`, still keeps `hasDocument` as a boolean rather than exposing the raw `documentId`). **Scope, per explicit user choice**: whole-org queue, not scoped to a per-facilitator assignment concept -- there's no existing notion of a visa application being "assigned" to a specific facilitator, and adding one would be real unrequested scope. **"Missing documents", per explicit user choice**: ANY status with no document attached counts as missing (not just `APPROVED` ones) -- `uploadDocument`/`setDocument` have no status gate anywhere in the code, so this is a new UI-level definition, not an existing invariant being surfaced. **"Visa deadlines", per explicit user choice**: derived live via `Traveler.bookingId -> Booking.departureId -> Departure.startDate` (or `Booking.customTravelStart` for a `TAILOR_MADE` booking) since `VisaApplication` has no date field of its own for this -- required two new narrow lookups to make the cross-module join possible: `bookingRepository.findTravelerById` + `bookingService.getBookingForTraveler(ctx, travelerId)` (a reverse traveler->booking lookup, "caller already gates" convention like `listVehiclesByIds`/DR-030's `listTravelersForDeparture`, since the visa module's `visa.process` caller already has legitimate reach to any traveler in the org). Sorted soonest-travel-first; a row whose booking/departure can't be resolved (e.g. a `COMPLETED` departure `catalogService.getDepartureDetail` no longer shows a non-operator role) is caught per-row and sorts last with a null date, rather than failing the whole queue. New `GET /api/v1/visa/queue` route (exposed as a real REST route, unlike DR-030's `listTravelersForDeparture` -- this one takes no caller-supplied id at all, same safe shape as `listForCountry`/`listMyAssignments`) and `/staff/visa-queue` page, read-only (decide/resubmit/upload stay API-only, matching the existing precedent -- this is the "discovery" dashboard, not a new decision-making UI) + a new `StaffNav` "Visa queue" link. Found and fixed two stale `tests/rbac.test.ts` assertions written before DR-030 gave `TOUR_GUIDE` `fleet.read` (never updated at the time, since that test file wasn't run during DR-030's verification). `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V5, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-032 | 2026-07-16 | Immigration/Officers removal -- per explicit user instruction ("Immigration and Officers, remove them, no longer needed"), a full teardown of the `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER` role and everything built around it (DR-019/020), not just a UI hide. **Live data**: 2 `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER` accounts existed in the shared Neon DB, both confirmed test fixtures (`io-na-*@example.test`/`io-cd-*@example.test`, no name, identical batch-creation timestamp from DR-020-era testing) -- deleted outright per explicit user choice, not reassigned. **Schema**: `Role` enum's `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER` value dropped (needs the same create-new-type/migrate-column/drop-old-type dance as DR-027's `BookingStatus` replacement, safe here since the only 2 rows holding it were deleted first); `User.assignedCountry` column dropped entirely (existed only for this role's BR-10 country-scoping). **RBAC**: `immigration.read` removed from the `Permission` union and the `MATRIX`; `TOUR_GUIDE`/`DRIVER`/etc. permission rows unaffected. **Removed code**: `authService.assignOfficerCountry`/`listOfficers` + their repository methods (`updateAssignedCountry`/`findOrganizationCountries`/`listByRole`, none used elsewhere); `visaService.listForCountry` (its only remaining caller after DR-031 was the officer's own country-scoped read plus an admin fallback -- DR-031's `listForFacilitator` already gives admins a superset whole-org view, so nothing was left needing it) along with `OfficerVisaView`/`toOfficerView` and the repository's `listForCountry`/`listAll` methods; `AssignOfficerCountryInput`; `ASSIGNABLE_ROLES` no longer offers this role to the admin user-creation form. **Removed surface**: `/staff/immigration` + `/staff/admin/officers` pages (and their `StaffNav` links), `GET /api/v1/immigration/visa-applications`, `GET /api/v1/immigration/officers`, `PATCH /api/v1/users/{userId}/assign-country`. `docs/openapi.yaml` updated to match (paths/schemas removed, version bumped 0.8.0 -> 0.9.0). **Tests**: deleted `tests/api/immigration-officers.api.test.ts` and `tests/api/visa.security.test.ts` (both entirely about the removed officer/BR-10 country-scoping behavior, nothing salvageable); trimmed officer-specific assertions and fixtures out of `tests/rbac.test.ts`, `tests/staff-guard.test.ts`, `tests/fleet.service.test.ts`, `tests/api/bookings.security.test.ts`, `tests/api/users.api.test.ts`, and `tests/api/visa.api.test.ts` (replaced the DR-026 multi-role union test's `IMMIGRATION_OFFICER`/`immigration.read` example pair with `VISA_FACILITATOR`/`TOUR_GUIDE` vs. `fleet.read`/`visa.process`, and the "any staff role reaches the baseline gate" example with `VISA_FACILITATOR`). VISA_FACILITATOR's whole-org queue (`/staff/visa-queue`, DR-031) remains the sole visa-overview surface for staff going forward. `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V8 |
| DR-033 | 2026-07-16 | Itinerary Management -- against an external spec's sixth section, introducing a first-class `Itinerary` entity ("the operational plan for the entire tour," 1:1 with a Booking) where "itinerary" previously only meant "a Departure with Assignments" (DR-028's `convertToItinerary`). The largest single increment this session -- confirmed via research that `Hotel`/`Restaurant` (mentioned only in marketing prose before this), a day-by-day schedule model, and any real behavioral difference between `SUPERADMIN`/`PLATFORM_ADMIN` were all completely absent from the codebase. **New standalone `itinerary` module** (explicit user choice, mirrors DR-018's `assignment` module precedent) composing `booking`/`assignment`/`catalog` through their public interfaces rather than duplicating vehicle/driver/guide assignment data, which stays owned by the `assignment` module (keyed by `Departure`, shared across every booking on a `PREDEFINED_PACKAGE` departure -- an `Itinerary` cannot own that data 1:1 without breaking that sharing). **New schema**: `Itinerary` (status `DRAFT`/`IN_REVIEW`/`APPROVED`, notes, trip-level emergency contact -- distinct from `Traveler.emergencyContactName` et al, DR-030, which is per-traveler); `ItineraryDay` (one row per calendar day: date, `departureTime`/`arrivalTime` as plain "HH:MM" strings since no time-of-day type exists in this schema, pickup/drop-off location, planned sites, activities, estimated travel minutes, notes); `Hotel`/`Restaurant` (lightweight reusable reference entities -- name/country/address/contact only, no compliance tracking like the fleet module has, per explicit user choice over both a no-entity freeform-text option and a full fleet-depth option) plus `ItineraryHotel`/`ItineraryRestaurant` join tables (many-to-many, a trip can use several of each, no day-scoping -- "Assigned hotel(s)/restaurant(s)" reads as trip-level in the spec, unlike the explicitly per-day fields). **RBAC, explicit user choice**: the spec's literal Super-Admin-vs-Platform-Admin split (create/approve vs. review/confirm-logistics) was NOT introduced -- these two roles stay 100% identical everywhere in this codebase (verified zero exceptions before deciding), matching DR-027/028's "Tour operator = platform admin" precedent; new `itinerary.read`/`itinerary.write`/`itinerary.approve` permissions all go to `TOUR_OPERATOR` (plus `SUPERADMIN`/`PLATFORM_ADMIN` via `'*'`), while `TOUR_GUIDE`/`DRIVER` get only `itinerary.read`, anti-BOLA-scoped in `itinerary/service.ts` to itineraries whose booking sits on one of their own assigned departures (resolved via `assignmentService.listMyAssignments`, same "caller already gates" shape as DR-030/031). **"Remove Departure" clarified mid-session**: the user's shorthand meant removing the redundant `/staff/departures` staff-dashboard **nav tab**, not the `Departure` Prisma model or its shared-capacity-pooling role -- `/staff/departures/[departureId]` (the working vehicle/driver/guide assignment page, DR-018/029) stays fully intact and untouched, just unlinked from `StaffNav` and reached instead via a link from the new itinerary page and from the booking-detail page (widened from `TAILOR_MADE`-only to any booking with a `departureId`, closing a gap the nav removal would otherwise have opened for `PREDEFINED_PACKAGE` bookings). New `bookingService.createItinerary`-adjacent flow: booking-detail page gained a "Create itinerary" button (absent) / status badge + link (present) section, distinct from the existing DR-028 "Convert to operational itinerary" button (which creates the underlying bespoke `Departure` for a `TAILOR_MADE` booking -- a prerequisite, not a duplicate, of the new Itinerary). **Guide/driver read-only access** needed no new page at all: the same `/staff/itineraries/{id}` detail page already renders read-only once its `canWrite`/`canApprove` checks (`can(ctx.roles, ...)`) are false, so `/staff/schedule` (DR-021/030/031) only gained a discovery section (`itineraryService.listMine`) linking into that same page. New routes: `POST/GET /bookings/{id}/itinerary`, `GET /itineraries` (manager) + `/itineraries/mine` (guide/driver), `GET/PATCH /itineraries/{id}`, `POST .../review`/`.../send-back`/`.../approve`, `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE .../days(/{dayId})`, `GET/POST .../hotels(/{hotelId})` + same for restaurants, and `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /hotels`/`/restaurants` for the reference catalogs. `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V5, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-034 | 2026-07-16 | Immigration Module + Country Regulations -- against an external spec ("The Tour Operator is by default also a Visa Facilitator role... every Platform Admin can [process visas]... Only the Super Admin can manage immigration regulations and country requirements"), plus full platform expansion to Zambia (ZM) and Zimbabwe (ZW). **RBAC**: `TOUR_OPERATOR` gains `visa.process` (it already held `documents.read`/`write`) -- explicit user instruction, same effect as VISA_FACILITATOR without literally merging the two roles. **New schema**: `CountryRegulation` (visa requirements, required documents, processing time, entry conditions, immigration fee as integer minor units + `Currency` per BR-02, embassy contact fields, health requirements, travel advisories, special restrictions) -- platform-wide reference data, no `organizationId`, no RLS policy, same precedent as `TaxRate` (verified: `tax_rates` has zero RLS policy in `rls.sql` and `src/lib/tax.ts` never uses `withOrg`). **The first real behavioral gap between SUPERADMIN and PLATFORM_ADMIN in this codebase**: country-regulation write (add/edit/delete) is SUPERADMIN-only, explicitly excluding PLATFORM_ADMIN, per direct user confirmation. Since both admin roles hold `'*'` in `rbac.ts`'s MATRIX (and `grantsPermission()` short-circuits true for any wildcard role regardless of which permission is checked), this can't be expressed through the permission matrix alone -- the new `country_regulation.write` permission passes the route-level gate for both admin roles via wildcard, but a new `immigration/domain.ts` `isCountryRegulationWriter(roles)` (`roles.includes('SUPERADMIN')`) is checked inside `immigration/service.ts`'s mutation methods, rejecting PLATFORM_ADMIN despite it passing the broader gate -- mirrors the existing "RBAC decides broad category, service does the narrower role-identity check" layering already used by `isItineraryManager`/`isFleetManager`. New `country_regulation.read` granted to `TOUR_OPERATOR`/`VISA_FACILITATOR` (admins get it via wildcard). New standalone `immigration` module (mirrors the `assignment`/`itinerary` module precedent) -- `listRegulations`/`getRegulation`/`createRegulation`/`updateRegulation`/`deleteRegulation`, all audited. New `/staff/country-regulations` pages: read-only for anyone with `country_regulation.read`, write controls (and the underlying write routes) rendered only for `SUPERADMIN` so `PLATFORM_ADMIN` never sees a control that would 403. **Two new real actions** on the existing `visa` module (not the new one, since they operate on `Traveler`/`VisaApplication` data): `contactTraveler`/`requestMissingDocuments`, both real notification-triggering actions (explicit user choice) via the existing `notificationsService` -- since a `Traveler` isn't itself a `User` account, both resolve the notification recipient through the existing `bookingService.getBookingForTraveler` reverse lookup to the booking's tour lead. New `NotificationEvent` values `VISA_CONTACT_TRAVELER`/`VISA_MISSING_DOCUMENTS` + EN/FR templates. New routes: `POST .../visa/contact`, `POST .../visa/request-documents`, plus surfaced directly on the existing `/staff/visa-queue` page (now also reachable by `TOUR_OPERATOR`). **Zambia/Zimbabwe expansion** (explicit user choice: full platform support, not just regulation-reference data) -- confirmed the schema/zod/tax-lookup layers are already country-agnostic (plain strings, no enums, `tax.ts` is a pure table query), so this was a data/config/UI change only: `Organization.countries` (Lam's org) += `['ZM','ZW']`, two new `TaxRate` rows, 4 seeded `CountryRegulation` rows (DRC/Namibia/Zambia/Zimbabwe, general knowledge flagged for `SUPERADMIN` review, not verified against each country's immigration authority), new country options across every guest/staff package-country dropdown, `destination-sites.ts`'s quiz data, the homepage `AfricaMap`'s highlight set + `country-facts.ts` tooltip data, About page prose, and two new hedged FAQ entries. Packages in the two new countries stay priced in the existing `USD`/`EUR` `Currency` values (no `ZMW`/`ZWL` enum addition), matching the existing precedent of DRC demo packages priced in USD rather than `CDF`. Per explicit user choice, the guest Contact page's office list was **not** extended to Zambia/Zimbabwe (no physical presence there to honestly claim, matching that page's existing no-fabrication discipline). `docs/design-package/` volumes still don't exist in-repo -- flagged, not fabricated. | V1, V4, V5, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-035 | 2026-07-16 | User Management -- against an external spec ("Super Admin: full system access... Add users, Remove users, Edit users, Reset passwords, Manage permissions, Configure system settings" vs. "Tour Operator: operational access only... Cannot: Create users, Delete users, Change permissions, Modify system settings"). User picked the large reading of "Manage permissions": a full runtime permission-matrix editor, not just per-user role editing. **This is the first change to how RBAC itself is sourced in this codebase.** New `RolePermission` table (`role`, `permission`, `@@unique([role, permission])`) -- global, no `organizationId`, no RLS, same precedent as `TaxRate`/`CountryRegulation` (DR-034). `rbac.ts`'s static `MATRIX` is renamed `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS` and demoted to a one-time seed source (`prisma/seed.ts` writes one row per role+permission for every `EDITABLE_ROLES` entry); it is never read by `can()`/`assertCan()` again after seeding. **SUPERADMIN is the one permanent exception**: it never gets `RolePermission` rows and stays a hardcoded, unconditional wildcard in `can()` -- "a role that can never be locked out of the system" -- while **PLATFORM_ADMIN loses its own hardcoded wildcard entirely** and becomes the first fully DB-editable admin role (seeded with every permission except `country_regulation.write`, which DR-034's `isCountryRegulationWriter` already blocks unconditionally regardless of what the matrix says). `can`/`assertCan` change signature from `(roles: Role[], permission)` to `(ctx: PermissionSource, permission)` where `PermissionSource = { roles, permissions: ReadonlySet<Permission> }` -- deliberately kept **synchronous**: the effective permission set is resolved once per request inside the already-async `authService.resolveSession` and attached to `AuthContext.permissions`, rather than making `can`/`assertCan` themselves async, which would have broken `StaffNav` (a client component that can't `await`) and turned `tests/rbac.test.ts`'s pure unit tests into DB-backed ones for no benefit. New `authService.updateUser`/`resetPassword` (edit name/email/phone/roles; generate-and-reveal-once a new temporary password + force `mustChangePassword`, reusing `createUser`'s existing reveal-once UX pattern) and `getPermissionMatrix`/`setRolePermission`, the last two SUPERADMIN-only via the same "RBAC decides the broad category (`admin.all`), service does the narrower role-identity check" layering DR-034 established for `isCountryRegulationWriter` -- `PLATFORM_ADMIN` passes the route gate but is rejected inside the service, and the SUPERADMIN-only staff page (`/staff/admin/permissions`) additionally redirects any non-SUPERADMIN caller before it ever calls the service, so PLATFORM_ADMIN never sees a control that would 403. New routes `PATCH /users/{userId}`, `POST /users/{userId}/reset-password`, `GET/PATCH /permissions`. New staff pages: `/staff/admin/users/{userId}` (edit + reset-password panel) and `/staff/admin/permissions` (168-checkbox matrix, `EDITABLE_ROLES` x `ALL_PERMISSIONS`, auto-submits per checkbox via a server action, no batch save step). "Configure system settings" (the spec's third Super-Admin capability) was explicitly raised but the user did not answer which concrete settings (tax rates / notification toggles / branding / other) belong to it -- **deliberately not built**, flagged for a future increment rather than guessed at. | V4, V5, V8 |
| DR-036 | 2026-07-16 | Staff can book for a client with no pre-existing account -- closes a gap DR-014 explicitly deferred ("staff can only book on behalf of a tourist who already has an account, found by email; creating one on the spot is explicitly deferred") that had become inconsistent with the rest of the app once DR-016 established that tourists never sign up at all, ever (guest checkout via better-auth's anonymous plugin). Staff-entered client email now resolves-or-creates a real `Booking.touristUserId` row transparently instead of erroring. New `authRepository.createBareTourist` (a login-less `User` row: `role: TOURIST`, no `Account`/credential row, can never sign in) and `authService.findOrCreateTouristByEmail` (existing-user lookup first, create-bare-tourist fallback; throws `forbidden` if the caller has no `organizationId` -- no internal permission check otherwise, same "caller already gates" convention as `getUserByEmail`, since the only caller is `booking.create`-gated). `createBookingForClientAction`/`createTailorMadeBookingAction` (`src/app/staff/(dashboard)/bookings/new/actions.ts`) switched from `getUserByEmail` + redirect-with-`client_not_found`-error to this new method -- the error state and its UI (`Alert`, copy) are gone from `bookings/new/page.tsx` since it's no longer reachable; the email field's label now reads "Client email (or the tour lead's email, for a group)". A client created this way can still find their booking via the existing `bookingService.lookupByConfirmationCode` (confirmation code + last name), same as a guest checkout -- no new lookup path needed. Deliberately did NOT touch the *other* `authService.getUserByEmail` call sites (fleet guide/driver/vehicle-owner-account lookup, departure-assignment guide lookup) -- those resolve real operational-role accounts with logins, where "must already exist" is still the correct rule. New `tests/auth-find-or-create-tourist.test.ts` (create-on-first-use, reuse-on-repeat, forbidden-with-no-org), following the DR-035 fixture-cleanup guard convention. No schema/permission/RLS change. | V4, V6, V7 |
| DR-037 | 2026-07-16 | Customer Ratings & Feedback -- against an external spec's Module 14 (five modules were presented in one request; the user chose to build this one first as the smallest, most self-contained, and the one that directly closes a gap CLAUDE.md already documented: DR-029/030 deliberately left `DriverProfile`/`GuideProfile` with no `rating` field, "deferred until a real reviews system exists" -- **this is that reviews system**, the first one in this codebase).
