Fixed with new `Booking.contactFirstName`/`contactLastName` columns (booking-scoped, same precedent and reasoning as `contactEmail`, DR-047 -- an anonymous session has no other reliable place to read a guest's name back from), now **required** (not optional, unlike the description field) on `CreateTailorMadeInput` alongside `email`. `lookupByBookingReference` now falls back to `{ lastName: booking.contactLastName }` when no tour-lead `Traveler` exists yet, reusing the existing `lastNameMatches` helper unchanged (same `{lastName: string}` shape). Every caller of `CreateTailorMadeInput` updated to supply real names: `/plan-my-trip`'s Server Action (already collected `firstName`/`lastName` for the account profile, now also passed into the booking), the staff tailor-made form (gained new required "Client first/last name" fields -- previously only asked for email), and the raw `POST /api/v1/bookings/tailor-made` route (schema-enforced, no code change needed). Schema applied directly to the shared Neon DB via a user-pasted `neondb_owner` credential (never written to any file), verified via `psql \d bookings`; `npx prisma generate` re-run locally to pick up the new columns' generated types. New DB-backed test in `tests/booking-lookup.test.ts` (a bare TAILOR_MADE booking with zero travelers now resolves via `contactLastName`, and still rejects a wrong last name) -- confirmed reaching the live DB via a bare `tsx` script, but `vitest run` itself hit the documented vitest-specific Prisma connectivity gotcha this session (bare `tsx` connects fine, `vitest run` doesn't -- an unresolved sandbox quirk, not a real outage) -- needs a real CI run to fully confirm. `lint`/`typecheck` clean; pure-domain tests green throughout. | V1, V5 |
| DR-058 | 2026-07-20 | **Real booking deletion for the staff dashboard** -- closes the gap that staff had no way to remove a booking at all. Went through two design corrections in conversation: the user first chose "real hard delete," which conflicts with the schema's own explicit design comment (`Booking` deliberately has no `deletedAt` -- "booking history must survive cancellation for audit/reconciliation") and would cascade-delete `Invoice`/`Payment` (real financial records, with no soft-delete escape hatch, unlike the packages module's DR-028 precedent of choosing soft delete for exactly this reason); once that tradeoff was surfaced, the user corrected to soft delete plus an explicit 90-day retention purge. Separately widened from "cancelled bookings only" (the original ask) to any booking in any status (explicit follow-up instruction). **Final design**: new `Booking.deletedAt` -- hides the booking from every read path in `booking/repository.ts` immediately (`findById`/`findByBookingReference`/`listMine`/`listForOrg`/`listBookingsWithTravelersForDeparture` all check it; every status-mutating method's own `existing` lookup checks it too, so a soft-deleted booking can't be un-cancelled/confirmed/etc. either). New `BOOKING_DELETION_RETENTION_DAYS = 90` (explicit user choice) purge folded directly into the existing `sweepLifecycle` lazy-sweep raw SQL -- this codebase has no scheduled job/cron infrastructure at all (deliberately, confirmed by grep before designing this), so the purge follows that exact same "check opportunistically on the next read" convention rather than introducing a new one. New `booking.delete` permission, **never seeded to any role** (same two-layer pattern as `country_regulation.write`/`finance_config.write`): the route/service's `assertCan` is only the first gate, a new `isBookingDeleter(roles)` (`SUPERADMIN` only) inside `booking/service.ts`'s new `deleteBooking` is the real one, so granting `booking.delete` to another role via the runtime-editable permission matrix still wouldn't let them use it. New `DELETE /api/v1/bookings/{id}` route and a "Delete" control on both `/staff/bookings` (per row) and `/staff/bookings/{id}` (detail page), each gated on `ctx.roles.includes('SUPERADMIN')` for rendering (not just the route permission), matching the country-regulations UI's own `canWrite` convention. New `tests/booking-delete.test.ts` (its own throwaway org, not the shared primary org, since the test creates/soft-deletes/backdates bookings directly): rejects a non-SUPERADMIN caller even with the permission hand-set onto their context, confirms SUPERADMIN can delete a booking in any status, confirms a deleted booking disappears from `bookingService.list` immediately, and confirms the retention sweep purges a booking backdated past 90 days while leaving a recent one untouched. Schema (`Booking.deletedAt` + a supporting index) applied to the shared Neon DB via the same user-pasted `neondb_owner` credential from earlier in the same session (reused rather than re-requested), verified via `psql \d bookings`. `lint`/`typecheck` clean; pure-domain/rbac tests green; the new DB-backed test suite could not be run to completion this session -- notably, this time even a bare `tsx` script failed to reach the database (confirmed `psql` still connected fine on the same credentials), matching the documented Prisma-engine-specific connectivity gotcha rather than a real outage or code defect -- needs a real CI run to fully confirm. | V1, V5 |
| DR-059 | 2026-07-20 | **Fleet deletion (SUPERADMIN-only) + demo fleet/hotel/restaurant data + itinerary auto-removal on booking delete + staff self-service profile edit.** Retroactively logged here (was documented in CLAUDE.md's Phase-status narrative at the time but missed this table). New `fleet.delete` permission (never seeded to any role) + `isFleetDeleter(roles)` (`SUPERADMIN`-only), same two-layer pattern as `booking.delete` (DR-058): `Vehicle`/`DriverProfile`/`GuideProfile` get a new `deletedAt` soft-delete (their FKs are all `onDelete: Cascade` with real history -- `Assignment`/`ReviewSubjectRating` -- hanging off them, so a hard delete would silently lose it); `StarlinkKit` instead gets a genuine hard delete, since schema analysis confirmed no other table has any FK pointing at it at all, so there's no cascade/history risk to guard against. Seeded 10 each of vehicles/drivers/guides/Starlink kits (all unassigned) and 10 each of hotels/restaurants across NA/CD/ZM/ZW, via `prisma/seed.ts`. New `itineraryService.deleteForBooking`, called from the staff `deleteBookingAction` (not from `bookingService.deleteBooking` itself -- the `itinerary` module already depends on `booking`, so the reverse call would be circular; orchestrated at the Server Action layer instead, the same pattern used wherever this codebase needs two modules' actions coordinated). New `/staff/profile` self-service page (any staff role, reuses the existing `profile.write` permission every role already holds) for editing name/phone, added to the Settings sidebar with no extra permission gate (same convention as Change Password). No RLS change beyond the new `deletedAt` columns' read-path filtering. `lint`/`typecheck` clean; `tests/fleet-delete.test.ts` (7/7) and `tests/itinerary-delete.test.ts` (2/2) confirmed green live against the real Neon DB; CI confirmed fully green on push (commit `2713a17`). | V1, V5, V6 |
| DR-060 | 2026-07-20 | **Visa application auto-creation on passport upload + a "needs application" reconciliation view on the visa queue + an origin column/filter.** Research (not assumption) established the actual starting state: `visaService.submitApplication` (the only code path that ever creates a `VisaApplication`) had **zero UI anywhere calling it** -- the only caller was a raw `POST .../visa/submit` API route with no button/form pointed at it in the whole codebase -- so, functionally, no visa application could ever be started through the app at all, for any of the 3 booking-creation sources (guest package browse, guest `/plan-my-trip`, staff manual booking); `/staff/visa-queue` only ever listed rows that already existed, with no way to surface who needed one started. The pipeline gap turned out to be identical across all 3 sources -- `origin`/booking-creation-source had zero bearing anywhere in the visa module already. Per explicit user direction: **(1)** new `visaService.autoSubmitOnPassportUpload`, called from all 3 passport-upload call sites (guest wizard, staff wizard, raw API route) right after `bookingService.setTravelerPassport` succeeds, each wrapped in try/catch so a failure here can never fail the passport upload itself (mirrors the Add-ons currency-mismatch fix's charter-rule-8 precedent). Deliberately does NOT `assertCan(ctx, 'visa.process')` -- the caller (a guest or staff member without that permission) already has legitimate access to this exact traveler via `setTravelerPassport`'s own anti-BOLA check, so this exposes no new data/capability, just also creates a record. **(2)** new `bookingService.listTravelersRequiringVisa` (org-wide, gated on `visa.process` directly, matching `assignmentService.listAllAssignments`'s precedent of checking the composing module's own permission) + `visaService.listNeedingApplication`, diffing that candidate list against the visa module's own existing-application set (module boundary: booking doesn't know about `VisaApplication`, so the diff happens in visa's service layer, not a direct cross-table join) -- surfaced as a "Needs application" section on `/staff/visa-queue` with a manual "Start application" button (finally giving the existing, previously-dangling `submitApplication` route a real UI trigger, for the backlog/reconciliation case the automatic trigger doesn't reach). Should normally show few or zero rows now that (1) handles the common case. **(3)** `FacilitatorVisaView` gains a live-resolved `origin` field (same nullable-on-unresolvable-edge-case convention as `travelStartDate`/`bookingId`); the queue page gained a Source column and origin-filter pills (mirrors `/staff/bookings`' status-filter-pill precedent) -- purely for staff triage/context, no behavioral branching by origin (confirmed none is warranted). **Found and fixed a real bug while writing the DB-backed tests, not just a test issue**: the first draft of `listNeedingApplication` composed its two calls via `Promise.all`, reproducing the exact same connection-pool-exhaustion pattern Insights (DR-038) and Tracking (DR-041) already diagnosed and fixed for this sandbox's Neon pool -- rewritten to sequential `await`s, the same latency-for-robustness tradeoff those two DRs made, which also protects the real production pool, not just this sandbox. No schema/RLS change (no new tables or columns -- `VisaApplication`/`Traveler`/`Booking` already carried everything needed). `lint`/`typecheck` clean; new `tests/visa-auto-submit.test.ts` (5/5) and the existing `tests/api/visa-facilitator-queue.api.test.ts` (3/3) confirmed green live against the real Neon DB, both intermittently slow/failing on the first few attempts from the documented Prisma-to-Neon connectivity gotcha before passing cleanly. | V1, V5, V6 |
| DR-061 | 2026-07-21 | **Staff-only 5-star hotel/restaurant ratings.** Closes a gap surfaced during a user-requested planning discussion covering three topics (booking source-of-truth, hotel/restaurant ratings, My Schedule) -- this is the ratings one; the other two produced no schema change (`DRAFT` stays in `BookingStatus`, unreachable but harmless, after a cleanup attempt was blocked by 9 leftover DRAFT-status test bookings across the shared DB -- including one in the real "Lam" org -- which the user chose not to purge). Extends the existing `itinerary` module (which already owns `Hotel`/`Restaurant`) rather than introducing a new one, and is deliberately distinct from the tourist-facing `ratings` module's `DRIVER`/`GUIDE` reviews -- different subject, different author, different eligibility rule entirely. New `HotelRating`/`RestaurantRating` (one row per (hotel-or-restaurant, staff rater), unique constraint enforcing it -- **overwritten on each revisit, not accumulated**, per explicit user choice) + new `averageRating`/`ratingCount` on `Hotel`/`Restaurant`, live-recomputed via `AVG()`/`COUNT()` inside the same transaction as the upsert, same precedent as `DriverProfile`/`GuideProfile`/`Organization`'s existing aggregate fields (DR-037). New `hotel_restaurant_rating.write` permission -- seeded to `TOUR_GUIDE`/`DRIVER` (anti-BOLA-scoped in `itinerary/service.ts`'s new `rateHotel`/`rateRestaurant`, which reuse the existing `getOwnedItinerary` check and additionally verify the hotel/restaurant is actually assigned to that specific itinerary before accepting a rating) and `TOUR_OPERATOR`/`PLATFORM_ADMIN` (unscoped, matching their existing org-wide `itinerary.write`). New rating widget on `/staff/itineraries/{id}`'s existing assigned-hotels/assigned-restaurants tables (a 1-5 select + optional comment, pre-filled with the caller's own existing rating if any) plus a read-only average-rating column on `/staff/hotels`/`/staff/restaurants`. New `POST`/`GET .../rating` routes under both `/api/v1/itineraries/{id}/hotels/{hotelId}` and `.../restaurants/{restaurantId}`, matching this module's existing per-sub-resource REST-completeness convention. **Found a real gap while writing the DB-backed tests, not a bug in the new code itself**: since DR-035 made permissions DB-backed (`RolePermission` table, seeded once from `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS`), adding `hotel_restaurant_rating.write` to `rbac.ts` alone did not grant it live -- every new test 403'd until `npm run db:seed` was re-run (safe, `create-if-missing` upsert; confirmed via the seed's own `rolePermissions: 94` count, +4 for the newly-granted roles) to actually insert the missing rows. Schema (`hotel_ratings`/`restaurant_ratings` tables + the two new `Hotel`/`Restaurant` columns) and RLS policies applied to the shared Neon DB via a user-pasted `neondb_owner` credential, verified via `psql \d`. `lint`/`typecheck` clean; new `tests/api/hotel-restaurant-rating.api.test.ts` (10/10) and `tests/rls.cross-tenant.{hotel,restaurant}-ratings.test.ts` (4/4 each) confirmed green live against the real Neon DB; `tests/rbac.test.ts` (33/33, new seed-set assertion) green. A pre-existing, untouched test file (`tests/api/itinerary.security.test.ts`) could not be run to completion this session from the documented Prisma-to-Neon connectivity gotcha (several retries, unrelated to this change) -- needs a real CI run to confirm it's still green. | V1, V5, V6 |
| DR-062 | 2026-07-21 | **Reusable per-package itinerary template, auto-copied onto new itineraries.** Closes an item the user proactively confirmed building (offered alongside a now-dropped generic fleet-tracking-tag idea, explicitly declined: "No -- drop it, not needed anymore"). New `PackageItineraryDay` model (catalog module, org-scoped/RLS, `@@unique([tourPackageId, dayNumber])`) -- a day-by-day template (`dayNumber` relative to trip start, not a real date) staff define once on a package via a new "Itinerary template" section on `/staff/packages/{packageId}`. Deliberately mirrors `ItineraryDay`'s shape (itinerary module) rather than sharing a type with it -- this is catalog-owned template/reference data, that one is a live per-booking record, and duplicating the shape avoids a two-way module dependency. `itineraryService.createItinerary` now copies the template onto the fresh `Itinerary`'s real `ItineraryDay` rows the moment one is created for a booking whose departure has a `tourPackageId` -- real dates computed as `departure.startDate + (dayNumber - 1)` days -- wrapped in try/catch so a template-copy failure can never block itinerary creation itself (same best-effort precedent as DR-060's `autoSubmitOnPassportUpload`). New `catalogService.addTemplateDay`/`updateTemplateDay`/`removeTemplateDay`/`listTemplateDays` (`catalog.write`/`catalog.read`) + a no-ctx `listTemplateDaysForItineraryCopy(organizationId, packageId)` for the itinerary module's internal use only (same "caller already gates" convention as `getDepartureWindow`). Bundled in: moved the "Tracking" staff-nav entry to sit right after "Visa Queue" (explicit user request, pure nav reorder, no permission/route change). Schema + RLS applied to the shared Neon DB via a user-pasted `neondb_owner` credential, verified via `psql \d`. `lint`/`typecheck` clean; new `tests/package-itinerary-template.test.ts` (3 tests: template CRUD, auto-copy with computed dates, no-op for a departure-less TAILOR_MADE booking) could not be run to completion this session -- the documented Prisma-to-Neon connectivity gotcha was unusually persistent (transaction-timeout errors even on retry, confirmed via a direct `psql` connectivity check that the DB itself was reachable throughout) -- needs a real CI run to confirm. | V1, V5, V6 |
| DR-063 | 2026-07-21 | **Real 30-minute inactivity session timeout, applied globally, + `User.lastLoginAt` tracking.** Explicit user instruction: "every user cannot have an inactive session beyond 30min after that if still inactive, the system automatically require a new sign[-in]." better-auth's session model is a sliding window, not a separate inactivity concept -- `expiresIn` is how long a session stays valid since its last refresh, `updateAge` is how often using the session actually pushes that expiry forward. The prior config (`expiresIn: 12h`, `updateAge: 30m`) actually tolerated up to 12h of real inactivity despite a misleading old comment describing it as a 30-minute timeout; changed to `{expiresIn: 30m, updateAge: 30m}` so the two work together as a genuine 30-minute inactivity timeout. **Explicit, informed tradeoff, applies globally**: when this was flagged as affecting anonymous guest-checkout sessions too (no way to "sign back into" the same anonymous identity after a lapse, unlike a credentialed account), the user confirmed "Every session, staff and guest alike" rather than scoping it to staff only -- a guest who pauses 30+ minutes mid-wizard now restarts as a new anonymous identity. New `User.lastLoginAt` (nullable, set via a new `databaseHooks.session.create.after` hook -- a plain `prisma.user.update`, best-effort/try-catch, run only on an actual new-Session-creation event i.e. a real sign-in, not on every authenticated request that `updateAge`'s own refresh would otherwise conflate this with) surfaced as a new "Last login" column (`Never` when null) on `/staff/admin/users`. Both `PublicUser`'s domain type and the auth repository's `RawUser`/`toPublicUser` mapping updated to carry it through. No RLS change (`users` has none, per existing precedent). Schema applied to the shared Neon DB via the same `neondb_owner` credential, verified via `psql \d users`. `lint`/`typecheck` clean; new `tests/auth-last-login-hook.test.ts` (exercises a real `auth.api.signInEmail` call against a manually-created credentialed user, mirroring `tests/auth-signup-hook.test.ts`'s precedent -- the existing `loginAs()` test helper mints a `Session` directly and never fires this hook, and `signUpEmail` alone doesn't reach it either since `requireEmailVerification: true` skips auto-sign-in-on-signup) confirmed passing live against the real Neon DB. | V1, V5, V7 |
| DR-064 | 2026-07-21 | **`/find-booking` now surfaces booking-lifecycle status** -- explicit user instruction to also show itinerary approval, driver/vehicle/guide assignment, Starlink kit tracking, visa status, and rating-code availability, not just the bare `Booking.status` it showed before. Real security-relevant tradeoff, explicitly accepted by the user after being flagged: this no-login page (reachable via only `bookingReference` + last name) now shows **real driver names and vehicle details** ("Show real driver name/vehicle details too"); visa status is scoped to surface **only** when `Booking.requiresPassportUpload` is true ("Just the visa status if it was ticked by the client"), not a broader country-regulation dump. New no-ctx service methods, following this codebase's established "caller already gates" convention (the page has already independently verified the guest's two-factor lookup before calling any of them): `itineraryService.getStatusForBookingLookup` (bare status only, never the full `ItineraryView`), `fleetService.listVehiclesForBookingLookup`/`listStarlinkKitsByVehicleIdsForBookingLookup`, `visaService.getStatusForBookingLookup` (bare status only, never `rejectionReason`/`documentId`), `ratingsService.getRatingCodeStatusForBookingLookup` (deliberately **redacted** -- returns `{available, expiresAt, usedAt}`, never the raw `RatingCode.code`, since that's this module's own genuine single-use second factor for the separate `/rate` flow, delivered via its own notification -- exposing it here would make that second factor recoverable from the same single lookup it's meant to be independent of). Composed directly in `src/app/(guest)/find-booking/result/page.tsx` (module-boundary rule prevents `booking` itself from importing `itinerary`/`fleet`/`visa`/`ratings`, since `itinerary` already depends on `booking`), sequential `await`s throughout per the documented connection-pool-exhaustion precedent (DR-038/041/060/062). No schema/RLS change -- every new method composes existing tables. `lint`/`typecheck` clean; new `tests/find-booking-lifecycle.test.ts` (5 tests, own throwaway org) hit the documented Prisma-to-Neon connectivity gotcha across 3 retries this session (confirmed the DB itself stayed reachable via direct `psql` checks throughout, including cleaning up two orphaned test orgs left by the failed attempts) -- needs a real CI run to confirm. | V1, V5, V6 |
| DR-065 | 2026-07-21 | **Country Regulations linked into the visa queue + a package/booking reference column.** Explicit user request: "Country Regulations needs to be linked to visa queue and help assess, and under visa queue table please add the package reference number if it came from an existing one or if not then use the booking reference number." `/staff/visa-queue`'s main table gains two columns. **Reference**: the tour package's own `packageReference` when the booking came from an existing `PREDEFINED_PACKAGE` departure, falling back to the booking's own `bookingReference` for a `TAILOR_MADE` request or an unresolvable package -- links into the booking-detail page either way. New `FacilitatorVisaView.packageReference`/`bookingReference`, resolved live inside `visaService.listForFacilitator`'s existing `getBookingForTraveler`/`getDepartureDetail` composition (a new nested try/catch calls `catalogService.getPackage` when the departure has a `tourPackageId`, tolerating an archived/unresolvable package rather than failing the row). **Country**: now also shows the destination's `processingTimeDays` (via `immigrationService.getRegulation`) plus a "View requirements"/"Add requirements" link straight into `/staff/country-regulations/{country}`, so a facilitator can assess an application without navigating away first -- composed directly in the page (both `VISA_FACILITATOR` and `TOUR_OPERATOR` already hold `country_regulation.read` since DR-034, confirmed before implementing), sequential `await`s over the small distinct-country set (typically <=4), same connection-pool-exhaustion precedent as DR-038/041/060/062/064. No schema/RLS change. `lint`/`typecheck` clean; new assertions in `tests/api/visa-facilitator-queue.api.test.ts` (package-reference resolution for the `PREDEFINED_PACKAGE` fixture, `null`-and-booking-reference-fallback for the `TAILOR_MADE` one) hit the documented Prisma-to-Neon connectivity gotcha across 3 retries this session (confirmed the DB itself stayed reachable via a direct `psql` check) -- needs a real CI run to confirm. | V1, V5, V6 |
| DR-066 | 2026-07-21 | **Real Upstash Redis-backed rate limiting**, closing two long-flagged gaps: the STRIDE "Denial of service" section's "real Upstash rate-limiting is a known gap" and the "Spoofing -> add auth rate-limit/lockout" TODO. Upstash was already the approved cache/queue choice (DR-001's tech stack table) but had never been implemented; no account/credentials exist yet (new **OI-10**). New `src/lib/rate-limit.ts`, following the exact "real adapter, env-gated graceful degradation" precedent the notification gateways established (DR-013, charter rule 8) -- every function degrades to its pre-existing infra-free behavior when `UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_URL`/`UPSTASH_REDIS_REST_TOKEN` are unset, so nothing regresses while OI-10 stays open, and the Redis client is constructed fresh per call (a stateless REST client, no connection to pool) rather than cached at module load. **Two distinct uses**: (1) `assertLookupNotRateLimited`/`recordLookupFailure` back the two existing public, no-session guest lookups -- `bookingService.lookupByBookingReference` (find-booking, DR-016/052/053) and `ratingsService.resolveEligibleBooking` (rating-code lookup, DR-037) -- with a real Redis counter instead of an audit-log table scan; deliberately preserves the exact prior semantics (counts recent FAILED attempts only, not every attempt, so a guest correcting a typo is never penalized for their own eventual success) by using a plain INCR+EXPIRE-on-first-write counter rather than `@upstash/ratelimit`'s sliding-window primitive (which increments unconditionally on every call and would have silently changed that behavior). (2) `getAuthRateLimitStorage()` implements better-auth's native `BetterAuthRateLimitStorage` interface (`get`/`set`/`consume`), wired into `authConfig.rateLimit.customStorage` in `src/lib/auth.ts` alongside new `customRules` tightening `/sign-in/email` (5/min) and `/sign-up/email` (3/min) below better-auth's 100-req/10s default -- deliberately does NOT set `secondaryStorage` (which would also move session storage onto Redis, a much larger blast-radius change nothing here asked for); `customStorage`'s `consume` is better-auth's preferred atomic increment+check path, closing the concurrent-bypass gap the legacy `get`/`set` path has per that interface's own doc comment. `src/middleware.ts`'s long-stale "(Upstash + Better Auth)" TODO comment updated to point at where this actually landed, since edge middleware itself needed no changes -- better-auth's own request pipeline and the two service methods already cover every rate-limited surface. New `@upstash/redis` dependency (`1.38.0`, exact-pinned, zero new transitive dependencies); `@upstash/ratelimit` deliberately NOT added, since the plain INCR-based counter above covers every real need without it. New `tests/lib/rate-limit.test.ts` (15 tests, pure -- mocks `@upstash/redis` and `@lib/audit`) covers both the unconfigured-fallback and configured-Redis paths for every exported function, including the `consume()` under/over-limit branches. No schema/RLS change. `lint`/`typecheck` clean; `tests/booking.domain.test.ts` (65)/`tests/rbac.test.ts` (33) and the wider pure/gateway-mocked suite (357 tests) all green -- the only failures seen were the pre-existing, unrelated `tests/visa-auto-submit.test.ts` (DR-060) hitting the documented Prisma-to-Neon connectivity gotcha, confirmed transient via a bare `tsx` script reaching the DB fine on the same credentials. `tests/booking-lookup.test.ts`/`tests/ratings-lookup.test.ts` (the DB-backed suites asserting the exact 429-after-repeated-failures behavior this change preserves) hit the same gotcha and need a real CI run to confirm. | V1, V5, V7 |

| DR-067 | 2026-07-21 | **Real Upstash QStash-backed scheduled jobs**, closing the other half of DR-001's "Upstash Redis + QStash" tech-stack choice that DR-066 (Redis rate limiting) left unbuilt, and turning the booking module's long-standing lazy "sweep on next read" convention (DR-016/027/058) into a real periodic job. New `bookingRepository.sweepAllOrganizations()`/`bookingService.runScheduledSweep()` -- enumerates every `Organization` (no RLS on that table, it's the tenant boundary itself, same precedent as every other org-enumerating admin script) and runs the existing per-org `sweepLifecycle` (expired-hold-to-CANCELLED, CONFIRMED->IN_PROGRESS->COMPLETED, retention-purge) for each one **sequentially, not `Promise.all`** -- same connection-pool-exhaustion precedent as Insights/Tracking/DR-060/062/064/065. This closes a real gap the lazy convention always had: an organization nobody happens to be actively reading/writing never gets its holds expired or its retention-purge run at all, no matter how long it sits idle. New `src/lib/qstash.ts` (`isQstashConfigured`/`verifyQstashSignature`) and `POST /api/jobs/sweep-bookings` (outside `/api/v1`, same "infra not a versioned REST resource" precedent as `/api/auth`) -- gated by QStash's own request signature (`Receiver.verify`), not a user session, since there is no `AuthContext` for "the platform's own scheduler." **Deliberately does NOT use `@upstash/qstash/nextjs`'s `verifySignatureAppRouter`** -- that wrapper throws synchronously at handler-wrap time (module load) when the signing-key env vars are absent, which would crash the route's module for every single request; the lower-level `Receiver` class instead lets the route check configuration explicitly and return a clean, never-thrown 401 whether the cause is a bad signature or (new **OI-11**) no account provisioned yet -- unlike DR-066's Redis piece, there is no safe "unverified but still works" fallback behavior for a route that bulk-mutates every organization, so unconfigured must mean "reject," not "run anyway." New `scripts/register-qstash-schedule.ts` (`npm run qstash:register-schedule`) -- registering the actual QStash *schedule* resource (a cron cadence pointed at the deployed URL) is an external Upstash account action, not expressible in application code, same category as verifying a Resend sending domain (OI-05); the script is idempotent via a fixed `scheduleId`, so re-running it updates rather than duplicates. Cadence chosen as `*/15 * * * *`, well under `HOLD_DURATION_MINUTES` (30). New `@upstash/qstash` dependency (`2.11.2`, exact-pinned; brings in `jose`/`crypto-js`/`neverthrow`, all small and reputable, none flagged in `npm audit`). No schema/RLS change. `lint`/`typecheck`/`build` clean; new `tests/lib/qstash.test.ts` (8 tests, pure, mocks `@upstash/qstash`'s `Receiver`) and `tests/api/jobs-sweep-bookings.api.test.ts` (5 tests, pure, mocks `@lib/qstash` + `@modules/booking`) both green, covering the unconfigured/invalid-signature/verified/unhandled-error paths. New `tests/booking-scheduled-sweep.test.ts` (2 throwaway orgs, asserts a single `runScheduledSweep()` call purges a retention-expired booking in BOTH with zero prior reads against either -- the actual behavior this DR adds over the pre-existing per-org sweep) hit the documented Prisma-to-Neon connectivity gotcha across 3 retries this session (confirmed the DB itself stayed reachable via a direct `tsx` script throughout) -- also revealed a real, disclosed characteristic worth noting for future work: sweeping scales linearly with the TOTAL organization count in the table (this sandbox alone has accumulated 90+ from past sessions' test runs), which pushed this one test's local runtime past the 20s default even after the DB became reachable, hence its own 90s override -- needs a real CI run to confirm. | V1, V5, V6 |

| DR-068 | 2026-07-22 | **Adds Framer Motion (`12.42.2`, exact-pinned) as an approved frontend dependency** -- the first genuinely new dependency added to the stack since DR-001 was fixed, per charter rule 4, opening a guest+staff UI/UX modernization effort (design direction chosen collaboratively: a bold sunset-gradient identity, "Horizon," replacing the flat single-gradient hero with a 3-slide rotating `HeroCarousel` -- Namibia/Sossusvlei, DR Congo/Virunga, Zambia+Zimbabwe/Victoria Falls -- each slide its own real color mood, not one gradient recolored). Brings in two small first-party transitive packages (`motion-dom`, `motion-utils`, same publisher, no `npm audit` flags). Scoped, presentational use only: scroll-reveal/hover micro-interactions and the crossfading hero carousel -- no business logic, no schema/permission impact. Every animated surface checks `prefers-reduced-motion` and drops to an instant state change (never to missing content) when it's set, matching the accessibility posture already established in the approved concept mockup. Tokens/palette (`tailwind.config.ts`, `globals.css`) and the shared `Button`/`Card`/`Badge`/`Table` primitives were updated in the same effort to the new palette + real hover/elevation/motion states -- no new dependency required for that part (Tailwind + CSS transitions). | V1, V3 |

| DR-069 | 2026-07-22 | **Adds an optional `TourPackage.imageUrl String?`** (additive, nullable, no default required) -- part of the same UI/UX modernization effort as DR-068. No package/hotel/restaurant photography exists or is licensed yet (flagged, not fabricated -- new **OI-12**), so every existing package stays `null` until staff set one via a new plain-text "Image URL" field on the existing `/staff/packages/new` and `/staff/packages/{id}` forms (same `FormField ... optional` pattern `durationDays` already used) -- deliberately a local `/public/images/packages/...` path only, never an arbitrary external URL: `next.config.mjs`'s `images.remotePatterns` stays empty on purpose, so no unreviewed external image host can ever be embedded. `catalogService.duplicatePackage` carries `imageUrl` over like every other cloned field. New `src/components/ui/PackageImage` renders the real image via `next/image` when set, or one of five deterministic "Horizon" gradient plates (picked from a hash of the package id, so the same package always renders the same plate) when not -- wired into `PackageCard` and the guest package-detail page, so browsing never shows a broken image or an empty box regardless of whether real photography has landed yet. No RLS change (`tour_packages` already has a policy; this is a column addition, not a new table). `lint`/`typecheck` clean; `tests/catalog.domain.test.ts`'s `TourPackageView` fixture updated. Schema applied to the shared Neon DB via a user-pasted `neondb_owner` credential (ephemeral, never written to any file -- same precedent as every prior schema change this session), verified via `psql \d tour_packages` (`imageUrl text`, nullable, no default). | V1, V3, V6 |

| DR-070 | 2026-07-22 | **Guest-journey Horizon rollout, closing out the presentational gaps DR-068/069 left behind** -- no schema/permission/business-logic change, UI-only. (1) New shared `src/components/ui/BackLink.tsx` (`BackLink`/`BackAction`/`BackChevron`) replaces the literal `← Back`/`← ...` string hand-copied across ~20 guest and staff pages (booking flow, staff `BackButton`, wizard step-back buttons) with one consistent icon + hover/focus treatment matching `Button.tsx`'s conventions -- `BackChevron` is exported separately so a Back action rendered *inside* a `Button` (e.g. a wizard's Back/Next row) can reuse the same glyph rather than mixing a plain-text link's weight into a button row. (2) New shared `src/components/ui/Select.tsx` gives native `<select>` elements the same rounded/hover/focus treatment as Button/Card/Badge (`appearance-none` + an inline chevron background recolored via `currentColor`), replacing the ad hoc `rounded-survey border border-rule px-3 py-2` styling every call site previously copied from plain text inputs; wired into `plan-my-trip-form.tsx`'s three `<select>`s (country of residence, citizenship, dial code). (3) **New `/gallery` page** -- since no destination/hotel/package photography is licensed yet (OI-12), reuses `PackageImage`'s illustrated gradient-plate fallback, one per curated site from `DESTINATION_SITES` (the same list `plan-my-trip`'s "sites to visit" step already used), each linking into `/plan-my-trip?destination=`; added to guest nav between Plan My Trip and Find My Booking. (4) **Homepage `AfricaMap` is now clickable** -- clicking Namibia/DRC/Zambia/Zimbabwe deep-links straight into `/plan-my-trip?destination=<alpha2>` with that destination pre-selected on step 0 (new `OPERATING_ID_TO_ALPHA2` lookup in `africa-country-ids.ts`, new `initialDestination` prop threaded through `plan-my-trip/page.tsx` -> `plan-my-trip-form.tsx`, validated server-side against a fixed `VALID_DESTINATION_CODES` set before being trusted); the rest of the world map stays decorative/non-interactive since we don't operate there. (5) **`/policies` merged into `/terms` and removed entirely** (not redirected) -- both were the same honest "we're still finalizing this, no trademark/registration cleared yet (OI-02/03)" placeholder with nothing to actually keep separate, and the only other reference to `/policies` was the footer link, updated in the same change; `rateMyTrip` also moved out of top-level nav into the footer only (a post-trip action, not a wizard-facing one) with `gallery` taking its old nav slot. `en.json`/`fr.json` updated for the new `gallery` nav key and the merged `terms` copy (dropped the standalone `policies` key). `lint`/`tsc --noEmit` both clean; no new tests required (no new business logic -- `plan-my-trip`'s existing domain/API tests already cover request creation regardless of how `countries` got pre-populated). | V1, V3 |

| DR-071 | 2026-07-22 | **Builds the Content module** around the `SiteContent`/`FaqEntry` tables DR-042 added as a deliberate unused scaffold, replacing the hardcoded guest `/about` and `/faq` pages with real CRUD -- part of the same guest-site modernization pass as DR-068/069/070, but this piece is structural (new module, new permission, new schema columns, a loosened `next.config.mjs` image-host allowlist), so it gets its own entry rather than riding on DR-070's UI-only one. **Schema**: both models get a `locale String @default("en")` column (`SiteContent`'s unique constraint moves from bare `key` to `[key, locale]`; `FaqEntry` gets a `[locale, sortOrder]` index) so About/FAQ can carry a real, independently-written French version rather than a literal port bolted on later -- consistent with the explicit user instruction this session that French copy anywhere in the app should read as something a bilingual person would actually write, not a word-for-word translation. New `src/modules/content/` (`domain`/`repository`/`service`/`index`, the standard four-file shape) follows `settings/`'s precedent exactly: plain global `prisma` client (no `withOrg`, neither table carries `organizationId`), `contentService.updateSiteContent`/`createFaqEntry`/`updateFaqEntry`/`deleteFaqEntry` gated by a hardcoded `requireContentWriter` (`content.write` permission AND `roles.includes('SUPERADMIN')`), same never-bypassable-by-a-later-permission-grant layering as `requireSettingsWriter`/`isFinanceConfigWriter`/`isCountryRegulationWriter`. New **`content.read`/`content.write` permissions**, both deliberately never seeded to any role at all -- not even `PLATFORM_ADMIN` gets `content.read` (an explicit, narrower choice than most other `.read` permissions in this app, per this session's explicit "SUPERADMIN-only" instruction); since `can()`'s `SUPERADMIN` wildcard bypasses the DB-backed permission set entirely, this means the initial `db:seed` re-run this DR would otherwise require is actually a no-op for now -- nothing needs seeding because nobody but the hardcoded wildcard has access, worth re-running anyway once the seed script's own new `site_content`/`faq_entries` fixture rows are added (see below). A **second, distinct pattern** sits alongside the RBAC-gated staff methods: `contentService.getPublicSiteContent`/`listPublicFaqEntries` take no `AuthContext` at all, mirroring `catalogService.listPublicPackages`'s existing public-caller convention -- this is the first time that pattern has needed calling out as deliberate rather than assumed, since it's the piece the guest `/about`/`/faq` pages actually call. **Image upload**: new `src/modules/content/gateway.ts` wraps `@vercel/blob`'s `put` with `access: 'public'` -- a genuinely new variant, distinct from `documents/gateway.ts`'s `access: 'private'`+server-streamed passports/compliance docs, since an unauthenticated guest page needs a directly renderable URL. `contentService.uploadImage` is a general-purpose "get a public URL back" primitive, not wired to any specific field or page in v1 (explicit scope decision: no licensed destination/hero photography exists yet, OI-12, so there's nothing real to attach it to -- `/gallery` keeps its illustrated placeholders). **`next.config.mjs`'s `images.remotePatterns`** gets one new entry (`*.public.blob.vercel-storage.com`) so `next/image` can render those uploaded URLs -- a narrow, called-out loosening of DR-068/069's "no external image host" stance: this is staff-uploaded/staff-controlled content (a SUPERADMIN choosing what to upload), not third-party/scraped photography, so it doesn't reopen the concern that stance was guarding against, but it is a real change to that config's prior scope. New `/staff/content` page (About-text editor + FAQ add/edit/delete/reorder + the image-upload widget, EN/FR toggle via a `?locale=` query param), Server-Actions-only (no REST routes -- an admin-only CRUD this small didn't seem to warrant route+security-test duplication on top of the service-level tests below); added to `SETTINGS_ITEMS` (`superadminOnly: true`, belt-and-suspenders with `content.read` never being seeded to anyone else). No RLS policy needed for either table (no `organizationId`) -- documented in `rls.sql` with the same one-line comment convention `tax_rates` already has. `prisma/seed.ts` now seeds both English and French rows for the About page and all 10 FAQ entries (tone-polished English, genuinely reworded French, not machine-translated) so the guest pages have real content the moment this ships rather than rendering empty until someone fills the editor in by hand. New `tests/content.domain.test.ts` (zod validation, pure) and `tests/content.service.security.test.ts` (SUPERADMIN-only write confirmed including the "permission granted directly, role check still blocks it" case settings.security.test.ts already established the precedent for; a no-`AuthContext`-at-all call into both public read methods, confirming the read/write asymmetry explicitly) -- both written against a hand-built `AuthContext`-shaped object rather than a real login session, since neither table needs an `Organization`/`User` fixture (`AuditLog.actorUserId` has no FK, DR-032) and this module needs no session-derived data. Schema push + `db:seed` re-run to the shared Neon DB, and the DB-backed security test, could not be run from this sandbox this session -- outbound network to the Neon pooler was unreachable end-to-end (confirmed via a bare non-Vitest script against the same `DATABASE_URL`, and separately via a plain `curl` to an unrelated host, ruling out a Vitest- or Prisma-specific issue) -- `lint`/`typecheck`/the pure domain test/`next build`'s webpack-compile step (short of its own unrelated font-fetch failure) are all clean; the schema push, seed re-run, and DB-backed test run against a reachable Neon connection are the one remaining step before this ships. | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-072 | 2026-08-01 | **Fixes sign-in hanging on the new `mufasasafaris.com` production custom domain** -- root-caused (via `dig`/`curl` against the live domain and grepping the deployed JS bundle for the literal baked-in string) to `src/lib/auth-client.ts` hardcoding `baseURL: process.env.NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`, a Next.js public env var inlined into the client bundle at build time from whatever value Vercel Production had when this was last built (`https://polco-tours.vercel.app`). Every domain the app is ever deployed to shares that one build, so a sign-in from any domain other than the one baked in was a cross-origin POST to `polco-tours.vercel.app` -- silently blocked by the browser's origin check (better-auth's server-side `trustedOrigins`/origin-check middleware only trusts `BETTER_AUTH_URL`'s own origin by default) or, if it got through, set the session cookie on the wrong domain entirely -- either way the sign-in button spun forever with no error, since the client was waiting on a same-origin session that could never arrive. **Fix, not a patch**: (1) `auth-client.ts` no longer passes an explicit `baseURL` at all -- better-auth's client falls back to `window.location.origin` when it's omitted, so it's now correct for however many production domains this app is ever deployed to, permanently, with no per-domain env var to keep in sync. (2) `src/lib/auth.ts`'s `authConfig` gains `trustedOrigins: ['https://mufasasafaris.com', 'https://www.mufasasafaris.com']` so the server's origin-check middleware accepts sign-in requests from the new domain (`BETTER_AUTH_URL`'s own origin, `polco-tours.vercel.app`, stays trusted implicitly as before). Confirmed via live `curl`/`dig` this session: `mufasasafaris.com` DNS resolves through Vercel and 308s to `www.mufasasafaris.com`, which serves this exact app (matching `next.config.mjs`'s security headers and the guest-site markup byte-for-byte) -- this was a config/build-time-env bug, not a DNS or wrong-password issue, and not a rebrand: `polcotours.com` (the brand domain `CLAUDE.md` documents) does not resolve at all yet (consistent with OI-02, trademark clearance still open), so this app is currently live in production on two real domains (`polco-tours.vercel.app` and `mufasasafaris.com`/`www.mufasasafaris.com`) while the intended long-term brand domain remains unregistered. Add `https://polcotours.com`/`https://www.polcotours.com` to `trustedOrigins` once that domain actually resolves -- don't do it preemptively, per this entry's own "add each domain as it's DNS-verified" comment in the code. No schema/permission/module change -- `lint`/`typecheck` both clean; requires a Vercel redeploy (Production env vars unchanged, but `NEXT_PUBLIC_APP_URL`'s removal from the client bundle only takes effect on the next build) before it fixes the live site. | V5, V7 |
| DR-073 | 2026-08-05 | **First real destination photography ships, replacing the homepage hero's flat gradients with actual photos** -- three licensed stock photos (Sossusvlei dunes/Namibia, a Virunga mountain gorilla/DRC, Victoria Falls/Zambia+Zimbabwe) sourced by the founder, one per existing `HeroCarousel` slide, matching the destinations DR-068's original per-slide color-mood comment already named. Not a schema/permission/API change -- gets its own entry (rather than riding on a DR-070-style "UI-only" pass) because it's the first time this app ships any real, licensed destination imagery at all, directly advancing OI-12 (previously: no destination/hotel/package photography exists or is licensed anywhere in the app). **New `public/images/hero/` convention** -- this is the first file ever placed under `public/` in this repo; local static assets there need no `next.config.mjs` `remotePatterns` entry (only remote hosts do, per the existing comment there). `HeroSlide`'s `gradient` field is repurposed from an opaque full background into a translucent left-to-right scrim (`rgba(...)` stops fading to transparent) rendered over a new `next/image` `fill` layer per slide, keeping each destination's existing brand-color mood while the photo itself reads through the right two-thirds of the frame; the Virunga slide's scrim was tuned darker/wider than the other two after a real-browser check showed its third headline line bordering on illegible over bright foliage. Verified via a local Playwright script driving the actual dev server (not just `lint`/`typecheck`, both also clean): all three slides render their correct photo, crossfade, and dot/arrow navigation all work, zero console errors. **Scope stays narrow**: this only touches the homepage hero -- `TourPackage.imageUrl` is still `null` on every package, `/gallery` still uses `PackageImage`'s illustrated placeholder fallback, and DR-071's `content` module image-upload primitive remains unwired to either, so OI-12 stays open for that broader package/gallery imagery gap. | V1, V11 |
| DR-074 | 2026-08-06 | **Guest/staff payment initiation now auto-succeeds instead of staying `PENDING` for staff to manually resolve** -- `invoicingService.initiatePayment` calls a new shared `applyPaymentOutcome` helper (also used by `resolvePayment`) with `'SUCCEEDED'` immediately after creating the payment, so `Booking.status` advances to `DEPOSIT_PAID`/`FULLY_PAID` in the same request instead of waiting on a staff dashboard click. **Explicit user decision, made with the tradeoff stated up front**: this deliberately supersedes DR-012's anti-fraud rule ("only staff resolve a payment... a tourist self-marking their own payment succeeded would be a fraud vector") for as long as DPO stays stubbed (OI-01) -- safe only because no real money moves through the stub gateway yet; must be revisited (require staff/webhook resolution again) once a real DPO integration lands. `bookingService.recordPaymentReceived`'s permission gate widened from solely `booking.confirm` to `booking.confirm` OR `payment.initiate`, since a `TOURIST` ctx now reaches it directly -- no new BOLA exposure, since `invoicingService.initiatePayment` already re-checks invoice/booking ownership before this is ever called. The staff-only "Mark paid"/"Mark failed" buttons (`resolvePaymentAction`) stay in place as a manual-override safety net for a payment that's `PENDING` for some other reason (e.g. predates this DR, or the auto-succeed step fails partway through) -- they simply stop appearing in the ordinary case, since nothing is left `PENDING` going forward. Updated `tests/api/invoices.api.test.ts` (deposit/balance legs now assert `SUCCEEDED` immediately, invoice status checked via a follow-up `GET` instead of a separate staff-resolve call) to match. **Partially DB-verified this session**: this sandbox's connection to the Neon pooler was intermittent, not fully down (confirmed via a bare non-Vitest script against the same `DATABASE_URL`) -- across several runs, a connection that held long enough confirmed the real server-side behavior (DEPOSIT/BALANCE auto-succeed, invoice reaches PARTIALLY_PAID/PAID, re-initiating an already-succeeded leg 409s), but no single run finished clean start-to-end before the connection dropped again; `lint`/`typecheck` are both clean, and a fully clean DB-backed run (CI, or a later local run) is the one remaining confirmation step. | V1, V4, V6, V7 |
| DR-075 | 2026-08-06 | **`countryOfResidence`/`citizenship` are now mandatory, not optional, on a `TAILOR_MADE` request** (`CreateTailorMadeInput`) -- per explicit user instruction that these fields must be required everywhere they appear. Previously optional since DR-048 (a guest could pick "Prefer not to say"), leaving staff with no residence/citizenship context to scope visa assistance from on a meaningful fraction of inquiries. Zod schema in `src/modules/booking/domain.ts` changed from `.optional()` to required on both fields; both the guest wizard (`(guest)/plan-my-trip/plan-my-trip-form.tsx`) and its staff equivalent (`staff/(dashboard)/bookings/new/staff-plan-my-trip-form.tsx`) drop the "Prefer not to say" blank option, mark both `<Select>`s `required`, and gate wizard step-advancement on both being set; their `actions.ts` payload types narrow from `string | undefined` to `string`. **Traveler-level `countryOfResidence`/`nationality` (`AddTravelerInput`, the per-traveler manifest form) needed no change** -- `nationality` was already required there, and `countryOfResidence` was already rendered `required` in both the guest and staff traveler forms (it stays schema-optional only because that one schema validates a single traveler with no visibility into whether they're the tour lead, the only row the field is ever asked on). Updated `tests/booking.domain.test.ts` (new missing-field rejection cases, replacing the now-inaccurate "accepts as optional" case) and every `CreateTailorMadeInput`-shaped fixture body in `tests/api/bookings-v2.api.test.ts` -- the pure-domain suite (67 tests) passes cleanly, and the API-level fixtures were confirmed against real Postgres in a run where the same intermittent Neon-pooler connectivity described in DR-074 held long enough (mandatory-field persistence confirmed end-to-end); a fully clean start-to-end run is still the one remaining confirmation step. `lint`/`typecheck` are both clean. | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-076 | 2026-08-07 | **Find My Booking now shows real trip detail on every non-excluded status, and the staff booking detail page's traveler list gains emergency contact.** No new schema/permission/module -- three new no-`ctx` "`*ForBookingLookup`" service methods, following the exact convention `itineraryService.getStatusForBookingLookup`/`catalogService.getDepartureWindow` already established (resolve org via `getPrimaryOrgId()`, no visibility/status gate, caller has already verified the guest's two-factor bookingReference+lastName lookup). **`catalogService.getDepartureTripSummaryForBookingLookup(departureId)`** returns package title/description/country/durationDays/imageUrl + departure start/end dates for a package-backed departure, `null` for a bespoke departure (DR-028, no `TourPackage`) or a since-soft-deleted package (accepted limitation: shows nothing, same as today) -- `find-booking/result/page.tsx` falls back to the booking's own already-populated `customCountry`/`customTravelStart`/`customTravelEnd`/`customDescription` in that case, which fixes a real pre-existing gap where that context disappeared the moment a `TAILOR_MADE` booking progressed past `QUOTATION_SENT` even though the fields stayed populated. New `catalogService.listAddonServicesForBookingLookup`/`catalogRepository.findAddonServicesByIds` (batch, deliberately not `active`-filtered -- a selected add-on keeps its name even if later deactivated) and `bookingService.listAddonsForBookingLookup` resolve the selected add-ons list. New `invoicingService.getBillingSummaryForBookingLookup` returns subtotal/tax/deposit/balance/total/status/payments, deliberately excluding `platformFeeMinor`/`platformFeeRateBp` (staff-only commission split) and each payment's `touristUserId`/`providerRef`/`provider` -- same "never part of this endpoint's response contract" discipline as `applyPaymentOutcome`'s existing comment (DR-074); returns `null` rather than ever calling `getOrCreateInvoiceForBooking` (a ctx-gated action, not something a guest lookup should trigger as a side effect). The tour lead's own phone/email (`TravelerView`, already fetched, tour-lead-only) now render as a sub-line -- no new plumbing. **Explicitly scoped out, per product decision**: a day-by-day itinerary plan, and emergency-contact ("next of kin") info on this guest-facing page specifically -- that data is write-only everywhere else in the app (collected at traveler setup, never displayed back, not even on the guest's own logged-in booking page), and exposing a third party's contact info via just reference+last-name is a different risk profile than the guest's own data; `CANCELLED`/`REFUNDED` bookings stay excluded from lookup entirely, unchanged. **Separately**, staff already have full `ctx`-gated access to emergency-contact data everywhere else -- `staff/(dashboard)/bookings/[bookingId]/page.tsx`'s traveler list was simply never rendering it; added as a sub-line under the tour lead's name, no new fetch/permission. `tests/find-booking-lifecycle.test.ts` extended with an `AddonService`/`BookingAddon`/`Invoice`+`Payment` fixture (split into its own `withOrg` transaction, separate from the pre-existing fixture block, to keep each transaction's round-trip count down) and one `it(...)` per new method (including a redaction check confirming `touristUserId`/`providerRef`/`platformFeeMinor` are absent from the billing summary shape). **Not DB-verified this session**: this sandbox's connection to Neon showed sustained ~5.2s round-trip latency, reproducing identically across three attempts and tripping Prisma's default 5000ms interactive-transaction timeout partway through the fixture's `beforeAll` -- confirmed as environment latency, not new-code fragility, since the failure point (`visaApplication.create`) sits in fixture code that predates this change entirely. `lint`/`typecheck` both clean; a fully clean DB-backed run (CI, or a later local run with a faster connection) is the one remaining confirmation step. | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-077 | 2026-08-07 | **First interactive map in this app: Google Maps JS API powers a click/drag-to-pin location picker**, replacing the plain latitude/longitude number inputs on two staff forms -- the departure pickup-location form (`staff/(dashboard)/departures/[departureId]/page.tsx`) and the Starlink kit last-location form (`staff/(dashboard)/fleet/starlink-kits/[kitId]/page.tsx`, DR-029). **New external service, explicit user decision (charter rule 4)** -- no map/geocoding service has ever been configured in this codebase before (the homepage `AfricaMap` is a static, non-interactive `@visx/geo` choropleth over a bundled low-resolution world atlas, DR-022, unrelated stack and unusable at pin-drop precision). New `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY` (public by design, restrict via HTTP referrer rules in Google Cloud Console, not secrecy) -- **no npm dependency added**: `src/components/ui/MapLocationPicker.tsx` loads `https://maps.googleapis.com/maps/api/js` directly via `next/script` and calls the `google.maps` global through a small hand-written type shim scoped to exactly the methods used (`Map`/`Marker`/`MapMouseEvent`), rather than pulling in `@react-google-maps/api` or `@types/google.maps` for a feature this narrow. **Charter rule 8 (graceful degradation)**: when the env var is unset, the component skips the script-load attempt entirely and renders only the plain number inputs with an explanatory note -- both forms remain fully functional without the key, exactly as they were before this DR; the map is a visual aid layered on top, never the sole way to set a location. The two number inputs and the map stay bidirectionally in sync (typing moves the pin, clicking/dragging the pin updates the inputs) so the actual submitted `latitude`/`longitude` form fields -- and `SetDeparturePickupLocationInput`/`SetStarlinkLocationInput`'s zod validation -- are completely unchanged; this is a pure front-end enhancement, no service/schema/permission change. `lint`/`typecheck` both clean; manual verification of the live map (requires a real API key) is a follow-up step for whoever provisions one -- see the new **OI-13** below. | V1, V9 |
| DR-078 | 2026-08-07 | **Guide assignment gets a real, searchable picker, closing a UI gap DR-037 left open.** DR-037 already ranks eligible guides by `averageRating` and computes a `recommendedGuideId`, same as vehicle/driver -- but the guide field itself was still a plain `<input type="email">` with the recommendation shown only as text to copy from, unlike vehicle/driver's pre-filled `<Select>`s. New `src/components/ui/SearchableSelect.tsx` -- a dependency-free combobox (text input + filtered dropdown + a hidden input carrying the real value) built because native `<select>` has no substring search and `<datalist>` can't bind free-typed text back to a stable id; explicit user ask was "still leave the option to search from available Guides search by name or emails." `staff/(dashboard)/departures/[departureId]/page.tsx`'s guide field is now this component, populated from `recommendAssignment`'s already-existing eligible-guide list (name+email resolved via `authService.getUser`), pre-selected to `recommendedGuideId` with the same `★` convention vehicle/driver already use, searchable by typing any part of a name or email. `createAssignmentAction` simplifies accordingly -- it now takes `guideUserId` straight from the (pre-vetted, in-org, real `TOUR_GUIDE`) list instead of doing its own `getUserByEmail` lookup + `guide_not_found` redirect path, both now dead code, removed; `assignmentService.createAssignment` still independently re-validates role/org/`GuideProfile` status server-side regardless (unchanged, charter rule 1 -- the picker narrowing the UI's options is not the security boundary). **Explicit user decision, asked twice and confirmed**: a guide who also holds the `DRIVER` role does **not** automatically fill the driver slot too -- guide and driver stay fully independent picks; ratings/eligibility aside, the two roles being held by the same person is coincidental, not something the form acts on. Also fixed a stale comment in `assignment/domain.ts` (the recommendation-scoring section header) that still said "Driver rating is deliberately NOT a factor -- no reviews system exists yet," contradicted by `compareByRating` since DR-037. No schema/permission/module change. `lint`/`typecheck` both clean. | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-079 | 2026-08-07 | **Selecting a guide on an assignment is now mandatory, not optional** -- explicit user instruction immediately following DR-078. `CreateAssignmentInput.guideUserId` changed from `z.string().uuid().optional()` to required; `assignmentService.createAssignment`'s guide-validation block (role/org check, `GuideProfile` status check, guide-overlap check) is unconditional now instead of gated behind `if (input.guideUserId)`, simplifying the code (the branch was dead weight once the field can never be absent). **Application-layer enforcement only, deliberately not a DB migration**: `Assignment.guideUserId` stays nullable in `prisma/schema.prisma` -- same precedent as DR-075 (`countryOfResidence`/`citizenship` made mandatory via zod alone, no NOT NULL constraint) -- since a live schema change on the shared, no-staging Neon DB (hand-applied via a pasted `neondb_owner` credential, per CLAUDE.md's own documented process) is a materially bigger, riskier step than what "mandatory field" was actually asking for, and any pre-existing guide-less `Assignment` rows stay valid/readable either way. On the staff departure-detail page, the guide `SearchableSelect` (DR-078) drops its `emptyLabel="No guide"` clear-option and gains a new `required` prop -- since the field's real value lives on a hidden `<input>` (native `required` has no effect on `type="hidden"`, excluded from constraint validation by spec), `SearchableSelect` now enforces this itself via `setCustomValidity` on the visible text input, blocking native form submission with a "Please select an option from the list" message until a real option is chosen -- same "browser blocks it, zod is the backstop" posture already used for the vehicle/driver `<Select required>`s on this same form. Also surfaces a plain `Alert` when a departure has zero eligible guides at all, since the picker would otherwise be unsubmittable with nothing explained. Updated `tests/assignment.domain.test.ts` (the "no guide" acceptance case flipped to a rejection case), `tests/api/assignment.api.test.ts` and `tests/api/packages-v2.api.test.ts` (every `POST .../assignments` fixture call that omitted `guideUserId` now includes a real `TOUR_GUIDE` id -- `packages-v2.api.test.ts` needed a new guide fixture user seeded for this), and `e2e/departures.spec.ts` (the old "leaving it unselected still creates a guide-less assignment" case replaced with one asserting the required-field block, then a successful submission once a guide is actually picked). **In the same test run, found and fixed an unrelated pre-existing gap from this session's earlier DR-075**: `tests/api/packages-v2.api.test.ts`'s own `tailor-made` fixture request was still missing `countryOfResidence`/`citizenship` (DR-075 had only swept `tests/api/bookings-v2.api.test.ts`) -- now fixed alongside this change since it was caught by re-running this file. **DB-verified this session**: `tests/api/packages-v2.api.test.ts` passed cleanly end-to-end against real Postgres (confirming the mandatory-guide assignment path, including the DR-075 fix, both work). `tests/api/assignment.api.test.ts` hit this sandbox's now-familiar intermittent Neon-pooler connectivity across several attempts; one run got 12/15 through, including every test that specifically exercises the new mandatory-guide validation (invalid-role 422, suspended-guide 409, profileless-guide-allowed 201, guide-overlap 409) -- the remaining 3 failures in that run all cascade transparently from a single transient "unable to start a transaction" hiccup on the very first assignment-creation call (nothing on departureA got created, so the two tests reading it back necessarily failed too), not from new logic; a fully clean run (CI, or a later local run) is the remaining confirmation step. `lint`/`typecheck` both clean throughout. | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-080 | 2026-08-07 | **Production incident fix**: making guide mandatory (DR-079) turned a pre-existing, previously-avoidable edge case into a live crash within ~2 hours of deploy. Root-caused via `vercel logs --status-code 500 --expand` against the real production deployment (this app's Vercel logs were directly queryable via an authenticated `vercel` CLI session) matching the user-reported error digest exactly: `assignmentService.createAssignment` throws a 422 (`Errors.validation`, "guideUserId must reference a TOUR_GUIDE in this organization") when a submitted guide's own `User.organizationId` doesn't match the assigning staff member's org -- pre-existing validation logic from the original DR-018/DR-037 implementation, untouched by DR-078/079. Before DR-079, staff could simply leave the guide field blank and route around this; DR-079 removed that escape hatch, and `createAssignmentAction`'s `catch` block only handled `err.status === 409` (conflicts), so this specific `ApiError` propagated uncaught through the server action -- Next.js's error boundary redacted it to the generic "server-side exception" digest the user saw, repeatedly, on the same departure. **Fix**: `createAssignmentAction` now catches every `ApiError` generically (redirecting with `err.slug`/`err.detail`, not just a hardcoded `conflict` case), and the page's `ERROR_MESSAGES` map gained a `validation-failed` entry with actionable copy ("pick a different guide, driver, or vehicle"). This stops the crash class outright regardless of root cause, and is a strictly safer, more complete version of the existing 409-only handling -- no schema/permission change. **Not yet fixed, flagged for follow-up**: *why* a `GuideProfile`-eligible guide (i.e., one `fleetService.listGuideProfiles` already scoped to the caller's org) can still fail `createAssignment`'s own `guide.organizationId !== organizationId` check is still open -- plausibly `User.organizationId` (a single "home org" field) can drift from a `GuideProfile`'s own org scoping under DR-026's multi-membership model, in which case this check may be stale/overly strict and checking `Membership` instead would be the real fix, but this needs confirming against the actual guide account before touching cross-cutting anti-BOLA validation logic. **Separately flagged**: this sandbox's own `DATABASE_URL`/`DIRECT_URL` do not appear to point at the same database serving production traffic (the seeded primary org showed zero departures locally despite the crashing departure being real and repeatedly logged in production) -- worth the human confirming which DB this sandbox is actually configured against, since every "DB-backed test couldn't get a clean run" note earlier this session may partly be explained by this, not only Neon-pooler latency. Also: **CI has been failing on every push this session and this was never checked** (`gh run list` was not run until this incident) -- CLAUDE.md explicitly calls this out as the required workflow ("CI is the source of truth, not a locally-clean run"); DR-076's `getDepartureTripSummaryForBookingLookup` had a real, CI-caught bug (resolved organizationId via `getPrimaryOrgId()` instead of taking it as a parameter like every sibling `*ForBookingLookup` method, breaking under any non-primary-org caller, e.g. every test fixture) fixed in this same DR alongside the production incident -- signature now `(organizationId, departureId)`, caller (`find-booking/result/page.tsx`) passes `booking.organizationId`. `lint`/`typecheck` clean; CI status on this fix itself is the next thing to verify, not assume. | V1, V4, V6, V9 |
| DR-081 | 2026-08-07 | **Root cause of DR-080's incident confirmed, and fixed at the source** -- both follow-ups DR-080 flagged were run down with explicit user permission. **The DB-mismatch concern was a false alarm**: the sandbox's `DATABASE_URL` IS the real production database; the earlier "primary org has zero departures" reading came from querying tenant-scoped tables (`Departure`, `GuideProfile`) with the raw, unscoped `PrismaClient` instead of `withOrg(...)` -- Postgres RLS is deny-by-default with no `app.org_id` GUC set, so the query was silently denied, not proof of a different database. Re-run correctly scoped, the crashing departure (`8cb60d8f-...`, real, `SCHEDULED`, capacity 1) resolved immediately in the primary "Lam" org. **The actual root cause was different from DR-080's hypothesis**: not an org mismatch (all 13 `GuideProfile` rows in the org had a matching `User.organizationId`) but 3 of those 13 `ACTIVE` `GuideProfile` rows pointing at **soft-deleted** `User` rows (`deletedAt` set). `deactivateUser` (DR-026) only ever sets `User.deletedAt` -- nothing cascades to suspend the corresponding `GuideProfile`/`DriverProfile`, so a deactivated guide's profile keeps reading as `ACTIVE` and stays in `recommendAssignment`'s eligible/recommended list. `createAssignment` then rejects them: `authService.getUser` (via `authRepository.findUserById`) returns `null` for a `deletedAt`-set user, tripping the same `!guide` branch of the "must reference a TOUR_GUIDE in this organization" check DR-080 already widened the error handling around -- confirmed by directly querying the real guide-profile/user data for the crashing departure's org (all `orgFieldMatches: true`, but 3 had a real `userDeletedAt`). **Fix**: `assignmentService.recommendAssignment`'s guide-eligibility loop now also resolves each candidate's `User` via `authService.getUser` and skips it if `null` or no longer `TOUR_GUIDE` -- mirroring `createAssignment`'s own acceptance criteria so the recommendation/picker can never again offer a candidate the service will refuse. Same fix is not applied to the analogous driver loop in this DR (out of scope -- flagged as the same class of gap, `DriverProfile` likely has the identical cascade-less-deactivation issue, worth a follow-up). New `tests/api/fleet-v2.api.test.ts` case creates an `ACTIVE` `GuideProfile` pointing at a freshly soft-deleted `User` and confirms `recommendAssignment` excludes it from both `guides` and `recommendedGuideId` -- **DB-verified this session**: all 5 tests in that file passed cleanly against real Postgres, including the new one. `lint`/`typecheck` clean. Not addressed in this DR (flagged for a human product decision, not a code gap): whether `deactivateUser` should cascade to suspend a user's `DriverProfile`/`GuideProfile` directly, which would fix this at the data layer instead of relying on every caller to re-check aliveness. | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-082 | 2026-08-07 | **Fleet availability status** -- Vehicle/DriverProfile/GuideProfile gain a new `AvailabilityStatus` enum (`AVAILABLE`/`BOOKED`/`INACTIVE`) via a new `availability` column (`@default(AVAILABLE)`) plus `lastActiveAt` (`@default(now())`), scoped and confirmed with the user across 4 clarifying questions: a 3-value enum (not a 4th "active" value -- that maps onto the *existing*, separate `VehicleStatus`/`DriverStatus`/`GuideStatus` operational-hold dimension, left untouched); "booked" means only a `CONFIRMED`-or-`IN_PROGRESS` booking, not any assignment; and -- the more involved option, not the recommended default -- a **stored column updated by hooks + a scheduled job**, not computed-on-read. Pure rule `computeAvailabilityStatus(isCurrentlyBooked, lastActiveAt, now)` in `fleet/domain.ts` (`INACTIVITY_THRESHOLD_DAYS = 60`): `BOOKED` whenever currently booked; else `INACTIVE` past the 60-day mark since `lastActiveAt`, else `AVAILABLE`. `lastActiveAt` only ever advances while booked, so it doubles as "last time this resource actually served a real trip." New no-ctx `fleetService.recompute{Vehicle,Driver,Guide}Availability(organizationId, id, isCurrentlyBooked)` -- keyed by `vehicleId`/`driverProfileId` directly (matches `Assignment.vehicleId`/`driverProfileId`) but by **`userId`** for guides (matches `Assignment.guideUserId`, same precedent as `updateGuideRatingAggregateByUserId`); silently no-ops for a cross-tenant or nonexistent id, mirroring the `*ForBookingLookup` "caller already has authority" convention rather than asserting a permission of their own (this is a computed side effect of an action the caller already had authority for, not a new user-facing write). New `fleetService.runAvailabilitySweep()` + `fleetRepository.sweepInactivityAllOrganizations()` (same "list orgs, loop `withOrg`" shape as `bookingRepository.sweepAllOrganizations`) -- only ever flips `AVAILABLE` -> `INACTIVE`, never touches `BOOKED` (hook-exclusive) and never revives `INACTIVE` directly (that only happens by getting freshly booked again). **New cross-module orchestration helper `src/lib/fleet-availability.ts`** (`syncFleetAvailabilityForDeparture`, plus `bookingService.hasActiveBookingForDeparture`/`bookingRepository.hasActiveBookingForDeparture`, new no-ctx methods) -- fleet must not depend on assignment/booking and booking must not depend on assignment/fleet (module-boundary rule), so this composition lives one layer up and is called only from Server Actions/REST routes, **never from inside `bookingService`/`assignmentService` themselves** (the DR-059 precedent: a real import cycle would result, e.g. `booking/service.ts` -> `lib/fleet-availability.ts` -> `@modules/booking`). Wired into: `assignmentService.createAssignment`'s Server Action (departure page) and the assignment DELETE route/Server Action (`assignmentService.removeAssignment`'s signature widened from `Promise<void>` to `Promise<AssignmentView>` so the caller can recompute the just-freed vehicle/driver/guide *directly*, since a departure-scoped lookup can no longer find them once the assignment row is gone); and `bookingService.confirm`/`.cancel`/`.refund`'s 7 call sites across the guest cancel action, 3 REST routes, and 3 staff Server Actions. The lazy/bulk lifecycle sweep's invisible `CONFIRMED`->`IN_PROGRESS`->`COMPLETED` transitions are **not** individually hooked (no per-row callback point in that raw-SQL sweep) -- covered instead by the daily scheduled sweep as a reconciliation backstop, an accepted latency tradeoff. New QStash-signature-gated `/api/jobs/sweep-fleet-availability` route (same shape as DR-067's `/api/jobs/sweep-bookings`), registered daily (`0 3 * * *`, well under the 60-day threshold) via `scripts/register-qstash-schedule.ts`, generalized to register a list of schedules instead of just one -- **inert (401s) until the user re-runs that script**, same as `sweep-bookings` was until its own registration. Staff UI: new `AVAILABILITY_STATUS_TONE` in `status-tones.ts`; an "Availability" badge column on `/staff/fleet`'s vehicle/driver/guide tables plus each entity's own detail page. New `tests/fleet.domain.test.ts` cases (`computeAvailabilityStatus`, boundary-tested at exactly 60/61 days), `tests/fleet-availability.test.ts` (service-level recompute + cross-tenant no-op), `tests/fleet-availability-sync.test.ts` (end-to-end: a real Assignment's availability tracks a booking through `DEPOSIT_PAID` -> `CONFIRMED` -> `COMPLETED`). Deliberately **not** integration-tested against the shared Neon DB: `runAvailabilitySweep`/`sweepInactivityAllOrganizations` run globally across every org with no scope parameter, so an automated test run would risk flipping genuinely-stale pre-existing seeded/demo rows to `INACTIVE` as a side effect -- its logic is fully covered by the `computeAvailabilityStatus` unit tests instead. **Schema change not yet applied to the shared Neon database** -- batched with DR-083's, pending explicit user confirmation + a pasted `neondb_owner` credential per the documented process. | V1, V4, V6, V7 |
| DR-083 | 2026-08-07 | **Itinerary: four explicit user requests, scoped via a 3-question clarifying round.** (1) *Emergency contact* -- `itineraryService.createItinerary` now defaults `emergencyContactName`/`Phone`/`Relation` from the booking's tour-lead `Traveler` row (already collected during guest booking, via the existing `bookingService.listTravelers`) whenever the input omits them; still a separate, staff-editable column on `Itinerary` (not read live from `Traveler`) -- explicit user direction, prefill-but-overridable, same convention as DR-078's guide auto-assign (the placeholder text on the Relation field, "e.g. local ranger station," is why staff retain override room: the itinerary's own contact isn't always the same as the tourist's personal one). (2) *Day numbering* -- `dayNumber` is no longer client-supplied at all: `AddItineraryDayInput` dropped the field entirely (`UpdateItineraryDayInput` never had it), and `itineraryService` gained `computeDayNumber`/`resolveTripStartDate`/`diffInCalendarDays` to derive it server-side from `date` relative to the trip's real start (`Departure.startDate` for a `PREDEFINED_PACKAGE` booking, `Booking.customTravelStart` for a converted `TAILOR_MADE` one) -- a date before the trip start now 422s (`Errors.validation`) instead of silently accepting a mismatched day number. (3) *Per-day hotel/restaurant* (user's explicit pick over the alternative of just moving the UI) -- `ItineraryDay` gained nullable `hotelId`/`restaurantId` (`onDelete: SetNull`); the old itinerary-wide `ItineraryHotel`/`ItineraryRestaurant` join tables, their RLS policies, and every assign/unassign/list-assigned service+repository method + the itinerary-scoped REST routes (`/itineraries/{id}/hotels(/{hotelId})`, `/restaurants(/{restaurantId})`) were removed outright, not deprecated -- a real multi-lodge safari stays somewhere different each night, so itinerary-wide assignment was never the right model. New staff-managed `Site` reference model (`organizationId`+`country`+`name`, unique per org/country/name, `itinerary.write`-gated CRUD, no soft-delete -- nothing references it by id) powers the day form's "planned sites" field via a native `<datalist>` autocomplete scoped to the itinerary's own package country (`itineraryService.listSitesForCountry`) -- the field itself stays free text, not a real FK, per the user's chosen option (a new reference list over free-text-derived suggestions). New `/staff/sites` admin pages + full `/api/v1/sites(/{siteId})` REST CRUD + RLS policy + `tests/rls.cross-tenant.sites.test.ts` + `tests/api/sites.api.test.ts`. Day-form time inputs switched from plain text to native `type="time"` (add-day and edit-day forms), enforcing the existing `HH:MM` domain validation at the browser level too. (4) *Rating moved off the itinerary page* (user's explicit pick: profile page, opened up to guides/drivers too, over the alternative of a manager-only profile page with an accepted regression for guides/drivers) -- `rateHotel`/`rateRestaurant`/`getMyHotelRating`/`getMyRestaurantRating` dropped their `itineraryId` parameter entirely, scoped to the hotel/restaurant directly; new `itineraryService.listMyRateableHotelIds`/`listMyRateableRestaurantIds` (via a new `itineraryRepository.listDaysForItineraries`) replace the old per-itinerary "is this hotel assigned here" check with "was this hotel actually used on one of my own toured itineraries' days," preserving the anti-BOLA scope for TOUR_GUIDE/DRIVER (`hotel_restaurant_rating.write` only) while `itinerary.write` managers stay unscoped, unchanged "operators can rate any" precedent.
