Rating UI moved from the itinerary detail page onto `/staff/hotels/[hotelId]`/`/staff/restaurants/[restaurantId]`, whose gate widened from `itinerary.write`-only to `itinerary.read` (broad view/rate access) with conditional edit-vs-rate-only rendering by permission -- `StaffNav`'s Hotels/Restaurants links widened to `anyOfPermissions: ['itinerary.write', 'hotel_restaurant_rating.write']` so guides/drivers can actually discover the page, which they could not reach at all before (previously gated `itinerary.write`-only). REST rating routes moved from `/itineraries/{itineraryId}/hotels/{hotelId}/rating` to top-level `/hotels/{hotelId}/rating` (and the restaurant counterpart). `docs/openapi.yaml` updated throughout to match (removed paths, added the two rating paths + full Sites CRUD, and opportunistically documented `Hotel`/`Restaurant.averageRating`/`ratingCount`, a pre-existing gap noticed while editing this section). New/updated tests: `tests/api/hotel-restaurant-rating.api.test.ts` and `tests/api/itinerary.api.test.ts` rewritten for the new fixture/route shapes; `tests/rls.cross-tenant.itinerary-hotels.test.ts`/`itinerary-restaurants.test.ts` deleted (the tables they tested no longer exist). **Schema change not yet applied to the shared Neon database** -- batched with DR-082's, pending explicit user confirmation + a pasted `neondb_owner` credential per the documented process. | V1, V4, V6, V7 |
| DR-084 | 2026-08-07 | **User dormancy: a staff account is flagged inactive, and sign-in is actually blocked, after 30 days without signing in.** New `User.inactiveAt` (nullable `DateTime`) -- a separate axis from the existing `deletedAt` (DR-026's manual, permanent deactivation): `inactiveAt` is automatic, staff-roles-only, and reversible by any `admin.all` holder, whereas `deletedAt` is a deliberate admin action with no automatic trigger. Scoped via 3 clarifying questions: staff roles only (TOURIST accounts are anonymous-session-based -- "30 days without signing in" doesn't map onto them at all); folded into the existing Active/Deactivated Status column on `/staff/admin/users` as a third state (`Deactivated` always wins when both are true, since that account can't sign in regardless of dormancy); and -- the more involved option over the recommended "informational badge only" default -- **sign-in is actually blocked** until an admin clicks Reactivate, not just a label. Pure rule `isDormant(referenceDate, now)` in `auth/domain.ts` (`DORMANCY_THRESHOLD_DAYS = 30`) -- `referenceDate` is `lastLoginAt` if the account has ever signed in, else `createdAt` (a staff account created but never once logged into is exactly as dormant as one that logged in 30+ days ago and never came back). New `authRepository.markDormantUsers(now)` (the scheduled-sweep entry point) -- staff roles only, and **excludes `SUPERADMIN`** (the one hardcoded, permanently-uneditable role; locking out the only superadmin would strand the whole platform with no one left able to reactivate them) -- a single global `updateMany` with no per-org loop, since (confirmed via the existing gotcha) `users` carries no RLS policy at all, unlike tenant tables. New `authRepository.reactivateUser`/`authService.reactivateUser` (`admin.all`, same gate as `deactivateUser`) -- the only way a dormant account is ever restored, since a dormant user can't authenticate to trigger anything themselves. **The sign-in block itself is a new `databaseHooks.session.create.before` hook in `src/lib/auth.ts`**, added alongside the existing `session.create.after` (lastLoginAt-tracking) hook -- reads `inactiveAt` via a direct Prisma query rather than `ctx.context.internalAdapter` (confirmed by reading the adapter's own `transformOutput` source: it only round-trips fields registered as `additionalFields`, and `inactiveAt`, like `lastLoginAt`, is deliberately not one) and throws `APIError.from('FORBIDDEN', { code: 'ACCOUNT_INACTIVE' })` -- the exact mechanism (and, once found, the confirmation that throwing from this hook cleanly aborts sign-in) came from reading better-auth's own official `admin` plugin source, which uses the identical hook for its built-in ban-user feature. New QStash-signature-gated `/api/jobs/sweep-user-dormancy` (daily, `0 4 * * *`) registered in `scripts/register-qstash-schedule.ts` alongside the other two scheduled jobs -- inert until the user re-runs that script. New `tests/auth.domain.test.ts` (`isDormant`, boundary-tested at 30/31 days) + `tests/auth-dormancy.test.ts` (real `auth.api.signInEmail` calls, mirroring `auth-last-login-hook.test.ts`'s "exercise the actual API, not `loginAs()`" precedent, since better-auth's `testUtils` login mints a Session directly and never touches `session.create.before` at all). **A real test bug was caught and fixed while verifying this**: the first draft's fixture email embedded the role in uppercase (`DRIVER`), and better-auth's `findUserByEmail` lookup is case-sensitive -- every sign-in attempt failed with `INVALID_EMAIL_OR_PASSWORD` before ever reaching the dormancy check, and the test still "passed" because it only asserted a bare `.rejects.toThrow()`, which any unrelated failure would also satisfy. Fixed by lowercasing the fixture email and tightening both assertions to the specific `FORBIDDEN`/`ACCOUNT_INACTIVE` shape -- a bare `toThrow()` is no longer trusted for this class of check. Deliberately **not** integration-tested: `markDormantUsers` itself, for the same reason as `fleetRepository.sweepInactivityAllOrganizations` (DR-082) -- a global `updateMany` with no scoping parameter would risk marking a genuinely-stale real staff account dormant as a side effect of running the test suite against the shared Neon database; its logic is fully covered by the `isDormant` unit test instead. Schema pushed to the shared Neon database (`User.inactiveAt`, purely additive/nullable, zero migration risk regardless of existing row count) in the same session as DR-085/086. | V1, V4, V5, V7 |
| DR-085 | 2026-08-07 | **Client deletion: a SUPERADMIN can delete a client (bare/anonymous TOURIST contact record) from the Clients directory, but only once every one of their non-deleted bookings is `COMPLETED` and reviewed** (a booking a superadmin already soft-deleted doesn't count against them at all). New `authService.deleteClient` (SUPERADMIN-only via `isSuperAdmin`, narrower than `listClients`' SUPERADMIN/TOUR_OPERATOR view access) performs a soft-delete (reuses `deletedAt`/`softDeleteUser`, same mechanism as staff deactivation -- consistent with this app's established "soft-delete, no hard cascade" precedent for anything with real history attached, rather than a real `DELETE` that would cascade away a completed/reviewed booking's history via `Booking.touristUserId`'s `onDelete: Cascade`). **The actual business-rule guard is NOT inside `authService`**: new `src/lib/client-deletion.ts` (`assertClientDeletable`) composes `bookingService`/`ratingsService`, and lives one layer up from `auth` for the same reason `fleet-availability.ts` (DR-082) does -- `auth` cannot depend on `booking`/`ratings` (booking already depends on auth; the reverse would be a real circular import). New `bookingService.listForTourist` (staff-only, arbitrary `touristUserId` rather than just the caller's own -- the one new booking-module surface this needed). New `admin/clients/actions.ts` (`deleteClientAction`) orchestrates: guard check, then the service call, catching `ApiError` and redirecting with the conflict detail shown as an `Alert` on the Clients page (same `?error=&detail=` convention as the departures page's assignment-error display). New `tests/client-deletion.test.ts` -- 5 cases (no bookings; active/`CONFIRMED`; `COMPLETED`-but-unreviewed; `COMPLETED`-and-reviewed; only booking already soft-deleted), fixtures built directly via Prisma rather than through `ratingsService.submitRating`'s public guest flow, which is hardcoded to `getPrimaryOrgId()` and so isn't usable against an isolated test-fixture org. No schema change. | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-086 | 2026-08-07 | **A batch of smaller changes from the same session as DR-084/085.** (1) *Confirm-before-delete, platform-wide* -- every destructive delete/deactivate `SubmitButton` (~20 files: fleet, hotels/restaurants/sites, bookings, clients, users, country regulations, finance/tax/platform rates, itinerary days, package template days, FAQ entries, assignment removal) now shows a native `confirm()` dialog before submitting, via a new `confirmMessage` prop on the shared `SubmitButton` component (cancelling calls `preventDefault()` on the submit button's own click event, which blocks the form submission it would otherwise trigger). Explicit user direction, scoped to actual delete/remove/deactivate actions -- ordinary workflow status transitions (Confirm/Cancel/Refund/Archive/Approve) were left alone. (2) *Site gains `province` (required, country-dependent dropdown) and `city` (optional) fields* -- new `src/lib/provinces.ts` (`PROVINCES_BY_COUNTRY`, real administrative regions for the 4 operating countries only; any other country falls back to a free-text province input) and a new client component `site-form.tsx` shared by the add/edit pages (the one part of this reference-data CRUD that needs client-side interactivity, since province options cascade from the selected country). Schema-safe to add as a required column: the `sites` table had zero rows at push time. `docs/openapi.yaml` and the existing Sites fixtures/tests updated to match. (3) *Sites moved from the top-level staff nav into the Settings sidebar* (explicit user direction) -- Hotels/Restaurants stay top-level since they're edited far more often day-to-day. (4) *Budget and Luxury trip preferences are mutually exclusive* -- both `plan-my-trip` forms (guest and staff-on-behalf-of) gained a `toggleTag` helper that clears the opposite tag when one is picked, and `CreateTailorMadeInput` gained a `.refine()` rejecting both at once server-side (charter rule: the frontend prevents it for UX, the backend is still the source of truth). (5) *Fixed a real bug*: the Fleet Locations "Update" link on `/staff/tracking` was built from `StarlinkKit.kitId` (the human-readable label, e.g. "KIT-001") instead of the kit's real row id, so it always 404'd -- `TrackingModule`'s `FleetLocationView` gained a separate `starlinkKitId` field (the real id) alongside the unchanged display-only `kitId`; new regression assertion in `tests/api/tracking.api.test.ts`. Schema changes (2) pushed to the shared Neon database in the same session as DR-084/085. | V1, V4, V6, V7 |
| DR-087 | 2026-08-07 | **Two follow-ups to DR-086, same session.** (1) *Site country restricted to this app's 4 operating countries* (explicit user direction) -- new `SITE_COUNTRIES`/`SITE_COUNTRY_CODES` in `src/lib/provinces.ts` (Namibia, DR Congo, Zambia, Zimbabwe only), replacing the full `COUNTRY_CODES` world list `site-form.tsx`'s Country dropdown previously offered; confirmed the existing `PROVINCES_BY_COUNTRY` lists were already the complete, current set of each country's top-level administrative divisions (Namibia's 14 regions, DRC's 26 provinces, Zambia's 10 provinces, Zimbabwe's 10 provinces) -- no free-text province fallback is reachable anymore, so it was removed from the form. `CreateSiteInput`/`UpdateSiteInput` gained matching server-side enforcement: `country` is a real `z.enum` now (not `z.string().length(2)`), and a `.refine()` rejects a province that isn't actually one of the selected country's divisions (e.g. "Gauteng" -- a real South African province -- can no longer be saved against `country: 'NA'`) -- same "client prevents for UX, server is still the source of truth" precedent as the Budget/Luxury refine. `UpdateSiteInput` couldn't reuse `CreateSiteInput.partial()` once refined (a refinement can't be checked against a partial object missing one of the two fields it compares), so it's a separately-defined schema with the equivalent optional-fields shape; known accepted gap: a PATCH that changes only `province` without also resending `country` skips the cross-field check entirely (the schema has no access to the record's existing country) -- not reachable via the real UI (the form always submits both together), only via a direct API caller. (2) *A real regression, caught by CI on the push after DR-086*: `e2e/departures.spec.ts`'s assignment-removal test started failing -- DR-086's confirm-before-delete change added a native `confirm()` dialog to the "Remove" button, and Playwright auto-dismisses `window.confirm()` by default unless a handler is registered to accept it, so the click silently did nothing and the form never submitted. Fixed by registering `page.once('dialog', d => d.accept())` immediately before the click; grepped the rest of the e2e suite for every other button label DR-086 touched (`Remove`/`Delete`/`Deactivate`/`Reactivate`) and confirmed this was the only affected test. `guest-checkout.spec.ts` remains red in the same CI run -- unrelated (a `page.waitForURL` timeout on the traveler-continue step), already flagged to the user earlier this session as a pre-existing, out-of-scope gap. New `tests/itinerary.domain.test.ts` cases for the country/province cross-field validation. No schema change. | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-088 | 2026-08-08 | **Geo-data foundation for the planned Map tab + route optimization** (both discussed with the user first; this DR is the shared schema/backfill groundwork, not either feature itself). `Hotel`/`Restaurant`/`Site` gain nullable `latitude`/`longitude` (`Float?`) -- none had coordinates before. `Site` gains `deletedAt` (soft-delete, matching `Hotel`/`Restaurant`) since a hard delete would now either FK-violate or corrupt a historical day once `ItineraryDaySite` can reference it; `listSites`/`listSitesForCountry`/`findSiteById`/`findSitesByIds` filter it, `deleteSite` became an update. `ItineraryDay` gains `pickupLatitude`/`pickupLongitude`/`dropoffLatitude`/`dropoffLongitude` (independent of the existing free-text `pickupLocation`/`dropoffLocation`, which stay as the human label alongside the pin) and **loses `plannedSites`** (`String? @db.Text`), replaced outright by a new join model `ItineraryDaySite` (`itineraryDayId`, `siteId`, staff-settable `sequence Int`, `@@unique([itineraryDayId, sequence])` + `@@unique([itineraryDayId, siteId])`, own `organizationId` + `tenant_isolation` RLS policy in `prisma/rls.sql`, identical shape to every other child table in that file -- no parent-join variant exists anywhere to imitate instead). Unlike `ItineraryDay.dayNumber` (server-computed, explicitly not client-settable per its own comment), `sequence` here genuinely is staff-settable -- a materially different UX, not a copy of that precedent. New repository methods `listDaySites`/`addDaySite`/`removeDaySite`/`moveDaySite`/`findSitesByIds`, and service wrappers of the same names (`itinerary.write`-gated, each audited: `itinerary_day_site.added`/`.removed`/`.reordered`) plus a read-side `listDaySites`/`listSitesByIds` (`itinerary.read`). `moveDaySite`'s sequence swap can't be a plain two-update swap: `withOrg` already runs its whole callback inside one transaction, `@@unique([itineraryDayId, sequence])` is not `DEFERRABLE`, and Postgres checks a unique constraint immediately after each statement, not at commit -- a direct swap would transiently collide. Routed through an unused negative sentinel value instead (three sequential updates: current -> -1, neighbor -> current's old value, current -> neighbor's old value), safe since `addDaySite` only ever assigns positive sequences. `catalog.PackageItineraryDay.plannedSites` is deliberately **left untouched** (still free text) -- its schema comment already explained it mirrors `ItineraryDay`'s old shape rather than sharing a type specifically so `catalog` never has to depend on `itinerary`; referencing the new `Site` table from a package template would invert that dependency direction, so `itineraryService.createItinerary`'s template-copy step simply stopped copying this one field (a fresh day now arrives with no sites pre-picked, same as `hotelId`/`restaurantId` already not being copied). Staff forms: `MapLocationPicker.tsx` gained an `optional` prop (drops `required` on its two number inputs) and a shared `id="google-maps-js"` on its `<Script>` tag -- the itinerary day edit form is the first place in the app rendering two picker instances at once (pickup + dropoff), and without a shared id Next.js would risk injecting Google's Maps JS API script twice. The old free-text "planned sites" `<input list="site-options">` (both add-day and edit-day forms) is replaced by a per-day ordered list (name + Remove/▲/▼, each a one-line bound Server Action -- no drag-and-drop, no new client dependency, matching this app's existing one-Server-Action-per-button idiom) plus a `<select>`-and-"Add site" mini-form; sites can only be added once a day exists (`ItineraryDaySite` needs a real `itineraryDayId`), so the "Add a day" form's own planned-sites field was removed outright, with a note pointing staff to "Edit day" afterward. `Hotel`/`Restaurant`/`Site` CRUD forms gained a plain lat/long input pair + `MapLocationPicker` (`optional`). New one-off `scripts/backfill-coordinates.ts` (`npm run coords:backfill`) -- the first real consumer of `GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_API_KEY` (OI-13, resolved 2026-08-08): geocodes every `Hotel`/`Restaurant`/`Site` row still missing coordinates via the Geocoding API (now enabled on that key), logs anything ungeocodable for a staff spot-check; not wired into `db:setup` or a QStash schedule, run by hand once. New `tests/rls.cross-tenant.itinerary-day-sites.test.ts` (mirrors `itinerary-days`' shape) and `tests/itinerary.domain.test.ts` cases for the removed `plannedSites` field and the new coordinate validation; `tests/package-itinerary-template.test.ts`'s template-copy assertion updated to confirm `plannedSites` is *not* copied, rather than asserting the old copy behavior. **Schema change not yet applied to the shared Neon database** -- pending explicit user confirmation + a pasted `neondb_owner` credential per the documented process. Deliberately deferred to a later DR: the Map tab itself (rendering/PDF export) and route optimization (Routes API `optimizeWaypointOrder`) -- this DR only builds the data both will need. | V1, V4, V6, V7 |
| DR-089 | 2026-08-08 | **The Map tab itself, built on DR-088's geo-data foundation.** Staff enter a booking's reference code, see the itinerary's day-by-day stops on a map, download each day as a PDF -- SUPERADMIN/TOUR_OPERATOR unrestricted, TOUR_GUIDE/DRIVER limited to bookings on a departure they're actually assigned to. Deliberately does **not** call Google's Directions/Routes API at all, in this DR or the next one that adds route optimization -- the feature only asks to see stops on a map, not a road-snapped driving path; a plain straight-line polyline between geocoded stops (drawn client-side via `google.maps.Polyline`, or via Static Maps' own `path=` parameter for the PDF) is correct and needs zero extra API calls. New `bookingService.getByBookingReference` (`booking/service.ts`) -- confirmed via `rbac.ts` that TOUR_GUIDE/DRIVER already hold `booking.read` *and* `itinerary.read`, so this is a same-shape sibling to the existing `getById`/`getOwnedBooking` (refactored into a shared `assertOwnedBooking` helper both now call), not a new authz surface. New `itineraryService.resolveMapOverview(ctx, bookingReference)` reuses the module's own existing `getItineraryForBooking`-style manager-or-assigned check (`isAssignedToItinerary`) rather than any new scoping logic. New `src/modules/itinerary/gateway.ts` (`StaticMapsGateway`) -- Static Maps API only, mirrors `notifications/gateway.ts`'s env-gated (`GOOGLE_MAPS_SERVER_API_KEY`) + `AbortSignal.timeout(5000)` shape, simplified (no retry/circuit-breaker, matching `documents/gateway.ts`'s plainer variant) since a failed render has no fallback channel to retry into. New dependency `@react-pdf/renderer@4.5.1` (exact-pinned, React 19-compatible) -- this repo's first PDF-generation capability; `src/modules/itinerary/map-pdf.tsx` lays out the Static Maps PNG + the day's stop list (flagging any stop with no coordinates as "not geocoded" rather than silently dropping it). New `itineraryService.streamDayMapPdf(ctx, dayId)` -- resolves + re-checks ownership via a new `getOwnedDay` helper (day has no owner of its own, only its parent itinerary does), 409s if a day has zero geocoded stops (rather than returning an empty map), and audits `itinerary_day_map.downloaded` immediately after the gateway call succeeds -- the same "read of sensitive data gets audited" precedent `documents/service.ts`'s `document.accessed` already set, now extended to itinerary location data. New routes: `GET /itineraries/map-overview?bookingReference=` (a static sibling of the `{itineraryId}` dynamic segment -- same coexistence `/itineraries/mine` already proved safe) and `GET /itineraries/{itineraryId}/days/{dayId}/map-pdf` (nested under `{itineraryId}` for shape-consistency with the existing day routes, though the handler only actually uses `dayId` -- `streamDayMapPdf` derives and re-checks ownership from the day itself, so a mismatched `itineraryId` in the URL can't be used to bypass anything). Both routes are thin pass-throughs, same convention as the passport streaming route. New staff page `/staff/map` (plain `method="get"` form, no Server Action needed -- the lookup result lives entirely in the query string) and a new read-only `src/components/ui/ItineraryDayMap.tsx` (browser-side, existing `NEXT_PUBLIC_GOOGLE_MAPS_API_KEY`, plain `Map`+`Marker`+`Polyline`, no `DirectionsService`). Extracted a shared `src/components/ui/google-maps-types.ts` from `MapLocationPicker.tsx`'s formerly-local hand-written type shim -- two files independently augmenting the same global `Window.google` with structurally different `GoogleMapsNamespace` shapes would conflict, TypeScript requires every declaration of a merged interface member to be identical. New nav entry "Map", deliberately gated `itinerary.read` (not `itinerary.write` like "Itineraries") -- TOUR_GUIDE/DRIVER's first nav-level entry point into itinerary data; before this they could only reach a day's detail by direct URL. No new RBAC/permission/seed change. `docs/openapi.yaml` updated (`MapOverview`/`MapDay`/`MapStop` schemas, both new paths). **DR-088's schema is now live** (pushed to the shared Neon DB the same session, `neondb_owner`-confirmed with the user; the one real non-null `plannedSites` row, "Library" on a live Lam-org itinerary day, was migrated into a real `Site`+`ItineraryDaySite` rather than dropped) -- both DRs are fully deployed, not just code-complete. One real incident from this rollout: the code was pushed to git well before Vercel's matching deploy actually went out, so the still-live *old* build crashed on every itinerary-day query once `plannedSites` was dropped (old code still selected that column) -- fixed by an immediate `vercel deploy --prod`; see the new CLAUDE.md gotcha on sequencing a destructive schema change with its deploy. Coordinate backfill (`scripts/backfill-coordinates.ts`) run for real: 22 rows geocoded, 1 restaurant left for manual pinning (ungeocodable via the Geocoding API as entered). | V1, V4, V6, V7 |
| DR-090 | 2026-08-08 | **Rating Code validity re-anchored to the tour's own last day, not to whenever staff happen to issue it (explicit user direction).** Previously two independently-anchored windows governed usability: `canSubmitRating`'s eligibility gate opened 48h after `tourEndDate`, while `RatingCode.expiresAt` closed 30 days after *issuance* -- unrelated to the tour at all. That issuance-anchored expiry had a real gap: a booking can be fully paid (issuance's only precondition, deliberately decoupled from `Booking.status` since a deposit-only payment can already reach `CONFIRMED`) well before or after the tour actually happens, so the old 30-day window had no guaranteed relationship to the tour's last day. New rule, both ends re-anchored to `tourEndDate` (`Departure.endDate` / `Booking.customTravelEnd`, the same fallback the booking lifecycle sweep already uses): usable starting the day after the tour ends (`RATING_ELIGIBILITY_DELAY_HOURS`: 48 -> 24, i.e. exactly one day), expires exactly 5 days after that (`RATING_CODE_VALIDITY_DAYS_AFTER_TOUR_END`, replacing the flat, issuance-anchored `RATING_CODE_VALIDITY_DAYS = 30`). New `domain.ts` function `ratingCodeExpiryFromTourEnd(tourEndDate)` replaces `ratingCodeExpiryFrom(now)`. `ratingsService.issueRatingCode` now resolves `tourEndDate` before computing `expiresAt` (previously never looked at tour dates at all) via a new shared helper `resolveTourEndDate`, factored out of `resolveEligibleBooking`'s existing derivation so both call sites stay in sync. Travel dates are expected to already be known by the time a booking is fully paid enough to issue a code, so a null `tourEndDate` at issuance is treated as a real data problem (409 `Errors.conflict`), not silently papered over with a fallback like "now." `tests/ratings.domain.test.ts` updated: the expiry test now asserts 5 days from a `tourEndDate` input (not 30 days from "now"), and the eligibility boundary test moved from 47h/48h to 23h/24h. No schema change (`RatingCode.expiresAt` was already a plain `DateTime` column; only how it's computed changed). | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-091 | 2026-08-08 | **Search, filter, and pagination for the staff admin Users and Clients directories** (explicit user request) -- neither page had ever needed any of the three before; every filtered list page elsewhere in this app (bookings' status pills, visa-queue's origin pills) fetches the whole table and filters in-memory, with **zero pagination precedent anywhere in the codebase**, so this establishes that pattern rather than matching one. New shared, pure `src/lib/directory-filters.ts` (`emailDomain`, `listEmailDomains`, `listPhoneDialCodes`/`matchesPhoneDialCode` built on the existing `country-codes.ts` `parseE164` -- several countries share a dial code, e.g. `+1`, so this filters by dial code, not a disambiguated country, and labels the option accordingly, e.g. "+1 (US +N more)", `matchesSearch`, `paginate`) used by both pages -- filter *options* are always derived from what's actually in the data (e.g. the email-domain dropdown only ever lists domains that exist), never a fixed guess-list. New shared `src/components/ui/Pagination.tsx` (numbered links with first/last + a small window around the current page, ellipsis-truncated so a large result set never renders dozens of links). Both pages stay query-param/GET-driven, no client JS, same "server-rendered link" convention as the rest of the staff dashboard's filtering -- one combined `<form method="get">` for the search box + dropdowns (submitting resets to page 1), plain `<Link>`s for column-header sorting and page numbers (each carrying every other active param forward). **Users** gets Status (Active/Inactive/Deactivated) as a real filter for the first time -- `authRepository.listStaff`'s `where` clause dropped its hardcoded `deletedAt: null` exclusion (deactivated accounts were previously excluded at the query level, never reachable via any UI state at all) in favor of an explicit, selectable filter; default view (no `status` param) now shows everyone rather than silently hiding deactivated accounts, a deliberate change from that likely-unintentional prior exclusion, not a preserved default. Also gets sortable Name/Email/Last-login column headers. **Clients** gets search + the two email/phone filters + pagination only -- no Status/last-login, since a client has neither a meaningful login history nor a reachable "deactivated" state (a deleted client is still fully excluded at the query level in `listClients`, unchanged). `deactivateUserAction`/`reactivateUserAction`/`deleteClientAction` all gained a `redirectQuery` parameter so a mutation from a filtered/paginated view redirects back to that same view instead of silently resetting to page 1, unfiltered -- a real gap the old bare `redirect('/staff/admin/users')` would have opened up the moment any filter/pagination state existed to lose. New `tests/lib/directory-filters.test.ts`. No schema change, no new permission (`admin.all`/`booking.create` gates unchanged). | V1, V8 |
| DR-092 | 2026-08-09 | **TAILOR_MADE booking cost-breakdown quotation, closing a real sequencing dead end** (driven entirely by user-directed Q&A on the SUPERADMIN/TOUR_OPERATOR journey after a `/plan-my-trip` request lands on `/staff/bookings`, not a unilateral design). The booking detail page pushed staff toward the add-ons step before a price existed, but `setAddons` required a price to already exist (to currency-match against) -- a dead end. Explicit user direction: add-ons must be selectable before pricing (their prices are already known in the system); the quotation total should be *computed* from a line-item cost breakdown (staff/hotel/restaurant/transport/activity/visa) plus those add-ons, not hand-typed; currency should be *inferred*, not staff-picked; and the base price should be a proper broken-down line-item cost breakdown, reusing the existing `finance` module cost-plus pricing engine (DR-039) rather than a new one. New `BookingCostBreakdown`/`BookingCostLineItem` tables, owned by `finance` (mirrors exactly where `PackageCostBreakdown` already lives) -- no `referenceGroupSize` (`Booking.seats` fills that role for the cost math, but there's no final per-seat division: `Booking.priceMinor` is a whole-booking total, unlike per-seat `TourPackage.priceMinor`); `currency` is derived, never caller-supplied. `financeService`'s six-rate resolution block (previously inline in `saveCostBreakdown`) is factored into a shared, throw-free `resolveRatesForCost` helper used by both the package and booking flows -- each caller keeps its own not-found/no-effective-rate `throw`s so wording stays accurate to which flow it's for. New `saveBookingCostBreakdown`/`getBookingCostBreakdown` (gated `booking.confirm`, no new permission -- matches `sendQuotation`'s own gate): resolves rates and the booking's already-selected add-ons, derives `currency` from whichever resolved rate/add-on actually has one (409 if they disagree, 409 if none resolve at all even with an override supplied -- a bare hand-typed number with no cost context belongs in the plain `sendQuotation` form instead), computes base cost -> selling price via the unchanged pure functions, and folds the add-ons total into `suggestedTotalMinor`. Deliberately does **not** write `Booking.priceMinor`/`currency` or send the quotation -- stays a "compute and persist a suggestion" step, decoupled from `sendQuotation` (unchanged), which remains the actual commit. `bookingService.setAddons`'s currency check is branched, not blanket-removed: unchanged (still rejects a mismatch) once `booking.currency` is set, relaxed to "internally consistent among themselves" only pre-quotation -- preserves the existing currency-mismatch protection/test for an already-priced booking while closing the actual pre-quotation dead end. The staff add-ons page's `if (!booking.currency)` "waiting on a quotation" block is removed, and its list-filter bug (`allAddons.filter(a => a.currency === booking.currency)` silently returning empty whenever `booking.currency` was `null`, since comparing against `null` is always false) is fixed alongside it, not left as a second, quieter dead end. New staff page `bookings/[bookingId]/cost-breakdown` (structural mirror of the package one, no reference-group-size/currency inputs, booking's own seats shown read-only) and API route `api/v1/bookings/[bookingId]/cost-breakdown`. The booking detail page's "Send quotation" amount field gains a `defaultValue` from the saved breakdown's `suggestedTotalMinor`, shown alongside a plain-text readout, plus a "Cost breakdown" link -- both gated on `can(ctx, 'booking.confirm')`, not just the page's own `booking.read` baseline, since TOUR_GUIDE/DRIVER/VISA_FACILITATOR/TOURIST can all view this page without holding `booking.confirm`. This is a new `finance` -> `booking` module dependency (finance previously only depended on `catalog`) -- confirmed acyclic (`booking`'s own imports are `{auth, catalog, notifications}`, none reaching back to `finance`); not a new pattern either, as `invoicing`/`visa`/`itinerary` already import `bookingService` the same way. New `tests/api/booking-cost-breakdown.api.test.ts`, `tests/rls.cross-tenant.booking-cost-breakdowns.test.ts` + `...booking-cost-line-items.test.ts`, and regression cases added to `tests/api/booking-setup.{api,security}.test.ts` for the branched `setAddons` fix and the new route's RBAC/anti-BOLA. Schema pushed to the shared Neon DB and RLS applied (additive-only: two new tables, no column changes to any existing table, no data-loss risk). | V1, V4, V6, V8 |
| DR-093 | 2026-08-09 | **Booking setup (add-ons + first-traveler) pages stop re-asking staff for what the guest already told `/plan-my-trip`.** Add-ons: `Booking.preferredAddons` (guest-declared interest, DR-048) is cross-referenced against the active add-on catalog on `/staff/bookings/[bookingId]/addons` -- a matching service is now pre-checked and labelled "Guest requested," instead of staff re-deriving the same signal from the raw plan-my-trip request by memory. Only applies before the first `finalizeAddonsAction` (checked via `booking.addonsFinalizedAt`); once real add-ons are on file, the page defers entirely to that actual selection, never overriding a staff edit with the original guest request on a revisit. Traveler entry: `/staff/bookings/[bookingId]/travelers/new`, when adding the tour lead on a `TAILOR_MADE` booking, now shows `Booking.contactFirstName`/`contactLastName`/`contactEmail`/`countryOfResidence` (already collected at DR-047/DR-057) and the tourist's `User.phone` (fetched via `authService.getUser(booking.touristUserId)`, parsed back to dial-code + local-number with the existing `parseE164`) as a read-only summary wired to hidden form inputs, rather than blank fields -- each falls back to its normal editable input independently if absent (a legacy `TAILOR_MADE` booking predating those columns, or no `User.phone` on file). Nationality stays editable even though `Booking.citizenship` is known, only pre-selected as a `defaultValue` -- citizenship (self-declared at plan-my-trip step 7) isn't guaranteed to equal passport nationality, so this one field intentionally isn't locked into a summary the way the others are. No schema change, no new permission, no new module dependency -- `authService.getUser` is called from the page (an orchestration layer above both modules), not from inside `bookingService`, so `booking`'s own import set (`{auth, catalog, notifications}`) is unaffected. Both pages already require `booking.create` and resolve the booking through the existing `bookingService.getById` org/ownership check before this data is read, so no new BOLA surface. | V1, V6 |
| DR-094 | 2026-08-10 | **Fixes a real gap in DR-082's fleet availability: the scheduled booking-lifecycle sweep never resynced it, so a vehicle/driver/guide could sit at stale availability indefinitely** (found investigating a live staff report -- a Toyota Hilux/driver/guide assigned to a genuinely `IN_PROGRESS` booking still showed `AVAILABLE`). Root cause: `src/lib/fleet-availability.ts`'s `syncFleetAvailabilityForDeparture` hook was only ever wired into manual actions -- assignment create/remove, and the booking confirm/cancel/refund routes. But a booking's `CONFIRMED -> IN_PROGRESS -> COMPLETED` lifecycle is driven entirely by `bookingRepository.sweepLifecycle`'s raw-SQL `UPDATE` statements (`/api/jobs/sweep-bookings`, the QStash job that runs every 15 minutes) -- a bulk statement with no way to call an application-layer hook, so this is the *only* path a real booking ever travels that transition through, and it was structurally invisible to fleet availability the entire time. `sweepLifecycle` now captures the affected `departureId`s from its `CONFIRMED -> IN_PROGRESS` and `IN_PROGRESS -> COMPLETED` statements via `$queryRaw`+`RETURNING` instead of `$executeRaw` (the third, expired-hold-cancellation statement is untouched -- `AWAITING_DEPOSIT` was never "active" per `hasActiveBookingForDeparture`, so it can't change fleet availability's answer either way). `bookingRepository.sweepAllOrganizations`/`bookingService.runScheduledSweep` now return a `transitionedDepartures: { organizationId, departureId }[]` alongside the existing `organizationsSwept` count; `/api/jobs/sweep-bookings/route.ts` (one level up from both modules, same convention as `fleet-availability.ts` itself) loops over it calling `syncFleetAvailabilityForDeparture` per departure, sequentially -- same connection-pool-exhaustion precedent as the sweep's own per-org loop. Deliberately scoped to the QStash cron path only, not `seatsTakenFor`'s lazy on-read sweep (`bookingService.getAvailability`, a hot guest-facing path) -- that lazy path finding a transition to make is the rare edge case now that the registered 15-minute schedule sweeps every organization already; a miss there still self-corrects at the next confirm/cancel/refund, reassignment, or scheduled sweep run, same "best-effort, self-correcting" posture `fleet-availability.ts` already documents. New test in `tests/booking-scheduled-sweep.test.ts` (a `CONFIRMED` booking on a departure with a past `startDate` advances to `IN_PROGRESS` and its departure is reported back) plus two new cases in `tests/api/jobs-sweep-bookings.api.test.ts` (resyncs once per transitioned departure; stays a no-op when nothing transitioned). No schema change, no new permission, no new module dependency. | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-095 | 2026-08-10 | **Fleet dashboard split into a card hub + one dedicated list page per type, each gaining search/filter/pagination** (explicit user request). `/staff/fleet` was one page rendering all four Vehicle/Driver/Guide/Starlink-Kit tables end to end, unpaginated -- fine at small scale but with no ceiling as the fleet grows. `/staff/fleet` is now a card hub (`Card` + `Link`, same clickable-card convention as the guest `PackageCard`) showing each type's count and linking to a new dedicated list page: `/staff/fleet/vehicles`, `/staff/fleet/drivers`, `/staff/fleet/guides`, `/staff/fleet/starlink-kits`. Each list page follows DR-091's established convention exactly -- query-param/GET-driven (no client JS), `PER_PAGE = 10`, reusing `directory-filters.ts`'s generic `paginate` and the shared `Pagination` component; the "Add X" button and delete-action table row moved from the old combined page onto each type's own list page, unchanged otherwise. Search/filters per type, chosen from each type's own displayed columns (never a field not already shown): **Vehicles** -- search plate/make/model/VIN; filter Status, Availability, and Type (derived from the data, `vehicleType` being intentionally open-ended per its own schema comment, same "derive options from what's actually there" convention as DR-091's email-domain filter). **Drivers** -- search name/email/license #; filter Status, Availability. **Guides** -- search name/email/language/specialty; filter Status, Availability, and Specialty (also derived from the data). **Starlink Kits** -- search Kit ID; filter Status and Assignment (assigned/unassigned, derived from `vehicleId` being set). `directory-filters.ts` itself is untouched (still Users/Clients-specific, `DirectoryPerson`-shaped) -- each fleet page's own `matchesQuery` is a small local pure function over that page's own fields, same "hand-copied per-file rather than a premature shared abstraction" convention already established elsewhere (e.g. `titleCase`/`addonLabel`). All four delete actions (`deleteVehicleAction`/`deleteDriverProfileAction`/`deleteGuideProfileAction`/`deleteStarlinkKitAction`) now redirect to their own type's list page instead of the old combined `/staff/fleet` route; every detail page and "new" form gained a `BackLink` to its type's list page (previously no back-navigation existed at all, since the combined page made every section reachable from one screen). `e2e/fleet.spec.ts` updated: the "renders vehicle and driver sections" test now also verifies the hub's four cards link through to their list pages, and the two post-creation assertions that used to check `/staff/fleet` for the new row now check the specific list page instead. No schema change, no new permission, no new module dependency -- purely a UI/query-param reshaping of data the existing `fleetService.list*` methods already returned. | V1, V8 |
| DR-096 | 2026-08-10 | **Vehicle Make/Model/Type become curated dropdown pickers with an "Other" free-text escape hatch, instead of three bare text inputs** (explicit user request: research real-world makes/types/models, but still allow adding one that isn't listed). `Vehicle.make`/`model`/`vehicleType` stay free-text in the schema, unchanged -- deliberately, per that column's own existing comment (varies too much across NA/DRC/Zambia/Zimbabwe for an enum) -- this is a UI suggestion layer only, never a constraint: every picker always offers "Other (not listed — type your own)", so a real vehicle is never blocked from being entered just because it isn't in the curated list. New `src/lib/vehicle-catalog.ts` (`VEHICLE_TYPES`, `VEHICLE_MAKES`, `VEHICLE_MODELS_BY_MAKE`), scoped to what a Southern/Central-African tourism operator's fleet plausibly contains -- 4x4s/pickups, guest-transfer sedans/vans, buses/coaches, support trucks, motorcycles/ATVs, and boats for river/lake activities -- not an exhaustive global catalog (ultra-exotic brands like Ferrari/Lamborghini omitted as unrealistic fleet vehicles, a deliberate scoping choice, not an oversight); model lists are curated deepest for the makes most likely to actually appear in this fleet (Toyota, Land Rover, Nissan, Ford, Isuzu, Mitsubishi, Mahindra, Mercedes-Benz, VW, plus bus/truck chassis brands Scania/MAN/Iveco/Hino and marine brands for boat activities), thinner elsewhere. New generic client component `SelectOrOther` (`src/components/ui/SelectOrOther.tsx`) -- a curated `<Select>` plus a trailing "Other" option that reveals a free-text input, with one hidden input carrying whichever is actually active under the field's real `name`, so no server action needed any change (`FormData.get('make')` etc. work exactly as before). A `defaultValue` not present in the curated list (an existing vehicle entered before this list existed, or with a genuinely one-off make/model) is never dropped -- the field just opens already in "Other" mode, pre-filled with that value. New `VehicleMakeModelFields` (`fleet/vehicles/vehicle-make-model-fields.tsx`) couples Make and Model in one client component, since Model's own suggestion list depends on the currently-selected Make (e.g. choosing Toyota offers "Hilux"/"Land Cruiser 300 Series"/...; a Make with no curated models, or a custom "Other" Make, just leaves Model as an Other-only free-text field) -- remounts Model (via `key={make}`) on every Make change so a stale "Other" model text typed for a previous Make is never carried over as a misleading default for a new one. Wired into both `/staff/fleet/vehicles/new` and the vehicle detail/edit form, replacing their plain `make`/`model`/`vehicleType` inputs one-for-one. `e2e/fleet.spec.ts`'s vehicle-registration test updated to exercise both modes -- Make and Type picked from the curated list, Model via the Other escape hatch (Toyota's own curated list only has suffixed Land Cruiser variants, e.g. "Land Cruiser 300 Series", not the bare name), which also proves Model's options genuinely follow the chosen Make. No new unit-test framework added for the client component itself -- this codebase has no React-component-level unit tests anywhere (Vitest here covers domain/service logic only; UI correctness is Playwright's job), so the e2e update is the appropriate, convention-matching coverage. No schema change, no new permission, no new module dependency. | V1, V8 |
| DR-097 | 2026-08-10 | **Packages dashboard split into a card hub + Public/Customized list pages** (explicit user request). `/staff/packages` was one flat, unpaginated table of every `TourPackage` regardless of status -- now a card hub (same `Card`+`Link` shape as DR-095's fleet hub) showing two counts: **Public Packages** (`status = PUBLISHED` -- the only status `catalogService`'s `isPackageVisible` ever shows a guest) and **Customized Packages** (`DRAFT`/`ARCHIVED` -- staff-only, never reflected on the guest site, per the user's own framing of the split). No schema change: `TourPackage.status` already had exactly these three values: this is a UI reshaping of an existing field's meaning, not a new concept. Two new list pages, `/staff/packages/public` and `/staff/packages/customized`, follow DR-091/095's established convention -- query-param/GET-driven, `PER_PAGE = 10`, reusing `directory-filters.ts`'s generic `paginate` and the shared `Pagination` component, with a page-local `matchesQuery` over title/reference/country. Public gets a Country filter (derived from the data, same convention as DR-095's vehicle Type filter); Customized gets a Status filter (Draft/Archived, the two sub-statuses within "not public") and is where the "New package" button now lives, since `createPackageAction` always creates a fresh `DRAFT` -- a brand-new package is Customized by definition until explicitly published. The package detail page's back link is now dynamic, not a fixed hub link -- it reflects the package's live status (Public list if currently `PUBLISHED`, Customized otherwise), so publishing or archiving a package while viewing it doesn't leave a stale "back" target. No new permission, no new module dependency, no e2e coverage existed for `/staff/packages` before this change (grepped `e2e/`/`tests/` -- zero hits), so none needed updating. | V1, V8 |
| DR-098 | 2026-08-10 | **Bookings dashboard gets the same card-hub-plus-list-pages shape, and "New booking" gets an explicit two-card chooser** (explicit user request). `/staff/bookings` was one page: a pill row (All + one pill per status, hiding CANCELLED/REFUNDED from the default view) above a single unpaginated table. It's now a card hub -- "All" plus one card per filterable `BookingStatus` (`FILTERABLE_STATUSES`, exported from `bookings/page.tsx` for the list pages to reuse; DRAFT stays omitted, same as the pill row it replaces, since no creation path ever actually sets it) -- each card always rendered regardless of count (unlike the old pills, which hid a zero-count status entirely), since CANCELLED/REFUNDED are no longer second-class now that they have their own dedicated cards. Two new list pages: `/staff/bookings/all` (every status, with its own Status + Source filters for narrowing without leaving) and the dynamic `/staff/bookings/status/[status]` (pre-scoped to one status via the route itself, 404s on anything not in `FILTERABLE_STATUSES`) -- both follow the DR-091/095/097 convention (query-param/GET, `PER_PAGE = 10`, `paginate`/`Pagination`, page-local `matchesQuery` over reference + source label), adding a Source filter (Package vs. Plan my trip) alongside Status. `/staff/bookings/new` ("New booking," SUPERADMIN/TOUR_OPERATOR-only) gets an explicit top-level two-card chooser -- **Make a booking from existing Packages** and **Create a tailor-made request** -- replacing the old default view, which silently defaulted to the package list with the tailor-made path relegated to a small inline text link easy to miss. A new `?mode=packages` query state holds that package list (previously the bare default); the specific-package booking form's back link now returns to `?mode=packages` (the list), not the new top-level chooser, preserving where the user actually came from. No schema change, no new permission, no new module dependency -- `deleteBookingAction` keeps redirecting to the hub (`/staff/bookings`) after a delete, a safe default since the deleted row can no longer belong to any specific list. `e2e/staff-dashboard.spec.ts`'s existing assertions (`getByRole('heading', {name: 'Bookings'})` on `/staff/bookings`) still pass unchanged, since the hub's own `<h1>` renders that same title; no e2e coverage existed for `/staff/bookings/new` to update. | V1, V8 |
| DR-099 | 2026-08-10 | **Search/filter/pagination added to the staff Hotels and Restaurants lists** (explicit user request) -- both were flat, unpaginated tables (Itinerary Management, DR-033/083, name + contact info only, no compliance tracking like the fleet module). No card-hub split this time (unlike DR-095/097/098) -- neither entity has a natural sub-category the way fleet types or booking statuses do, so this is a direct in-place addition to the existing single list page for each, same DR-091/095/097/098 convention otherwise (query-param/GET, `PER_PAGE = 10`, `paginate`/`Pagination`, page-local `matchesQuery` over name/address/contact name/email/phone, a Country filter derived from the data). Both pages keep their existing access-scoping unchanged -- a non-`itinerary.write` role (TOUR_GUIDE/DRIVER, `hotel_restaurant_rating.write` only) still sees only hotels/restaurants they've actually toured (anti-BOLA, same scope `rateHotel`/`rateRestaurant` themselves enforce) -- search/filter/pagination apply *after* that scoping, never widening it. No schema change, no new permission, no new module dependency, no existing e2e/unit coverage to update (grepped -- zero hits for either page). | V1, V8 |
| DR-100 | 2026-08-10 | **Search/filter/pagination added to the staff Itineraries list** (explicit user request, same treatment as DR-099) -- was a flat, unpaginated table joining every `Itinerary` to its `Booking` (manager-only, `itinerary.write`). No card-hub split -- `ItineraryStatus` (`DRAFT`/`IN_REVIEW`/`APPROVED`) is a small enough set that a filter dropdown reads better than three near-empty cards, same reasoning DR-099 gave for skipping a hub there. Search matches the joined booking's reference; two Status filters (Itinerary status and the joined Booking's own status, both real displayed columns) rather than one, since this list's two status dimensions are independent. A row whose booking has been soft-deleted past the 90-day retention purge (DR-058) shows `—` for booking status/reference exactly as before and simply never matches a booking-status filter, not a new failure mode. **Real bug fix, caught by CI, not by local typecheck/lint**: the original `FILTERABLE_STATUSES` constant introduced in DR-098 was exported directly from `bookings/page.tsx` -- Next.js's App Router rejects any named export from a `page.tsx` file other than its own well-known ones (`default`, `generateMetadata`, etc.); `next build`'s own type-checking catches this, plain `tsc --noEmit` does not, which is how it passed local `npm run typecheck`/`lint` and only failed in CI's Build step. Moved to a new `src/lib/booking-statuses.ts` (`FILTERABLE_BOOKING_STATUSES`), imported by the hub and both list pages instead of one page importing from another. Lesson applied going forward: grepped every staff-dashboard `page.tsx` touched this session for a stray non-default export (none found beyond the one already fixed) -- `npm run build` itself couldn't be run to full completion in this sandbox (no network access to fetch Google Fonts for `next/font`, an environment limitation unrelated to any code change), so CI's Build step remains the authoritative gate for this specific class of error. No schema change, no new permission, no new module dependency, no existing e2e/unit coverage to update. | V1, V8 |
| DR-101 | 2026-08-10 | **"My schedule" (self-service TOUR_GUIDE/DRIVER/VEHICLE_OWNER assignments) split into Past/In Progress/Future cards, each a dedicated list page with search/filter/pagination** (explicit user request). `/staff/schedule` used to render all three groupings (already grouped by `tracking.resolveTripProgress`'s date-based status, not `Departure.status`) as three flat, unpaginated tables inline on one page. Now a card hub (Past/In Progress/Future counts, same `Card`+`Link` shape as DR-095/097/098) linking to `/staff/schedule/past`, `/staff/schedule/in-progress`, `/staff/schedule/future` -- each with a Departure-status filter (`SCHEDULED`/`CANCELLED`/`COMPLETED`, a real, independent dimension from the date-based progress bucket -- e.g. a `CANCELLED` departure can still fall in "Future" by date) plus search over country/vehicle/driver/guide, `PER_PAGE = 10`, same DR-091/095/097/098/099/100 convention. The row-building (assignment -> departure/vehicle/driver/guide join, `Promise.allSettled`-tolerant of a departure gone `isDepartureVisible`-invisible for a completed trip, tracking-progress calc, sort) moved out of the page into `schedule/build-schedule-rows.ts` (`buildScheduleRows`, `matchesScheduleQuery`) and the table markup into `schedule/assignments-section.tsx` (`AssignmentsSection`) -- neither is a `page.tsx`/`route.ts`, so neither is subject to the DR-100 export restriction; all four routes (hub + 3 lists) import from these instead of one page importing from another or four copies of the same fetch logic. The hub's "Daily itinerary & clients" and "Itineraries" sections (TOUR_GUIDE/DRIVER-only, itinerary.read-gated respectively) are untouched, still on the hub page -- neither is naturally split by time-status the way the assignment list itself is. No schema change, no new permission, no new module dependency, no existing e2e/unit coverage to update (grepped -- zero hits). | V1, V8 |
| DR-102 | 2026-08-10 | **Staff Ratings page's "Agency overall" now renders 5 grey stars (not just bare text) when there are zero reviews, plus a jump-link to the reviews list** (explicit user request). Reuses the existing `RatingStars` component unchanged -- passing `rating={0}` clips its gold overlay to 0% width, leaving only the muted underlying row visible, i.e. genuinely 5 grey stars, not a new "empty state" variant. Deliberately different from the guest homepage's `TrustSummary` (DR-068), which hides itself entirely at zero reviews to avoid any appearance of fabricated social proof -- that concern doesn't apply here, since this is a staff-only insights view where showing an honestly-empty rating is informative, not persuasive. New `<a href="#individual-reviews">See users' ratings and comments</a>` under the average (both the zero-review and has-reviews states) scrolls down to the page's own existing "Individual reviews" section, now anchored with that id. No schema change, no new permission, no new component. | V1, V6 |
| DR-103 | 2026-08-10 | **Fixed a real, repo-wide test flakiness source: ~40 test files used `formatPackageReference(Date.now())` as a shortcut to fabricate a throwaway package reference, which collides for real under CI's parallel test-file execution** (found investigating a genuine, unrelated-to-DR-102 CI failure -- `tests/booking-guest-dates.test.ts`'s `tx.tourPackage.create()` hit a "Unique constraint failed" on `packageReference @unique`). `formatPackageReference()` itself is meant to be called with a real DB sequence value (`nextval()`, see `catalogRepository.createPackage`'s own `nextPackageReference` helper) -- it just stringifies+pads whatever's passed, so a raw `Date.now()` "works" until two workers read the identical millisecond, which is exactly the kind of collision parallel execution makes likely, not a one-off fluke. New `tests/helpers/package-reference.ts` (`testPackageReference()`) adds a random component (`Math.floor(Math.random() * 1e15)`) instead, removing the collision risk regardless of timing. Mechanically swapped across all ~40 affected files (verified beforehand that every `formatPackageReference(` call in each was this exact risky pattern, zero exceptions, before touching anything) -- including `tests/booking-scheduled-sweep.test.ts`, which I'd added this same risky pattern to earlier this session (DR-094) without yet causing a visible collision. One self-inflicted near-miss during the fix itself, caught before committing: the bulk sed also matched the new helper file's own docstring (which quoted the exact string being replaced, `formatPackageReference(Date.now())`, as a code example) and rewrote its self-import into a broken circular one -- caught by re-reading the file, not by typecheck (which cannot detect "imports itself" as different from "imports a real module" until the resulting cycle actually breaks something observable). Verified with `npm run typecheck` (clean across every touched file) and manual diff spot-checks of each of the four import-statement shapes touched (sole import, both orderings of a combined `catalogService`/`formatPackageReference` import, and one relative-path import). No schema change, no production code touched, test-infrastructure only. | V1 |
| DR-104 | 2026-08-11 | **Coupon percentage-discount codes** (explicit user request): SUPERADMIN sets a discount % (max 50%, hard-capped) plus an optional redemption cap/expiry in a new Settings page; the code itself is always system-generated in an exact user-specified format, `CPC-{YY}-{6 digits, never containing '3'}-{2 letters, never "AK"}` (e.g. `CPC-26-019654-HK`) -- `generateCouponCode` (`src/modules/settings/domain.ts`) uses crypto-random rejection sampling (same rigor as `generateBookingReference`, not `generateRatingCode`'s smaller accepted bias) so the exclusion constraints are exact, not approximate. New platform-wide (no `organizationId`/RLS, same precedent as `TaxRate`/`PlatformRate`) `Coupon` (third entity added to the existing `settings` module -- `discountBp`, nullable `maxRedemptions`/`expiresAt`/`deactivatedAt`; deactivation is a soft field, not a hard delete like the other two rate tables, since `CouponRedemption` has a real FK to it) and `CouponRedemption` (one row per `(couponId, invoiceId)`, created when a coupon is *applied* -- redemptions are counted at apply-time, not payment-time, a deliberate simplification: removing a coupon deletes its redemption row and releases the slot, so the only cost is a slot "wasted" by an applied-then-abandoned invoice). New `Invoice.couponCode`/`discountBp`/`discountMinor` columns (snapshotted at apply time, same convention as `taxRateBp`); `invoicing/domain.ts` gains a shared `computeInvoiceAmounts` helper (subtotal -> discount -> discounted subtotal -> tax **on the discounted subtotal** -> total -> deposit/balance split) that both `getOrCreateInvoiceForBooking` (discount omitted, zero behavior change, covered by a regression test) and the new `invoicingService.applyCoupon`/`removeCoupon` call -- the one place this math is written, never duplicated. New `src/lib/coupons.ts` (mirrors `tax.ts`/`platform-rate.ts`'s existing role: lets `invoicing` validate a code without importing `@modules/settings` directly, preserving the module-dependency direction). Cap enforcement under concurrency uses a `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` row lock on the `Coupon` row inside `withOrg`'s transaction (a bare `$transaction` wrapper alone doesn't stop two concurrent applies of a single-use code both reading "count < cap" as true before either inserts) -- covered by a dedicated `Promise.all`-racing test in `tests/api/invoices.api.test.ts`. `applyCoupon`/`removeCoupon` are gated by **`payment.initiate`, not `invoice.read`** -- verified against the actual closest precedent (`initiatePayment` itself uses this permission, not `invoice.read`; the latter only appears to "gate a mutation" as an idiosyncrasy of `getOrCreateInvoiceForBooking`'s own lazy get-or-create shape) -- so no new permission was needed anywhere in this feature; Coupon CRUD reuses `platform_settings.read`/`.write` and the existing `requireSettingsWriter` SUPERADMIN-only gate verbatim. Both apply and remove are blocked once any payment on the invoice has `SUCCEEDED` (matches the existing "frozen once priced/paid" precedent, e.g. DR-092's currency-consistency check). New `CouponForm` client component (`src/components/CouponForm.tsx`), shared verbatim between the guest booking page and the staff booking-detail page (only the bound Server Action differs) -- deliberately a plain `<form action>` + `useActionState`, not `AddonsForm`/`BookingForm`'s `onSubmit`+`router.push` pattern, since this form never navigates away, it only needs `revalidatePath` to refresh the current page's numbers. A "Discount" line was also added to the read-only `find-booking/result` guest lookup page (via a new `BillingSummaryView.discountMinor` field) beyond the two pages originally in scope, since its subtotal/tax/total would otherwise visibly fail to add up once a coupon was applied to that booking. New `GET/POST /api/v1/settings/coupons`, `POST /api/v1/settings/coupons/{id}/deactivate` (named `/deactivate`, not `DELETE`, since it's a soft state change), `POST/DELETE /api/v1/invoices/{invoiceId}/coupon` -- `docs/openapi.yaml` bumped to 0.15.0 with the `Invoice` schema extended and the new invoice-coupon path documented; the two settings routes were deliberately **not** added to `openapi.yaml`, matching the pre-existing gap that `tax-rates`/`platform-rates` were never documented there either (flagged here rather than silently fixed only for this feature's own corner of it). New/extended tests: `tests/settings.domain.test.ts` (input validation + code-format/exclusion/distinctness), `tests/invoicing.domain.test.ts` (`computeInvoiceAmounts`, incl. a no-discount regression check against the pre-refactor numbers), new `tests/lib/coupons.test.ts`, and extensions to `tests/api/invoices.api.test.ts`/`settings.api.test.ts` and both `.security.test.ts` files (SUPERADMIN-only write gate, anti-BOLA on apply/remove). **Explicitly deferred, not built**: rate-limiting `applyCoupon` guesses -- unlike `find-booking`/rating-code lookup, this requires an already-established guest/staff session tied to a specific booking (a materially lower threat profile), and CLAUDE.md's security posture already states per-class rate limiting beyond those two lookups is not built. Schema (`Coupon`, `CouponRedemption`, three new `Invoice` columns) applied by hand to the shared Neon DB with explicit user confirmation, same precedent as every prior schema change this session; no RLS change needed (`Coupon`/`CouponRedemption` are platform-wide, and the `Invoice` RLS policy is unaffected by adding nullable columns to an already-covered table). | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-105 | 2026-08-13 | **Hard-blocked edits on a terminal-status booking** (explicit user request, following a review of what stays editable after a booking is done): once `Booking.status` reaches `COMPLETED`, `CANCELLED`, or `REFUNDED`, four categories of edit now 409 server-side, with **no SUPERADMIN override** -- a real correction goes through a real process, not a bypass. Before this DR, `Booking.status` gated only its own transitions (`canTransition`/`TRANSITIONS`, `booking/domain.ts`); every other mutation attached to a booking had no status check at all, confirmed by direct code review -- the closest existing precedent, DR-085's `assertClientDeletable`, gates *client deletion*, not booking field edits. New single source of truth: `TERMINAL_BOOKING_STATUSES`/`isBookingLocked(status)` in `booking/domain.ts`, exported through `booking/index.ts` (the module's public interface) so the three already-legitimate downstream dependents -- `itinerary`, `finance`, `invoicing` (confirmed acyclic per the existing module-dependency direction: `booking` itself only imports `{auth, catalog, notifications}`) -- reuse one predicate instead of each hand-rolling their own array (avoiding the exact anti-pattern the two hand-copied `CANCELLABLE_STATUSES` UI arrays already are, left as-is/out of scope). Four call sites: (1) `bookingService.addTraveler`/`setTravelerPassport`/`setAddons` (`booking/service.ts`) -- each already loads the booking first, guard inserted immediately after, before any other conflict check; (2) `itineraryService.updateItinerary`/`addDay`/`updateDay`/`removeDay`/`addDaySite`/`removeDaySite`/`moveDaySite` (`itinerary/service.ts`) -- a new local `assertBookingNotLocked(ctx, bookingId)` helper (matching this file's existing small-helper style, e.g. `requireHotelExists`) resolves the parent booking via the same `bookingService.getById` pattern the file already uses elsewhere (`isAssignedToItinerary`, `createItinerary`); itinerary's own workflow transitions (`submitForReview`/`sendBackToDraft`/`approveItinerary`) and hotel/restaurant ratings are deliberately untouched -- out of scope, and ratings are meant to happen post-completion; (3) `financeService.saveBookingCostBreakdown` (`finance/service.ts`) -- booking already loaded, guard inserted before the existing `TAILOR_MADE`/`customCountry` checks; (4) `invoicingService.applyCoupon`/`removeCoupon` (`invoicing/service.ts`) -- these didn't previously load the Booking at all (only `InvoiceDetail`), so a new `bookingService.getById(ctx, detail.invoice.bookingId)` call was added, same pattern already used elsewhere in this file; this specifically closes a real gap the pre-existing "no SUCCEEDED payment yet" coupon guard didn't cover -- a CANCELLED/REFUNDED booking that never had a successful payment. Assignment (vehicle/driver/guide) is explicitly **out of scope** -- it's keyed by `Departure`, shared across every booking on that departure, so a single booking's status can't cleanly gate it. UI: the addons and new-traveler staff pages (both already load `booking`) show a locked-state message instead of the form; the itinerary detail page (already loads `booking` for its own soft-delete handling) computes `canEdit = canWrite && !bookingLocked` and gates every day/site/notes write affordance on it, leaving the itinerary workflow buttons on `canWrite` alone; the shared `CouponForm`'s visibility at both call sites (guest booking page, staff booking-detail page) now also checks `!isBookingLocked(booking.status)` alongside the pre-existing payment check. New i18n `bookingLocked` keys (EN+FR) in the `StaffAddonsPage`/`StaffTravelersPage`/`StaffItineraryDetail` namespaces. New/extended tests: `tests/booking.domain.test.ts` (`isBookingLocked` unit cases), and terminal-status 409 cases added to `tests/api/booking-setup.api.test.ts`, `tests/api/itinerary.api.test.ts`, `tests/api/booking-cost-breakdown.api.test.ts`, `tests/api/invoices.api.test.ts` -- plus a new `tests/itinerary-day-site-edit-lock.test.ts` calling `itineraryService` directly, since `addDaySite`/`removeDaySite`/`moveDaySite` have no dedicated REST route (staff drives them via the itinerary detail page's Server Actions). No schema change, no new permission. | V1, V4, V6, V7 |
| DR-106 | 2026-08-14 | **Blocked vehicle/driver/guide assignment changes on a completed departure** (explicit user request). Unlike DR-105's booking-status lock, `Assignment` is keyed by `Departure`, shared across every booking on that departure -- a single booking's status can't cleanly gate it, so this needed its own "is the tour over" signal. `Departure.status` (`DepartureStatus`: SCHEDULED/CANCELLED/COMPLETED) turned out to be dead for this purpose -- confirmed by code review that nothing anywhere ever programmatically sets it to CANCELLED or COMPLETED, it sits at SCHEDULED forever in practice (the team's own comment in `insights/domain.ts` already flags this: "DepartureStatus has no IN_PROGRESS value, only Booking does"). Per explicit user direction, "is this departure done" is instead date-based: new `hasDepartureEnded(endDate, now)` in `catalog/domain.ts` (`endDate != null && endDate < now`), the same real signal `bookingService`'s `sweepLifecycle` and `tracking.resolveTripProgress` already use, exported through `catalog/index.ts` for `assignment` to reuse (already a legitimate dependency -- `assignmentService.createAssignment` already calls `catalogService.getDepartureDetail`). Two guarded call sites in `assignment/service.ts`: `createAssignment` (departure already loaded, guard inserted right after) and `removeAssignment` (didn't previously load the departure at all -- now looks up the assignment first via a new `assignmentRepository.findById`, then the departure, before deleting). **Hard block, no SUPERADMIN override** -- the assignment becomes a fixed historical record of who actually served that departure, consistent with the DR-105 precedent. UI: `/staff/departures/[departureId]` computes `departureLocked` once and hides both the remove-assignment buttons and the create-assignment form when true, with an explanatory banner. No schema change, no new permission. New tests: `tests/catalog.domain.test.ts` (`hasDepartureEnded` unit cases). | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-107 | 2026-08-14 | **A vehicle/driver/guide now waits 24 hours after a tour ends before reading AVAILABLE again** (explicit user request) -- previously immediate: the moment a booking left `{CONFIRMED, IN_PROGRESS}` (including via DR-094's per-departure resync on the lifecycle sweep's COMPLETED transition), `syncFleetAvailabilityForDeparture` (`src/lib/fleet-availability.ts`) recomputed `isCurrentlyBooked = false` and the resource flipped straight to AVAILABLE, with zero turnaround/cleaning/rest window. New `POST_TOUR_AVAILABILITY_DELAY_HOURS = 24` + `isWithinPostTourCooldown(departureEndDate, now)` in `fleet/domain.ts` (same file/style as `INACTIVITY_THRESHOLD_DAYS`/`computeAvailabilityStatus`) -- `syncFleetAvailabilityForDeparture` now also fetches the departure's `endDate` (new no-ctx `catalogService.getDepartureEndDate`, same DR-067 "platform's own scheduler" precedent as `bookingService.hasActiveBookingForDeparture`) and ORs the cooldown into `isCurrentlyBooked`. Deliberately **Vehicle/DriverProfile/GuideProfile only** -- `StarlinkKit` has no parallel `availability` field today (only the separate ACTIVE/INACTIVE/MAINTENANCE `StarlinkStatus`), left out of scope per explicit user direction rather than overloading that operational-hold field the way DR-082 deliberately avoided doing for the other three. A precise 24h window can't be delivered by the existing daily 60-day-inactivity sweep (`/api/jobs/sweep-fleet-availability`) -- none of `syncFleetAvailabilityForDeparture`'s other call sites (assignment create/remove, booking confirm/cancel/refund, the lifecycle sweep's own COMPLETED transition) fire again on their own once the cooldown actually elapses, so a resource would otherwise stay stuck at BOOKED past its window forever. New hourly QStash job `/api/jobs/sweep-fleet-cooldowns` (`polco-sweep-fleet-cooldowns` in `scripts/register-qstash-schedule.ts` -- **not yet registered against the live deployment, requires a manual `npm run qstash:register-schedule` run**, same OI-style external-account-action category as every prior schedule addition) calls new `resyncRecentlyEndedDepartures()`, which re-runs the same sync (idempotent regardless of a departure's current state) for every departure that ended in the last 48h (new no-ctx, cross-org `catalogRepository.listRecentlyEndedDepartures`/`catalogService.listRecentlyEndedDepartures`, mirroring `bookingRepository.sweepAllOrganizations`'s org-loop shape) -- a 2x-cooldown window so a missed/delayed run still catches everything. No schema change, no new permission. New tests: `tests/fleet.domain.test.ts` (`isWithinPostTourCooldown` unit cases). | V1, V4, V6, V7 |
| DR-108 | 2026-08-14 | **Staff can turn an `AWAITING_QUOTATION` `TAILOR_MADE` request into a real, reusable DRAFT `TourPackage`, prefilled from the guest's own plan-my-trip answers** (explicit user request). Confirmed by code review that only 4 of the wizard's 9 steps have a real `TourPackage`-shaped counterpart (Your trip -> `description`, Destination -> `country`, Preferences -> `tags`, Dates -> `durationDays`, computed from `customTravelStart`/`customTravelEnd`) -- the other 5 (Travelers, Sites, Add-ons, Special requests, Contact) have no matching package field; per explicit user direction they're folded into the new package's `description` as appended text sections instead of left behind on the booking alone. New `Booking.customizedPackageId` (`@unique`, nullable FK to `TourPackage`, `onDelete: SetNull`) -- lives on `Booking`, not `TourPackage`, since `booking` already legitimately depends on `catalog` (the reverse would be circular); `TourPackage` gains the required Prisma back-relation field `customizedFromBooking`. New `bookingService.setCustomizedPackage`/`getByCustomizedPackageId` (gated `booking.confirm`, matching the existing cost-breakdown gate) -- one package per booking, never reassigned (409 if already set), blocked on a non-`TAILOR_MADE` origin and on `isBookingLocked` (DR-105) consistency. Building the actual `CreatePackageInput` from the booking's fields and calling `catalogService.createPackage` happens in a new `createCustomizedPackageAction` Server Action (`staff/bookings/[bookingId]/actions.ts`), not inside either module's service -- same "cross-module orchestration lives one level up" convention as `itineraryService` composing `booking`/`catalog`. New button "Create customized package from this request" on the booking detail page (`AWAITING_QUOTATION` + `TAILOR_MADE` + `booking.confirm`, alongside the existing cost-breakdown link), redirecting to the new package on success; once created, the same slot shows a "View customized package" link instead. New reverse-direction backlink on `/staff/packages/[packageId]` ("Created from booking {ref}"), `can()`-guarded on `booking.read` before the lookup. Schema change (`Booking.customizedPackageId`, `TourPackage.customizedFromBooking`) applied by hand to the shared Neon DB with explicit user confirmation, same precedent as every prior schema change this session; no RLS change needed (nullable column on an already-covered tenant table). No new permission. | V1, V4, V6 |
| DR-109 | 2026-08-14 | **Replaced the native `window.confirm()` dialog (DR-086) with an in-app modal for every destructive/irreversible action platform-wide** (explicit user request: "the warning comes from the browser, I want an in-app warning appearing in the middle of the page"). Confirmed a single choke point -- all 26 `confirmMessage`-using call sites go through one `SubmitButton` component, so the mechanism changed in exactly one place. New `src/components/ui/ConfirmDialog.tsx` (same centered-overlay shape as `gallery-grid.tsx`'s existing image lightbox -- `framer-motion` `AnimatePresence`, Escape and a backdrop click both cancel, an inner click stops propagation). `SubmitButton` now `preventDefault()`s a confirm-gated click, opens the dialog holding a reference to the button's own form, and calls `form.requestSubmit()` on confirm instead of relying on a native dialog's synchronous return value -- necessarily asynchronous where `window.confirm()` was synchronous, since a React-rendered modal can't block the call stack the way a native dialog does. New `Common.confirm` i18n key (EN+FR) alongside the existing `Common.cancel`. This also **retires the `page.once('dialog', (d) => d.accept())` Playwright pattern** documented as a gotcha since DR-087 -- there is no longer a native dialog for Playwright to auto-dismiss; the one e2e test that used it (`e2e/departures.spec.ts`) now just clicks the modal's own "Confirm" button (`getByRole('alertdialog')...getByRole('button', { name: 'Confirm' })`) like any other UI element. Grepped `e2e/` first to confirm this was the only affected test. No schema change, no new permission, no new module dependency. | V1, V6 |
| DR-110 | 2026-08-14 | **Homepage "Trusted by" partners/clients section, between How it works and the closing CTA band** (explicit user request, design agreed before content). New `src/components/PartnersMarquee.tsx` -- a continuous horizontal scroll (pauses on hover, seamless loop via a duplicated track animating to exactly -50%), falling back to a plain wrapped row under `prefers-reduced-motion` (same convention as `Reveal`, which wraps the whole section for its own scroll-in entrance). Per explicit user direction, no real partner names/logos exist yet -- placeholder data (`PARTNERS` in `(guest)/page.tsx`) repeats the `BrandMark` + "Polco Tours" lockup (the same icon+wordmark already used in the site header/footer) rather than fabricating a logo for a real organization, consistent with this app's "never fabricate imagery" convention (OI-12); each `Partner` entry optionally takes a `logoUrl` for when real per-partner logos are supplied, at which point an `<img>` renders in place of the text lockup. New `HomePage.partnersEyebrow`/`partnersTitle` i18n keys (EN+FR). No schema change, no new permission. | V1, V6 |
| DR-111 | 2026-08-14 | **Age/nationality/ID-passport-number optional for a TAILOR_MADE traveler, plus auto-creating (not just manually triggering) DR-108's customized package once setup finishes.** Per explicit user direction: plan-my-trip never collects real per-traveler age/nationality/passport data (only the tour lead's own citizenship/country of residence, and a bare seat count for everyone else), so staff setting up a TAILOR_MADE booking's travelers on `/staff/bookings/[bookingId]/travelers/new` shouldn't have to assume/fabricate a value just to satisfy the form -- a PREDEFINED_PACKAGE booking (real, immediate travel) keeps them required, unchanged. `Traveler.age`/`nationality`/`idOrPassportNumber` become nullable columns; `AddTravelerInput`'s zod schema now only validates their *shape* when present, with the actual requiredness enforced in `bookingService.addTraveler` via new `requiresFullTravelerDetails(origin)` (`false` only for `TAILOR_MADE`) -- a PREDEFINED_PACKAGE booking omitting one still 422s. The staff form's Nationality select gets an explicit "Not specified yet" blank option for a TAILOR_MADE booking (rather than silently submitting whichever country sorts first); Age/ID-passport-number inputs drop `required`. `visaService.submitApplication`/`autoSubmitOnPassportUpload` both now guard against a null nationality/idOrPassportNumber (a visa application genuinely cannot be filed without them) -- the manual path throws a clear validation error, the best-effort auto-submit-on-upload path just skips (same graceful-degradation precedent as an unresolvable destination country), leaving the traveler visible on the "needs application" reconciliation view for staff awareness. Second half, same explicit direction: the customized-package creation composed in DR-108's Server Action is extracted into a shared `src/lib/create-customized-package.ts` helper (still one-level-up cross-module orchestration, not inside either module's service) and now auto-fires -- once, `setCustomizedPackage` itself rejects a second one -- the moment a TAILOR_MADE booking's traveler/passport setup wizard reaches its true last step (whichever of `travelers/new` or `passport` finishes last, depending on whether the booking's add-ons required a passport upload), redirecting straight to the new package instead of the booking detail page; the manual "Create customized package" button on the booking detail page stays as the fallback for a booking whose setup completed before this change. No RLS change (column nullability only, not a new table); `docs/openapi.yaml`'s `Traveler`/`AddTravelerInput`/`TravelerDutyView` schemas updated to match. | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |
| DR-112 | 2026-08-15 | **Live production incident, caused by DR-111.** The staff booking-detail page (`/staff/bookings/[bookingId]/page.tsx`) unconditionally called `invoicingService.getOrCreateInvoiceForBooking` once traveler/passport setup was complete -- which throws a 409 ("no price yet") until a `TAILOR_MADE` booking's quotation is sent.
