Before DR-111 this was effectively unreachable (finishing traveler setup required knowing every traveler's real age/nationality/passport, which plan-my-trip never collects, so staff rarely if ever completed it before quoting); DR-111 made age/nationality/idOrPassportNumber optional and added an auto-redirect straight into this exact state, turning a latent bug into a routine crash -- confirmed live via Vercel's production error logs (`vercel logs --level error`) matching the reported digest's booking-detail request path. Fix: `invoice`/`payments` are only fetched when `booking.priceMinor != null`; the invoice card, payments section, and the rating-code panel's `invoice.status === 'PAID'` check are all now null-safe, falling back to the pre-existing setup/quotation UI (unaffected) for a booking still awaiting quotation. New e2e regression test (`staff-dashboard.spec.ts`, `seedStaffAndTailorMadeAwaitingQuotation` fixture) seeds exactly this state (finalized add-ons, one traveler with no age/nationality/idOrPassportNumber, `AWAITING_QUOTATION`) and asserts the page renders the quotation form rather than crashing. No schema/permission change. | V1, V6 |
| DR-113 | 2026-08-15 | **Guest-facing Weather page, linked from the footer only (explicit user direction: not top nav)** -- lists current conditions for 26 towns across the 4 operating countries, grouped by country (`/weather`), with a per-town detail page (`/weather/[town]`) showing current conditions, a 7-day forecast, and staff-authored seasonal/travel notes. Town list is a static config (`src/lib/weather-towns.ts`, mirroring `destination-sites.ts`'s convention), not a DB table -- no schema/RLS/admin-UI, since nothing here is tenant-scoped. New `weather` module (`domain.ts`/`gateway.ts`/`service.ts`/`index.ts`, no `repository.ts` -- same "no owned table" shape as `insights`/`tracking`): `weatherService.getPublicTownWeather`/`listPublicTowns` take no `AuthContext` at all (same "no ctx exists for these callers" precedent as `content`'s `getPublicSiteContent`), and both **degrade to a `null` current/forecast rather than throwing** if the gateway fails -- charter rule 8, since a page render must never hard-fail just because a third-party integration is down. `WeatherGateway` mirrors `itinerary/gateway.ts`'s `StaticMapsGateway` shape (env-gated, `AbortSignal.timeout(5000)`, one collapsed error type, zod-validated response) but deliberately adds **one bounded retry on a genuine failure** (never on timeout/abort, mirroring `notifications/gateway.ts`'s `ResendEmailGateway` rule) since this backs a high-traffic public page rather than a low-frequency admin-only download; still no circuit breaker, since call volume is already bounded by the new `weather-cache.ts` (Upstash Redis, same `getRedisClient()` construction pattern as `rate-limit.ts`, skips caching gracefully when unconfigured) -- a documented narrowing of charter rule 8's four listed protections, not a silent omission. No IP-based rate-limiting: this is a different risk category than `find-booking`/rating-code's enumeration guard (a small fixed set of 26 town slugs, not a guest-supplied lookup), and the cache is what actually bounds cost against the billed API. `seasonalNotes` is deliberately scoped to weather-driven logistics only (rainy season timing, road access, best months) and must never restate `CountryRegulation`'s travel-advisory content (the actual source of truth for that, staff-editable, immigration module) -- Goma/Bukavu's notes stay purely climate-focused with a neutral "check current travel conditions with us" pointer to `/contact`, since `CountryRegulation` has no guest-facing read path to link to and this page has no authority to make a security judgment. DR-097's Public/Customized staff hub and its two list pages updated: `/staff/packages/public` now also filters by an Available/Unavailable Status dropdown (new `StaffPackages.available`/`.unavailable` i18n keys), `/staff/packages/customized`'s existing DRAFT/ARCHIVED partition is unaffected (still "not published, either sub-status"). Package status badge (`PACKAGE_STATUS_TONE`) gives `PUBLISHED_UNAVAILABLE` its own `warning` tone, distinct from `PUBLISHED_AVAILABLE`'s `success`; new `PackageStatusLabel` EN/FR keys read "Published - Available"/"Published - Unavailable" (FR: "Publié - Disponible"/"Publié - Indisponible"). No guest-facing page changes were needed beyond the status-string rename -- the guest package detail page's existing `bookable` gate and its own generic `Common.available`/`.unavailable` badge (a pre-existing, differently-scoped "is this instance bookable right now" concept, not the same thing as this DR's package-level status) already do exactly the "still listed, CTA disabled" behavior once `bookable` narrows to `PUBLISHED_AVAILABLE`. Schema change: existing `PUBLISHED` rows must be migrated via `ALTER TYPE "PackageStatus" RENAME VALUE 'PUBLISHED' TO 'PUBLISHED_AVAILABLE'` before `ALTER TYPE ... ADD VALUE 'PUBLISHED_UNAVAILABLE'` and `db push` -- a plain `db push` cannot itself rename an enum value in use, so this order matters; applied by hand to the shared Neon DB with explicit user confirmation, same precedent as every prior schema change this session. `prisma/seed.ts`'s demo packages seed as `PUBLISHED_AVAILABLE`. No RLS change (same table, no new tenant-scoped column), no new permission, no new module dependency. | V1, V6 |

Unlike the Fleet/Packages/Bookings hubs, the Finance hub itself **keeps** the `SidebarShell`/`SETTINGS_ITEMS` wrapper (real gap found post-merge: it's `nav.tsx`'s top-level "Settings" aggregate link's landing page, so losing the sidebar there would orphan it from the rest of Settings -- Country Regulations, Sites, Insights, etc. -- one click away like every other `SETTINGS_ITEMS` page); `nav.tsx`'s Settings link now points at `/staff/settings/finance` instead of the no-longer-valid `/staff/settings/tax-rates`. | DR-124 | 2026-08-16 | **Root-caused a recurring `guest-checkout.spec.ts` CI flake** (the same E2E timeout had failed `guest-checkout.spec.ts:74` -- `page.waitForURL(/\/travelers\/new$/)` after the Add-ons step's "Continue" -- on essentially every CI run across many unrelated commits, both the original attempt and its retry, per the run history; three prior increments (see the file's own comments) had each fixed a *different* race at this same step without resolving the actual recurrence). The Playwright trace's page snapshot at time of failure was the key evidence: still showing the Add-ons page, "Continue" button back in its normal (non-pending) state, no error `Alert` rendered anywhere -- i.e. `finalizeAddonsAction` had already resolved successfully and `router.push()` had already been called (its non-awaited call returns immediately, and the `finally` block's `setPending(false)` runs right after), yet the URL never changed. Root cause: `src/app/(guest)/booking/[bookingId]/` (the whole wizard -- Add-ons/Travelers/Passport) had no `loading.tsx` of its own, and the only ancestor one (`(guest)/loading.tsx`) sits above every nested navigation within the guest route group and so never re-fires for a same-group step change -- with no Suspense boundary at or below the changing segment, Next.js's App Router defers the client-side URL/history update itself until the destination page's *entire* server render resolves, with zero interim state. `/travelers/new`'s render is the single heaviest in this journey (session resolution + booking/traveler reads + a conditional tour-lead profile fetch, several of them serialized) -- under CI's small default Prisma connection pool shared across 2 parallel Playwright workers, that occasionally took long enough to blow the test's 60s budget with the browser simply sitting on the OLD page the whole time: no error, no partial navigation, because from the URL's perspective none had started yet. Fix: new `src/app/(guest)/booking/[bookingId]/loading.tsx` gives every step-to-step transition in the wizard its own Suspense boundary, making navigation instant (URL updates immediately, a skeleton streams in) regardless of how long the destination's data fetch takes -- a real production UX fix (any guest on a slow connection was hitting the identical "frozen step" experience), not merely a test workaround. Also parallelized `/travelers/new/page.tsx`'s conditional `authService.getUser` call into its initial `Promise.all` (previously a serial fourth round trip on the common first-traveler path) -- a genuine latency reduction independent of the loading.tsx fix. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6 |

(2) Every staff-portal search field (17 pages: Sites, Hotels, Restaurants, Itineraries, admin Users/Clients, Bookings all + per-status, Packages public/customized, Fleet vehicles/drivers/guides/starlink-kits, Schedule past/in-progress/future) now updates results live as the user types instead of requiring a manual "Filter" click -- new `src/components/ui/SearchField.tsx` (client component) debounces each keystroke (300ms) into a `router.replace` carrying the rest of the current query string across (other filters preserved, `page` reset to 1). Deliberately still a plain input inside the existing GET `<form>`, not a new client-side filtering path -- all matching/filtering logic stays server-side in each page component (charter rule 1/2), so the "Filter" button and Enter-key submission still work with JS disabled; only *when* navigation fires changed. No schema, permission, or module-dependency change. | V1, V6 |
| DR-126 | 2026-08-16 | **Adds a seventh Operational Rate, `AdminCostRate` (explicit user request): a flat per-day administrative-overhead fee, configured per-country/effective-dated on `/staff/finance/rates` exactly like the existing six tables** (`StaffRate`/`HotelRate`/`TransportRate`/`FoodBeverageRate`/`ActivityFee`/`ImmigrationCostRate`, DR-039). Resolved by country + effective date at compute time (`financeRepository.findEffectiveAdminCostRate`, same "no id staff-picks from a dropdown" precedent as `StaffRate` -- there's only ever one active rate per country, unlike `HotelRate`/`ActivityFee`, which reference a specific sub-entity). New enum `AdminCostBasis` (`PER_PERSON` / `PER_GROUP`, default `PER_GROUP`) is a **per cost-breakdown** choice, not part of the rate itself -- staff pick it (alongside a plain `adminDays` count) when setting up a package's or booking's cost breakdown, deciding whether the resolved `dailyRateMinor` is charged once for the whole group (`PER_GROUP`, like the existing Staff/Transport buckets) or multiplied by `referenceGroupSize` (`PER_PERSON`, like the existing Restaurant/Visa buckets). `PackageCostBreakdown`/`BookingCostBreakdown` both gain `adminDays Int @default(0)` + `adminCostBasis AdminCostBasis @default(PER_GROUP)` (additive, same "applies to both cost-breakdown flows" precedent every other bucket already follows); `computeBaseCostMinor` (`finance/domain.ts`) gains one more summed bucket, `adminDailyRateMinor * adminDays * (basis === PER_PERSON ? referenceGroupSize : 1)`. Full stack mirrors the existing six rate tables exactly: `CreateAdminCostRateInput`/`AdminCostRateView` (domain), `listAdminCostRates`/`createAdminCostRate`/`deleteAdminCostRate`/`findEffectiveAdminCostRate` (repository/service, same `requireRateWriter` SUPERADMIN-only gate + `audit()` calls), a new Operational Rates card, and a matching REST pair (`/api/v1/finance/rates/admin-cost` + `[id]`) alongside the UI's server actions -- both package and booking cost-breakdown forms gain an "Admin Costs" section (days input + basis select) using the same "rates resolved automatically for {country}" notice the Staff Costs section already shows. No permission or module-dependency change (reuses `finance_config.read`/`.write` + the existing `catalog.write`/`booking.confirm` gates on the two breakdown flows). | V1, V6 |
| DR-127 | 2026-08-16 | **Business-rule change, explicit user request, confirmed via clarifying question before implementing** (the prior behavior was deliberate, not a bug -- flipping it changes what every tourist is actually charged): the platform rate is now a real customer-facing fee, added on top of the invoice total, instead of an internal commission silently absorbed out of it. Previously `invoicingService.getOrCreateInvoiceForBooking` computed `platformFeeMinor` as a separate `taxOf(totalMinor, platformFeeRateBp)` split that was stored on the invoice but never folded into `totalMinor`/`depositMinor`/`balanceMinor` -- the customer only ever paid subtotal + tax, per `InvoiceView`'s own comment ("never added to what the customer owes"). `computeInvoiceAmounts` (`invoicing/domain.ts`, the one shared formula used by both invoice creation and `applyCoupon`/`removeCoupon`) now takes `platformFeeRateBp` as a required input alongside `taxRateBp`, computes the fee on (discounted subtotal + tax) the same way it always has, and folds it into `totalMinor` before the 40/60 deposit/balance split -- so **the invoice total is now exactly package price (subtotal) + tax (per-country, DR-006) + platform rate (DR-042)**, payable either as a 40% deposit or in full (both already existed, `splitDeposit`/DR-012 and the `initiatePayment` DEPOSIT/FULL paths, DR-024 -- untouched by this DR). `applyCoupon`/`removeCoupon` (`invoicing/repository.ts`) now re-resolve the fee from the invoice's own snapshotted `platformFeeRateBp` (falling back to 0 for the rare grandfathered invoice predating DR-042, where it's `null`) every time they recompute, so a coupon applied/removed after this DR still recomputes every downstream amount consistently. Both the guest checkout page (`(guest)/booking/[bookingId]/page.tsx`) and the staff booking-detail page now show explicit "Platform fee" and "Total" line items on the invoice card -- the guest page previously showed neither (deposit/balance were the only totals visible at all); the staff page previously showed the fee but captioned it as non-charged commission. No schema change (`Invoice.platformFeeMinor`/`platformFeeRateBp` already existed, DR-042) and no permission/module-dependency change -- purely a formula + display change. | V1, V3, V6 |
| DR-128 | 2026-08-16 | **Explicit user request, following a full audit: "all prices must be pulled from Operational Rates."** The audit (agent-run, findings verified before acting) confirmed the core 7-bucket cost-plus engine, tax, coupons, and the platform fee were already correctly rate-table-sourced, and found three real gaps, all closed here. **(1) `sendQuotation` had no enforced link to a TAILOR_MADE booking's own cost breakdown** -- the form only pre-filled `suggestedTotalMinor`, but staff could freely overwrite it with zero audit trail distinguishing "matches Operational Rates" from "hand-typed." `SendQuotationInput` gains an optional `overrideReason`; `sendQuotationAction` (the Server Action, not `bookingService` itself -- `booking` can't import `financeService` without a cycle, since `finance` already depends on `booking`) fetches the breakdown and requires a reason whenever the submitted price/currency deviates from it, surfaced via a new optional "Reason" field + a `?error=quotationReasonRequired` banner on the booking detail page. `bookingService.sendQuotation` logs a distinct `booking.quotation_price_overridden` audit action (with the reason in `metadata`) instead of the plain `booking.quotation_sent` whenever one is supplied -- mirrors `PackageCostBreakdown`'s own override-reason precedent, no new DB column (the audit log is the record, same "not duplicated here" reasoning that entry's own override columns already state). **(2) `TourPackage.priceMinor` was still technically settable via a direct API call to `updatePackage`**, even though no UI had exposed it since DR-039 (confirmed by grep -- every reference is read-only `formatOrPending`). Removed `priceMinor` from `CreatePackageInput`/`UpdatePackageInput` entirely; `catalogService.saveCostBreakdown`'s write now goes through a new dedicated `catalogService.setComputedPrice(ctx, packageId, priceMinor)` (backed by a new `catalogRepository.updatePackagePrice`, bypassing `updatePackage`/`UpdatePackageInput` completely) -- the general package-edit path can now never set a price by any means other than a computed cost breakdown. **(3) Add-on prices (`AddonService.priceMinor`/`currency`) were flat, staff-typed, org-wide figures with zero country dimension or Operational-Rates backing at all** -- confirmed there was no create/edit UI or service method for `AddonService` in the first place (only raw test/seed fixtures), several with duplicate/inconsistent prices already in the shared DB. New 8th Operational Rate, `AddonRate` (country + `AddonCode` + price + currency, effective-dated, resolved by (country, code, date) with no id -- same "no dropdown, one active row" precedent as `StaffRate`/`AdminCostRate`), added to `/staff/finance/rates` with matching REST routes (`/api/v1/finance/rates/addon` + `[id]`) and `financeService` CRUD. Resolution itself lives in new `src/lib/addon-rates.ts` (`getEffectiveAddonRate`), a plain ungated helper -- same "guest checkout has no staff permissions to check" precedent as `src/lib/tax.ts` -- rather than behind `financeService`'s RBAC gate. New shared `bookingService.getBookingCountry` (factoring out the same PREDEFINED_PACKAGE-departure-country/TAILOR_MADE-customCountry resolution `invoicing/service.ts` already had inline) feeds both `setAddons` (now resolving each addon's real chargeable price from `AddonRate`, never `AddonService`'s own flat fields) and the guest/staff add-ons picker pages (now hiding any add-on with no rate configured for the booking's country -- explicit user decision, never falling back to the old flat price). `setAddons`'s new country/rate lookups run concurrently (`Promise.all`) rather than sequentially -- a genuine latency fix alongside the correctness one, not just a test convenience. No permission or module-dependency change (finance's dependency on booking/catalog is unchanged; the new lib helper isn't a module import at all). | V1, V6 |
| DR-129 | 2026-08-16 | **Explicit user request: a package's itinerary template can now be bulk-generated from its own `durationDays` instead of staff adding every day one at a time.** New `catalogService.generateTemplateDays(ctx, packageId)` reads the package's `durationDays` (throws `Errors.conflict` if it's not set yet, surfaced via the same `?error=&detail=` convention DR-115 already established on this page) and bulk-creates a bare `PackageItineraryDay` row (day number only, no times/hotel/restaurant/activities/notes) for every day number `1..durationDays` that doesn't already have one -- `catalogRepository.generateMissingTemplateDays` uses `createMany({ skipDuplicates: true })` against the existing `@@unique([tourPackageId, dayNumber])` constraint, so it's idempotent: re-running after staff have already filled in some days only fills the remaining gaps, never touches or duplicates an existing row. New "Generate {count} days" button on `/staff/packages/[packageId]` (gated `catalog.write`, same as the existing manual "Add a template day" form it sits above), hidden in favor of a hint when `durationDays` is null. Each generated day is then edited in place through the existing per-day edit form -- no change to `addTemplateDay`/`updateTemplateDay`/`removeTemplateDay`, `AddPackageItineraryDayInput`, or the `PackageItineraryDay` schema. No schema, permission, or module-dependency change. | V1, V6 |
| DR-130 | 2026-08-16 | **Live production bug, reported by the user as "Something went wrong uploading the image" on the staff package edit page.** Root-caused (not just re-logged): `src/lib/public-image-blob.ts`'s `uploadPublicImage` (DR-071, reused by DR-114 for package images) calls Vercel Blob's `put()` with `access: 'public'`, but the project's only Blob store, `polco-tours-documents`, was provisioned private-only for passport/visa documents (`documents/gateway.ts`) -- Vercel Blob stores are public-or-private store-wide, not per-object, so every public image upload has silently failed with a swallowed `catch {}` since DR-071 shipped (About/FAQ images were equally broken the whole time, just never reported). Confirmed by reproducing the exact `put()` call locally against the production token (`Vercel Blob: Cannot use public access on a private store`) before touching any infra. Fixed at two layers: (1) the previously-bare `catch {}` in `uploadPublicImage` now logs the real underlying error server-side (`logger(...).error`) before throwing the same generic `PublicImageBlobGatewayError` to the caller -- charter rule 8, no internals leaked to the client, but a real failure here is no longer invisible; (2) a second Blob store, `polco-tours-public-images` (`fra1`, matching the existing store's region), was provisioned and connected to the project under a dedicated env var (`PUBLIC_BLOB_READ_WRITE_TOKEN_READ_WRITE_TOKEN` -- Vercel's dashboard "Connect Project" flow namespaces a custom-named connection's sub-values under the chosen prefix, it isn't a single flat var), and `uploadPublicImage` now passes that token to `put()` explicitly rather than relying on the ambient default `BLOB_READ_WRITE_TOKEN` (which stays bound to the original private `polco-tours-documents` store, unchanged -- `documents/gateway.ts` needed no code change). Connected for Production + Preview only as of this writing; Development wasn't included in the dashboard step, so local `npm run dev` package-image upload will still fail with the same error until that's added too (see Open Items). No schema/permission/module-dependency change -- this is an integration/config fix, logged as a DR per the living-document mandate because it's a new external-service credential. | V1, V6, V8 |
| DR-131 | 2026-08-16 | **Explicit user request, confirmed via a clarifying question first since it partially reverses DR-118's "slug generated once at creation, never reassigned" rule.** `catalogService.updatePackage` now re-derives `TourPackage.slug` from the package's title at the moment it first goes DRAFT -> a published status (`PUBLISHED_AVAILABLE`/`PUBLISHED_UNAVAILABLE`), so the public URL reflects whichever title the package actually launches with rather than whatever it was called at creation -- title edits made while still `DRAFT` no longer leave a stale, never-updated slug once the package finally goes live. Slug generation stays frozen after that first publish (a later `PUBLISHED_AVAILABLE` <-> `PUBLISHED_UNAVAILABLE` toggle, even with a title change alongside it, does not touch the slug), matching DR-118's original link-rot-avoidance intent for anything already public. New `catalogRepository.regeneratePackageSlug` (bypasses `UpdatePackageInput`/`updatePackage`, same "no path other than this one may set it" precedent as DR-128's `updatePackagePrice`) is called from `updatePackage` only when the pre-update status was `DRAFT` and the incoming status is published. **Found and fixed a real latent bug in DR-118's original slug-uniqueness check while building this**, surfaced by an end-to-end collision test: `nextUniqueSlug`'s pre-write `SELECT` (deliberately run through the plain unscoped `prisma` client, not `withOrg`, specifically so it could see every org's slugs) actually returns **zero rows regardless of org** once connected through the correct least-privilege `polco_app` role, because `tour_packages` has `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and Postgres denies-by-default when no `app.org_id` GUC is set -- so the "is this slug already taken by another org" check has silently never worked when run through the intended production role, though a role with `BYPASSRLS` (e.g. `neondb_owner`) masks this locally. Replaced with `withUniqueSlug`, which detects collisions by attempting the actual write and retrying with `-2`, `-3`, ... on a real unique-constraint violation (`P2002`) instead -- Postgres enforces a unique index independent of RLS visibility, so this works correctly regardless of which role is connected. Both `createPackage` and the new `regeneratePackageSlug` now go through this helper. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6 |
| DR-132 | 2026-08-16 | **Explicit user request, confirmed via clarifying questions before implementing (four real ambiguities, each with a materially different financial outcome): the Accommodation, Restaurant, and Activity cost-breakdown buckets are no longer staff-typed inputs -- they're derived automatically from a package's own Day Template** (`PackageItineraryDay.hotelId`/`restaurantId`/`activityIds`, DR-116/119/120), resolved against Operational Rates by entity id + effective date, applying to **both** `PackageCostBreakdown` and `BookingCostBreakdown` (the latter via `Booking.customizedPackageId`, DR-108 -- 0 for all three buckets if no customized package is linked yet, not an error). Per the clarifying answers: **(1) Accommodation is now per-traveler** -- sum of each Day Template day's assigned hotel's effective `HotelRate.nightlyRateMinor`, x `referenceGroupSize`, replacing the old single staff-picked `hotelRateId` x `nights` x `roomsNeeded` whole-group figure (`roomsNeeded` removed). **(2) A new 9th Operational Rate, `RestaurantRate`** (country + `restaurantId` + one flat `dailyRateMinor`, effective-dated, same "required id, no legacy-nullable case" shape as `HotelRate` minus the nullable-for-history exception) replaces the old flat country-wide `FoodBeverageRate` `BREAKFAST`/`LUNCH`/`DINNER` categories for restaurant costing -- one rate per restaurant-day since a Day Template only ever assigns one restaurant per day, summed across days-with-a-restaurant x `referenceGroupSize`. Those 3 `FoodBeverageCategory` enum values are left in the schema (historical rows, no destructive enum change) but no longer read by `computeBaseCostMinor`; the create form on `/staff/finance/rates` no longer offers them. **(3) Activities are now fully automatic too** -- every `activityId` across every Day Template day resolves its effective `ActivityFee`, summed x `referenceGroupSize` (same day-appears-twice-charged-twice logic as before, just no longer a staff-picked line item). **(4) Applies to both flows**, per explicit choice. **Fully automatic, no manual override for these three buckets** (the fourth clarifying answer) -- `PackageCostBreakdown`/`BookingCostBreakdown` drop `hotelRateId`/`roomsNeeded`/`breakfastCount`/`lunchCount`/`dinnerCount` entirely (destructive columns, verified zero rows had any value in them on the shared DB before dropping) and gain three read-only snapshot columns, `computedAccommodationMinor`/`computedRestaurantMinor`/`computedActivitiesMinor`, shown on both cost-breakdown pages alongside the existing base-cost/selling-price card. `PackageCostLineItem`/`BookingCostLineItem` drop their `activityFeeId` variant (destructive, same zero-rows verification) -- they're drinks-only now (`foodBeverageRateId` required, no longer optional), staff-entered exactly as before since the Day Template has no concept of drinks. `finance/domain.ts`'s `computeBaseCostMinor` is refactored into a new `computeCostBuckets` (returns all eight buckets individually, for both the total and the persisted display snapshots) + `computeBaseCostMinor` (sums them) -- no business logic duplicated between the two. New `financeRepository.findEffectiveHotelRateForHotel`/`findEffectiveRestaurantRateForRestaurant`/`findEffectiveActivityFeeForActivity` (resolve-by-entity-id-and-date, the same "no id staff-picks from a dropdown" pattern `StaffRate`/`AdminCostRate` already use, now extended to entity-tied rates since staff no longer pick a specific rate row for these three buckets) replace the old `findHotelRateById`/`findActivityFeesByIds` for this purpose -- staff picking a *specific historical* rate row was never date-gated before; automatic resolution now genuinely needs to be. `saveCostBreakdown`/`saveBookingCostBreakdown` both gained a new currency-consistency check across every resolved Day-Template rate (this never existed for the package flow before -- a latent gap now closed alongside the redesign, since staff can no longer see/verify currencies via a dropdown for these three buckets) and a new `?error=&detail=` try/catch on the package cost-breakdown page's Server Action (previously absent; an incomplete Day Template's `Errors.conflict` -- now a common, expected outcome -- would otherwise crash to Next's generic error page, same convention DR-115 already established for the sibling booking-breakdown flow). No permission/module-dependency change (reuses `finance_config.read`/`.write`, `catalog.write`, `booking.confirm`, `itinerary.read` -- all already held together by every role that needs this). **Schema change (new `RestaurantRate` table, additive columns, destructive column drops verified zero-risk) applied by hand to the shared Neon DB** after confirming no existing row anywhere had data in any column being dropped. | V1, V6 |
| DR-133 | 2026-08-16 | **Live production bug, user-reported: "Application error: a server-side exception has occurred" when assigning a driver, guide, or vehicle.** Root-caused via `vercel logs --prod` (uncaught `ApiError` 404 "Departure not found") plus a direct repro script against the shared Neon DB. A `TourPackage` had been soft-deleted (`deletedAt` set) while its `Departure` still carried a live vehicle/driver/guide `Assignment` -- soft-deleting a package doesn't cascade to its Departure/Assignment rows, and `catalogService.getDepartureDetail` correctly 404s once its package is gone. The bug: `assignmentService`'s shared `hasOverlappingAssignment` helper (used by both `recommendAssignment`, the departure-detail page's data source, and `createAssignment` itself) calls `getDepartureDetail` on every *other* departure a candidate vehicle/driver/guide has ever been assigned to, with no error handling -- so once one such orphaned departure existed, viewing or creating an assignment for *any* unrelated departure crashed the whole page, as long as it involved that same vehicle/driver/guide. Fixed by catching a 404 `ApiError` from that lookup and treating it as "no conflict" (a departure that can no longer be resolved can't meaningfully overlap anything) instead of letting the exception escape -- `hasOverlappingAssignment` in `src/modules/assignment/service.ts`. Verified against the real data (the exact departure/vehicle/driver/guide combination from the production logs) via a throwaway repro script before and after the fix. No schema/permission/module-dependency change -- pure defensive-handling fix, no data was altered (the soft-deleted package and its orphaned departure/assignment are left as-is; cleaning up long-orphaned assignments once their package is gone is a possible future enhancement, not yet decided). | V1, V6, V8 |
| DR-134 | 2026-08-16 | **Explicit user request, walked through a concrete numeric cost-breakdown example first (base cost -> agency margin -> subtotal -> tax -> platform fee -> total) and confirmed via two clarifying questions before implementing, since the natural implementation collided with a real double-charging bug.** `financeService.saveCostBreakdown` now folds tax (`src/lib/tax.ts`, by the package's own `country`) and the platform fee (`src/lib/platform-rate.ts`) into the stored `TourPackage.priceMinor`, on top of the pre-existing base-cost-plus-agency-margin subtotal -- previously `priceMinor` was tax/fee-exclusive, with both added fresh at invoice time. Research surfaced that `TourPackage.priceMinor` flows straight through to `Booking.priceMinor` (scaled by seats, snapshotted at hold time in `bookingService.finalizeHold`) and then into `invoicingService.getOrCreateInvoiceForBooking`, which calls `computeInvoiceAmounts` to add tax + platform fee **again, fresh** -- so baking them into `priceMinor` unconditionally would have double-charged every `PREDEFINED_PACKAGE` guest at checkout. Presented with two ways to resolve this (keep `priceMinor` tax-exclusive and show a separate estimate for staff, vs. store the tax-inclusive total and skip re-taxing at invoice time using a snapshotted rate), the user chose the latter, explicitly accepting the resulting trade-off: a booking reflects whichever tax/platform rate was effective when its package's cost breakdown was last saved, not a rate that changes afterward (a deliberate, confirmed narrowing of DR-006/DR-127's "always resolve tax fresh" guarantee for this one flow) -- and asked that the agency margin step not be dropped from the formula. Implementation: new shared pure helper `src/lib/pricing.ts` (`applyTaxAndPlatformFee`, deliberately independent of invoicing's own `computeInvoiceAmounts` so this carries zero regression risk to the existing, tested invoicing/coupon formula; `impliedSubtotalMinor` inverts it for the override case, so a staff-typed final price still decomposes into a consistent subtotal+rate pair). `TourPackage` gains `priceSubtotalMinor`/`priceTaxRateBp`/`pricePlatformFeeRateBp` (per seat, additive, null unless `priceMinor` was set by this flow); `PackageCostBreakdown` gains five display/audit-trail snapshot columns (`computedTaxMinor`/`computedPlatformFeeMinor`/`computedTotalMinor`/`taxRateBpSnapshot`/`platformFeeRateBpSnapshot`, whole-group, mirroring the existing `computedBaseCostMinor`/`computedSellingPriceMinor` precedent); `Booking` gains the same three `price*` fields as `TourPackage` (whole-booking, i.e. already x seats), copied across in `finalizeHold` via an extended `DepartureDetail`/`CreateHoldParams`, but **only** when the departure is actually using the package's own price (null for a departure with its own manual `priceOverrideMinor`, a bespoke departure, or a `TAILOR_MADE` booking -- none of those has a package breakdown to attribute a price to). `invoicingService.getOrCreateInvoiceForBooking` branches on whether a booking carries this snapshot: if so, it trusts the snapshotted subtotal + rates instead of re-resolving `getEffectiveTaxRate`/`getEffectivePlatformRate` live; if not (every pre-DR-134 booking, every `TAILOR_MADE` booking, and every departure-price-override booking), behavior is byte-for-byte unchanged from before this DR. `applyCoupon`/`removeCoupon` needed no changes at all -- they already recompute purely from the persisted `Invoice` row's own `subtotalMinor`/`taxRateBp`/`platformFeeRateBp`, regardless of how those were originally derived. `saveBookingCostBreakdown`/`sendQuotation` (the `TAILOR_MADE` cost-breakdown flow) are deliberately untouched -- out of scope per the user's own framing ("apply this to the package cost breakdown"). **Schema change (nine new nullable columns across `TourPackage`/`PackageCostBreakdown`/`Booking`, no destructive step) applied by hand to the shared Neon DB** -- verified via `psql` that every new column is null on every pre-existing row. No permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6 |
| DR-135 | 2026-08-17 | **Explicit user request, content/layout nailed down across several rounds of clarifying questions rather than assumed** (per the user's own explicit "do not assume" instruction): a staff-only PDF, downloadable from `/staff/packages/[packageId]` in English or French, combining a plain-language cost summary with the package's day-by-day itinerary Day Template -- something staff can print/attach without walking someone through the live cost-breakdown screen. New `financeService.generatePackageSummaryPdf` (gated `catalog.write`, same as `getCostBreakdown`) composes `catalogService.getPackage`/`listTemplateDays`, `itineraryService.listHotelsByIds`/`listRestaurantsByIds`/`listActivitiesByIds` (finance already legitimately depends on both, no new module dependency), and a fresh per-day `HotelRate` resolution for the itemized accommodation table (day-by-day hotel/rate attribution was never persisted -- DR-132 only persists the summed bucket -- so this one part of the document is resolved live at generation time rather than from a snapshot; a day whose rate can no longer be resolved shows as such rather than failing the whole document). New `src/modules/finance/package-summary-pdf.tsx` renders it via `@react-pdf/renderer`, mirroring `itinerary/map-pdf.tsx`'s existing shape exactly (`Document`/`Page`/`View`/`Text`/`Svg` primitives, `renderToBuffer`) -- the site's own `BrandMark` placeholder mark (no real uploaded logo file exists anywhere in this repo) reproduced with `Svg`/`Circle`/`Path`. New route `api/v1/catalog/packages/[packageId]/summary-pdf` is a thin pass-through, same convention as the existing map-pdf route (`withAuth`, `nodejs`/`force-dynamic`, `Content-Disposition: attachment`). Every cost-summary row is explicitly labeled with its basis (per-person vs. whole-group total, since the user specified a genuine mix of both) and the Grand Total is always `TourPackage.priceMinor` directly -- never a hand-recomputed sum -- so the document can never disagree with what a guest is actually charged. Company/footer details (name, address, registration IDs, contact emails, operating-countries line, "Powered by Cyber PolCo") are fixed user-supplied constants in that one file, same "static reference data, not a staff-editable CRUD screen" precedent as `src/lib/weather-towns.ts`. Two real bugs found and fixed while building this, both isolated to this one new file: (1) `PackageCostBreakdown` had never individually persisted its Transport/Admin cost buckets -- `computeCostBuckets` already computed both at save time, DR-132 only added individual snapshot columns for Accommodation/Restaurant/Activities, so Transport/Admin were only ever visible folded into the aggregate `computedBaseCostMinor`; fixed by adding `computedTransportMinor`/`computedAdminMinor` (additive) and having `saveCostBreakdown` persist them like the other three, rather than recomputing them live at PDF-generation time (per DR-134's own lesson: never re-resolve a rate that might have drifted since save time). (2) The French-language PDF initially rendered every currency amount corrupted (`"24,915.00"` came out as `"24/915,00 $NA"`) -- `src/lib/money.ts`'s `format` (`Intl.NumberFormat`) is correct in every real browser, but react-pdf's built-in Helvetica font lacks the glyph for the narrow no-break space French locale data uses as its thousands separator, silently falling back to a wrong character; fixed with a new `formatMoneyForPdf` helper local to this one file (plain ASCII digits/comma/period only), left the app-wide `format` helper itself untouched since every other page renders in a real browser with full Unicode font support. Also confirmed (research only, not fixed -- out of scope, this PDF's own small hardcoded EN/FR label dictionary works around it entirely) that `src/i18n/request.ts`'s `getRequestConfig` ignores any explicit `locale` argument passed to `getTranslations`, always deriving from the cookie instead -- a latent gap for any *future* feature wanting a genuine per-request locale override. No permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6 |

| DR-136 | 2026-08-17 | **Explicit user request**: SUPERADMIN can now update an Operational Rate in place (all eight cost-plus rate tables -- Staff/Hotel/Restaurant/Transport/Food & Beverage/Activity Fee/Immigration Cost/Admin Cost -- plus Add-on Rate for UI consistency), not just delete-and-recreate it, on `/staff/finance/rates` (an inline `<details>`-based Edit form per row, no new client component) and via a matching `PATCH /api/v1/finance/rates/*/:id` on every existing rate route. **For the eight rates that feed the cost-plus engine, saving an update immediately triggers `financeService.reapplyRatesToAllCostBreakdowns`**: every existing `PackageCostBreakdown` and `BookingCostBreakdown` (any TAILOR_MADE booking that already has one) is replayed through the exact same `saveCostBreakdown`/`saveBookingCostBreakdown` path the staff form itself uses -- re-resolving every referenced rate (now at its current price) and re-saving, so `TourPackage.priceMinor` (and a booking's suggested total) reflects the new price immediately rather than staying pinned to whatever was effective when the breakdown was last saved. Confirmed via clarifying questions before building: (1) scope is package **and** tailor-made booking breakdowns, not packages alone; (2) a breakdown carrying a staff price override (`overridePriceMinor`) is replayed too -- its display-only `computed*Minor` bucket snapshots refresh against the new rate, but the override itself, and therefore `TourPackage.priceMinor`, is deliberately left untouched (an override is a deliberate final word, not something a rate change should silently overwrite -- `saveCostBreakdown`/`saveBookingCostBreakdown` already carry `overridePriceMinor` through unchanged, so this required no new logic, only replaying the breakdown's own persisted inputs); (3) one breakdown failing to recompute (an unresolved rate, a booking that's gone terminal since its breakdown was saved, a package no longer visible) is skipped, not fatal to the sweep or to the rate update itself -- the caller sees exact counts plus a per-item skip reason, surfaced to staff as a banner on `/staff/finance/rates` (`?reapplied=1&packagesUpdated=&packagesSkipped=&bookingsUpdated=&bookingsSkipped=`, same query-string-summary convention as the existing `?error=&detail=` one). Deliberately global rather than scoped to just the rate that changed: most rates resolve by (country, effective date) rather than a stored FK on the breakdown, so there is no cheap way to know in advance which breakdowns a given rate actually feeds -- replaying every one is the only reliable way to find out. `AddonRate`'s update has no reapply step at all -- it is resolved live at add-on-selection time (`src/lib/addon-rates.ts`), never snapshotted into a cost breakdown, so there is nothing to recompute. Also updates this page's own intro copy, which previously told staff to "add a new row rather than editing an old one" -- now explicitly documents both options (add a new row to preserve price history; edit in place to correct a price and cascade it everywhere). No schema/permission/module-dependency change -- `updateXRate` reuses each rate's own `CreateXInput` zod schema (full replace of every field except identity via URL id, not a separate "price-only" schema), the same ownership re-validation (`itineraryService.getHotel`/`getRestaurant`/`getActivity`) as each rate's create path, and the existing `requireRateWriter` SUPERADMIN-only gate. | V6, V7 |

| DR-137 | 2026-08-17 | **Explicit user request**: a detailed, whole-itinerary PDF is now downloadable from `/staff/itineraries/[itineraryId]` once the itinerary reaches `APPROVED` (the enum's terminal "confirmed" state -- there is no separate `CONFIRMED` value; the request's "once an itinerary is confirmed" maps onto this existing status, not a new one). New `itineraryService.streamItinerarySummaryPdf` (gated `itinerary.read`, same permission as the existing per-day map PDF) 404s via the existing anti-BOLA `getOwnedItinerary` scoping and 409s (`Errors.conflict`) if the itinerary isn't yet `APPROVED`, then composes `listDays` plus batch hotel/restaurant/activity/site name resolution (same lookups the detail page itself already does) into one document: every day's date/times, pickup/drop-off, hotel, restaurant, ordered planned sites, resolved activities plus the free-text activity note, estimated travel time, and day notes, plus the itinerary's own top-level notes and emergency contact. New `src/modules/itinerary/itinerary-summary-pdf.tsx` renders it via `@react-pdf/renderer`, deliberately mirroring `map-pdf.tsx`'s plain, letterhead-free layout rather than `finance/package-summary-pdf.tsx`'s branded/EN+FR one -- this is an internal operational document for staff/guide/driver use, not a client-facing proposal, so it skips the company letterhead and stays English-only (a deliberate, narrower scope than DR-135's EN/FR precedent; can be revisited if a French version is actually needed here later). "Without the prices" needed no stripping logic at all: neither `Itinerary` nor `ItineraryDay` has ever had a price/money field, so there was nothing to exclude. New route `api/v1/itineraries/[itineraryId]/summary-pdf` is a thin pass-through, same convention as the existing day map-pdf route (`withAuth('itinerary.read')`, `nodejs`/`force-dynamic`, `Content-Disposition: attachment`). The download link on the detail page is shown to anyone who can view the page at all (not just `itinerary.write`/`itinerary.approve` holders) once `itinerary.status === 'APPROVED'`, since a TOUR_GUIDE/DRIVER assigned to the trip is exactly who most needs this document in hand. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6 |

| DR-138 | 2026-08-17 | **Explicit user request**: creating (or later editing) a staff account that holds DRIVER, TOUR_GUIDE, and/or VEHICLE_OWNER now auto-provisions a starter fleet record for each of those roles, instead of leaving staff to remember to create one separately on `/staff/fleet/*` -- everything beyond the bare minimum is left for staff to fill in afterward, same "auto-create the shell, staff finishes it" precedent as DR-108's customized-package creation. New `src/lib/provision-fleet-profiles-for-user.ts` (`provisionFleetProfilesForUser`) composes `auth` + `fleet` one level up from both modules -- same convention as `create-customized-package.ts`/`client-deletion.ts`/`fleet-availability.ts` -- and is called from both the "create user" and "edit user" call sites (the staff Server Actions in `admin/users/actions.ts`/`admin/users/[userId]/actions.ts` and the matching `POST`/`PATCH /api/v1/users` REST routes), right after `authService.createUser`/`updateUser` resolves. Confirmed via three clarifying questions before building, since none of `DriverProfile`/`GuideProfile`/`Vehicle`'s required fields (besides `userId`/`organizationId`) have any source data on a freshly created `User`: (1) **VEHICLE_OWNER** gets one placeholder `Vehicle` (`plateNumber`/`make`/`model`/`vehicleType` all `"PENDING"`, `seatCapacity: 1`) rather than nothing -- `Vehicle` isn't a 1:1 profile like the other two (`ownerId` is nullable, un-unique, an owner can hold zero or many), so the guard is "does this user already own *any* vehicle yet," not a unique-constraint check; (2) `DriverProfile.licenseNumber` (required, no nullable fallback offered) is filled with the same `"PENDING"` placeholder rather than a schema change -- no schema/permission/module-dependency change at all for this DR; (3) auto-provisioning fires on both initial creation *and* a later role edit (not creation-only), so adding DRIVER to an already-existing user's role set on the edit page also provisions the profile, gated on `input.roles` actually being present in that edit's payload (a profile-only edit that never touches roles is a no-op, matching `authService.updateUser`'s own "`roles: undefined` = leave as-is" semantics already established there). `DriverProfile`/`GuideProfile` are true 1:1s (`userId @unique` on both tables) -- skipped via two new manager-gated lookups, `fleetService.findDriverProfileByUserId`/`findVehiclesByOwnerId` (mirroring the pre-existing `findGuideProfileByUserId`), if one already exists, so a role removed then re-added later never throws a duplicate-key error or clobbers a profile staff have since filled in; the same existence guard on `Vehicle` (via the new `findVehiclesByOwnerId`) stops toggling VEHICLE_OWNER on/off/on from piling up placeholder vehicles. Deliberately best-effort: the whole call is wrapped in a bare try/catch (no logging, matching `itineraryService.createItinerary`'s own template-copy precedent) so a fleet-provisioning failure -- e.g. a customized permission matrix granting `admin.all` to a role `fleetService`'s own manager check doesn't recognize -- can never fail the user create/edit action itself; staff can always fall back to creating the profile by hand, exactly as before this feature existed. A successful auto-provision is audited (`fleet.profile_auto_provisioned`, listing which record types were created) for traceability. New `fleetRepository.findVehiclesByOwnerId` backs the new service method, same shape as the existing `findVehiclesByIds`. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6 |

| DR-139 | 2026-08-17 | **Live production incident, user-reported**: the SUPERADMIN's own `User.name` kept silently changing to whatever client name was most recently typed into a guest booking wizard (reported as "it changed to Hanna today"). Root-caused directly in the code, not guessed: `authService.resolveSession()` resolves *whatever session cookie the browser carries*, with no staff-vs-guest distinction, and none of the three guest booking entry points (`(guest)/book/[departureId]/actions.ts`'s `createGuestBookingAction`, `(guest)/book-package/[packageId]/actions.ts`'s `createGuestPackageBookingAction`, `(guest)/plan-my-trip/actions.ts`'s `createPlanMyTripRequestAction`) ever check who that resolved session actually belongs to before calling `authService.updateProfile(ctx, { name, phone })` with the wizard's contact-step input. A staff member (any role, not just SUPERADMIN) who opens one of these guest-facing pages in the same browser they're signed into `/staff` with — to test the flow, or to walk a client through it in person on their own machine — gets their own real account back as `ctx`; typing the client's name into that step then overwrites the staff member's own `User.name`/`phone`. Fixed by skipping the `updateProfile` call in all three actions whenever `isStaffRole(ctx.roles)` (`src/lib/rbac.ts`) is true — explicit user direction to cover "any other logged in session," not just SUPERADMIN. The booking itself still proceeds under that same `ctx` afterward, unchanged (a staff member who does this ends up as the booking's own `touristUserId` — a separate, narrower oddity than the reported name-corruption bug, out of scope for this fix and not otherwise reported as a problem). New `tests/guest-actions-staff-guard.test.ts` (mocks `@modules/auth`/`next/headers`/`@modules/booking`, same `vi.mock`/`vi.hoisted` pattern as `tests/staff-guard.test.ts`) covers all three actions: a staff-role session skips the profile write, a real `TOURIST` session still gets it. No schema/permission/module-dependency change — the fix is a three-line guard per action plus one shared import. | V1, V8 |

| DR-140 | 2026-08-17 | **Explicit user request, follow-up to DR-139**: a guest booking wizard now rejects a contact email that already belongs to a real staff account (any non-`TOURIST` role) rather than silently accepting it. Confirmed via three clarifying questions first: (1) scope is staff accounts only, not every existing `User` row — a returning guest re-using their own email is expected and must keep working; (2) the check applies everywhere a guest actually types a real email today — `(guest)/plan-my-trip/actions.ts`'s `createPlanMyTripRequestAction` (the wizard's own contact-email field) and `(guest)/booking/[bookingId]/travelers/new/actions.ts`'s `addTravelerAction` (the tour lead's email, the only traveler role this form even collects one for) — `(guest)/book/[departureId]` and `(guest)/book-package/[packageId]`'s own Server Actions collect no email at all, so they needed no change; (3) a match hard-rejects with a clear error rather than silently proceeding or merging accounts. Both call sites resolve `authService.getUserByEmail(email)` (a pre-existing no-ctx, "caller already gates" lookup, unchanged) and check `isStaffRole(user.roles)` (`src/lib/rbac.ts`). `plan-my-trip`'s action returns `{ error }` through its existing `useActionState` convention (plain English string, matching that action's own pre-existing un-i18n'd error strings); `travelers/new`'s action has no such state today, so it gains the app's standard `redirect(...?error=email_in_use)` convention (same shape as the Passport step's `?error=missing_file`), with the page reading the new `searchParams.error` and rendering `TravelersPage.emailInUse` (added EN+FR, unlike `plan-my-trip`'s raw string) via the existing `Alert` component. New `tests/guest-actions-staff-guard.test.ts` cases (extending the DR-139 file, same mocking shape) cover: a staff email is rejected without creating a booking/traveler, and a real guest's own (unmatched) email still proceeds normally in both flows. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V8 |

| DR-141 | 2026-08-17 | **Explicit user request**: a staff account can now be permanently, irreversibly Deleted, distinct from the existing reversible Deactivate (DR-026). New `User.deletedPermanently` (`Boolean @default(false)`, additive) distinguishes the two on the shared `deletedAt` column both already write to — a Deactivated account has `deletedAt` set and `deletedPermanently: false` (unchanged from before); a Deleted account has both set, and `authService.reactivateUser` now throws `Errors.conflict` outright once `deletedPermanently` is true, rather than silently succeeding. New `authService.deleteUser` (SUPERADMIN-only via `isSuperAdmin`, same "route passes via the DB-editable `admin.all` permission, service still narrows with a hardcoded role check" layering as `isFleetDeleter`/`isBookingDeleter`/`isCountryRegulationWriter`/`isFinanceConfigWriter` — permanent user deletion is deliberately not something a customized permission matrix can grant to `PLATFORM_ADMIN` or any other role) blocks self-delete (same as `deactivateUser`) and re-deleting an already-deleted user, backed by new `authRepository.permanentlyDeleteUser`; audited as `auth.user_deleted`. Building this surfaced a real gap in `reactivateUser`'s existing lookup: `authRepository.findUserById` has always excluded any `deletedAt`-set row (by design, for every other caller), which meant `reactivateUser` calling it could never actually resolve the very Deactivated account it exists to restore — worked before only because nothing had previously tried to reactivate a manually-Deactivated (rather than merely dormant) user. Fixed with a new `authRepository.findUserByIdIncludingDeleted`, used by both `reactivateUser` and the new `deleteUser`; every other existing caller of `findUserById` is untouched. `reactivateUser` itself is also broadened (still `admin.all`, no SUPERADMIN narrowing — restoring is not itself destructive) to clear a plain Deactivate in addition to its original DR-084 dormancy-only case, since both are just "the account isn't active" states a caller wants undone in one action. `/staff/admin/users` gains a fourth `StatusFilter`/badge state ("Deleted", danger tone, wins over Deactivated wins over Inactive) and a SUPERADMIN-only "Delete" row action (confirm dialog, distinct copy from Deactivate's) alongside the existing Deactivate/Reactivate actions — Edit and Deactivate are hidden once `deletedPermanently` is true, Reactivate is hidden for the same reason (nothing left to undo). `isSuperAdmin` (already existed in `auth/domain.ts`, used internally by `deleteClient`/the permission-matrix routes) is now also exported through `auth/index.ts` for this UI check. New `tests/auth-user-delete.test.ts` (real-DB, hand-built `AuthContext`, same shape as `tests/auth-find-or-create-tourist.test.ts`) covers: SUPERADMIN deletes successfully; a PLATFORM_ADMIN holding bare `admin.all` cannot; self-delete is blocked; deleting an unknown or already-deleted user throws; `reactivateUser` still undoes a plain Deactivate; `reactivateUser` refuses once `deletedPermanently` is true. **Schema change (one additive, defaulted-`false` column on `User`, no destructive step) applied by hand to the shared Neon DB** — verified via `psql` that all 698 pre-existing rows read `false`. No permission-matrix or module-dependency change. | V1, V6, V8 |

| DR-142 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request, follow-up to DR-141; batches a second, unrelated small UI change, same "batch smaller changes into one entry" precedent as DR-086**: (1) a permanently Deleted staff account (DR-141) is no longer listed on `/staff/admin/users` at all — `authRepository.listStaff`'s query now excludes `deletedPermanently: true` at the DB level (previously it was returned like any other row and shown with a "Deleted" status/badge/filter option). The now-unreachable fourth `StatusFilter` state, its `<option>`, its badge branch, and the `deletedPermanently` guards on the Edit/Deactivate/Reactivate/Delete row actions (dead once such a row can never appear) were all removed from the page, along with the now-orphaned `deleted` EN/FR message key — Deactivated accounts are untouched, still listed and manageable exactly as before. A deleted account's audit trail (`auth.user_deleted`) is unaffected, still in `audit_logs`. (2) `/staff/sites`: page size raised from 10 to 15, and a Province filter `<Select>` added alongside the existing Search + Country filter (`listProvinces`, same "derive distinct values from the current dataset" pattern as the page's own `listCountries`) — no schema/permission change, `Site.province` already existed (DR-086). No schema/permission/module-dependency change for either part. | V1, V6, V8 |
| DR-143 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request**: removed the "Valid to" column from `/staff/settings/platform-rate`'s rate table — purely cosmetic, since `PlatformRate.validTo` was never actually settable (no create/edit path has ever written to it; every row's value is `null`, so the column only ever rendered "—"). `PlatformRate.validTo` itself is untouched in the schema (still read, as a no-op `OR: [{validTo: null}, ...]` clause, by `src/lib/platform-rate.ts`'s effective-rate resolution) — this is a display-only change, not a data model or business-rule change. The now-unused `StaffPlatformRate.validTo` EN/FR message key was removed alongside it. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6 |
| DR-145 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request, confirmed via three clarifying questions first**: a combo package/booking (its Day Template's hotels spanning 2+ countries) is now taxed per country instead of at a single flat rate. Scope, confirmed with the user: (1) tax only — Staff/Transport/Admin Cost/Immigration/Add-on rates keep resolving from the package's single primary `country` exactly as before, since Accommodation/Restaurant/Activity costs were already location-correct (`HotelRate`/`RestaurantRate`/`ActivityFee` resolve by the specific hotel/restaurant/activity id, DR-131, never by the package's `country`) and re-deriving those four buckets per-day would need new per-day country data that doesn't exist yet; (2) attribution is derived, not staff-typed — each Day Template night's country comes from its already-assigned hotel's own `HotelRate.country` (the ground truth for "where did this night actually happen," unlike `TourPackage.country`/`countries[]`, which are staff-typed and, per DR-114, never read by tax/rate resolution), weighted by night count; (3) applies to both standard packages and TAILOR_MADE bookings. New pure `finance/domain.ts`'s `blendedTaxRateBp` (weighted average, rounded to the nearest bp) backs a new async `computeBlendedTaxRate` (`finance/service.ts`): groups a Day Template's nights by each assigned hotel's `HotelRate.country`, resolves each distinct country's own effective tax rate, and blends — collapsing to a single plain `getEffectiveTaxRate` lookup (byte-identical to pre-existing behavior) for a single-country package or one with no hotel-tagged Day Template yet. `financeService.saveCostBreakdown` now calls this instead of resolving `pkg.country`'s tax rate directly — the result still flows through the unchanged DR-134 snapshot mechanism (`TourPackage.priceTaxRateBp`), so no schema change was needed; a blended rate is just a different number in the same existing column. Exposed cross-module as `financeService.resolveEffectiveTaxRateBp` (no-ctx, same "rate tables are platform-wide reference data" convention as `resolveRatesForCost`/`getEffectiveTaxRate` themselves) for **new `invoicing` → `finance` module dependency** (confirmed acyclic — `finance` only imports `{auth, catalog, booking, itinerary}`, never `invoicing`): `invoicingService.getOrCreateInvoiceForBooking`'s live tax-resolution branch (hit by a TAILOR_MADE booking, or a PREDEFINED_PACKAGE departure with its own manual `priceOverrideMinor`/pre-DR-134 booking) now blends using the linked `Booking.customizedPackageId`'s Day Template when one exists, rather than taxing the whole trip at just the booking's single `customCountry`/departure-package country. `financeService.saveBookingCostBreakdown` (`BookingCostBreakdown`, DR-092) is untouched — it never computed/snapshotted a tax rate itself; TAILOR_MADE tax has always resolved live at invoicing time, which is exactly where this change plugs in. New unit tests in `tests/finance.domain.test.ts` cover `blendedTaxRateBp`'s single-country passthrough, even/skewed weighting, and rounding. No schema change; no permission change. | V1, V6, V9 |

| DR-144 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request, reverses DR-104's original design** ("Coupons must have a delete not deactivate and update") — confirmed via two clarifying questions first, since a hard delete directly conflicted with DR-104's own documented reasoning for soft-deactivate-only (`CouponRedemption` has a real FK to `Coupon`, needed for the redemption cap count, that a hard delete would either be blocked by or wrongly cascade-destroy). User's answers: delete must always work, cascading away that coupon's redemption/usage history too (an explicit, accepted trade-off — loses the per-invoice audit trail DR-104 relied on; `settings.coupon_deleted`'s own audit entry, logging the code and how many redemptions it had, is what's left as the record); and Deactivate is removed entirely, Delete is the only destructive action from here on. Implementation: `CouponRedemption.coupon`'s relation gains `onDelete: Cascade` (`prisma/schema.prisma`); `Coupon.deactivatedAt` is dropped outright (destructive column, confirmed via `psql` that 0 of the 10 existing coupons had it set, so nothing meaningful was lost) rather than kept as dead code, since Deactivate itself is gone — a deleted coupon's row is gone outright, so `validateCoupon`'s pre-existing `NOT_FOUND` reason already covers "this code no longer works" with no separate state needed; the `INACTIVE` member is removed from `CouponUnavailableReason` (`src/lib/coupons.ts`) along with its check, and from `invoicing/service.ts`'s `couponErrorToApiError` switch. New `settingsService.deleteCoupon` (SUPERADMIN-only via `requireSettingsWriter`, same gate `createCoupon`/the old `deactivateCoupon` already used) replaces `deactivateCoupon`; new `settingsService.updateCoupon` is genuinely new (Coupons had no update path before at all) — full replace of `discountBp`/`maxRedemptions`/`expiresAt` (code and id stay immutable) via a reused `CreateCouponInput` schema aliased as `UpdateCouponInput`, same "reuse the create schema, blank clears the field" precedent as `financeService`'s `updateXRate` family (DR-136). New route `PATCH`/`DELETE /api/v1/settings/coupons/[id]` replaces the old `POST .../[id]/deactivate` (removed). `/staff/settings/coupons` gains an Edit disclosure (discountPercent/maxRedemptions/expiresAt, same local dependency-free `<details>`-based `EditDisclosure` convention as `finance/rates/page.tsx` — can't be imported from there directly, a `page.tsx` may only export `default` plus Next's well-known names) alongside a real Delete button (distinct confirm copy from the old Deactivate's). **Schema change (drops `Coupon.deactivatedAt`, changes `CouponRedemption.coupon`'s FK to `ON DELETE CASCADE`) applied by hand to the shared Neon DB** — verified via `psql` that the column is gone and the FK's `confdeltype` is now `c` (cascade). No permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6, V8 |

| DR-146 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request**: Tax Rates and Platform Rate can now be updated in place, not just added-as-a-new-row/deleted — same convention as `financeService`'s `updateXRate` family (DR-136) and Coupon's own Update (DR-144). New `UpdateTaxRateInput`/`UpdatePlatformRateInput` (both alias their respective `CreateXInput` schema, full replace of every editable field except id and the never-surfaced effective-dating fields) back new `settingsService.updateTaxRate`/`updatePlatformRate` (SUPERADMIN-only via the existing `requireSettingsWriter` gate) and a new `PATCH /api/v1/settings/tax-rates/[id]` / `platform-rates/[id]`. Since a package/booking's stored price/tax snapshot (DR-134/DR-145) is only refreshed by re-saving its own cost breakdown, both update paths also call `financeService.reapplyRatesToAllCostBreakdowns` (DR-136's own sweep, reused as-is) so a changed tax or platform rate doesn't leave every existing package/tailor-made-booking price stale — surfaced to staff as the same `?reapplied=1&packagesUpdated=&packagesSkipped=&bookingsUpdated=&bookingsSkipped=` banner convention DR-136 introduced. This is a **new `settings` → `finance` module dependency** (confirmed acyclic: `finance` only imports `{auth, catalog, booking, itinerary}`, never `settings`). `/staff/settings/tax-rates` and `/staff/settings/platform-rate` each gain a per-row Edit disclosure, same local dependency-free `<details>`-based `EditDisclosure` convention as `finance/rates/page.tsx`/`coupons/page.tsx` (can't be imported from there directly — a `page.tsx` may only export `default` plus Next's well-known names). Batches one small unrelated cosmetic fix, same "batch smaller changes into one entry" precedent as DR-086/142: the "Valid to" column is also removed from the Tax Rates table — `TaxRate.validTo`, like `PlatformRate.validTo` before it (DR-143), has never been settable by any create/edit path (no form anywhere exposes it), so the column only ever rendered "—"; the schema field and its no-op read in `src/lib/tax.ts`'s effective-rate resolution are untouched. New `tests/api/settings.api.test.ts` cases cover: a SUPERADMIN updates a tax/platform rate in place (200, values persisted), and updating an unknown id 404s, for both routes. No schema change; no permission change. | V1, V6, V9 |

| DR-147 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request**: removes `PHOTOGRAPHER`/`VIDEOGRAPHER` from `StaffRateRole` and drops `photographerDays`/`videographerDays` from both `PackageCostBreakdown` and `BookingCostBreakdown` — these duplicated the existing guest-facing `AddonService` `PHOTOGRAPHY`/`VIDEOGRAPHY` purchase (priced via `AddonRate`, DR-128) as a second, staff-typed "base inclusion" concept on the operational Staff cost-plus bucket; going forward a photographer/videographer is priced exactly one way, as an add-on purchased at booking time. `CreateStaffRateInput`'s `role` enum narrows to `DRIVER`/`GUIDE`; `resolveRatesForCost`/`computeCostBuckets` (`finance/service.ts`/`domain.ts`) no longer resolve or sum a photographer/videographer rate at all; both cost-breakdown forms (`packages/[packageId]/cost-breakdown` and `bookings/[bookingId]/cost-breakdown`) drop the two input fields; the Operational Rates page's Staff Rate role `<Select>` drops the two now-invalid options; the four now-orphaned EN/FR message keys (`photographerDays`/`videographerDays`/`rolePhotographer`/`roleVideographer`) are removed. `tests/finance.domain.test.ts`'s `CostInputs` fixture drops the two removed fields; `scripts/backfill-price-tax-fee.ts` (a DR-134 one-off migration script) drops the two removed fields from its `SaveCostBreakdownInput` construction to keep compiling. **Schema change (drops two `StaffRateRole` enum values + two `Int` columns) applied to the shared Neon DB** — verified via `psql` (2026-08-19) that the live `StaffRateRole` enum now has only `DRIVER`/`GUIDE`, neither cost-breakdown table has a `photographerDays`/`videographerDays` column, and the two blocking Namibia `PHOTOGRAPHER`/`VIDEOGRAPHER` `StaffRate` rows are gone. Separately noted, out of this change's scope: zero `AddonRate` rows exist yet for `PHOTOGRAPHY`/`VIDEOGRAPHY` in any country, so per DR-128's "hide an add-on with no rate configured for the booking's country" rule, both add-ons are currently invisible to every guest everywhere until staff configure at least one on `/staff/finance/rates`. No permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6, V9 |
| DR-148 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request**: SUPERADMIN can now genuinely (hard) delete an individual guest review (`Review`, plus its `ReviewSubjectRating` rows via schema cascade) from `/staff/ratings`'s "Individual reviews" table — `Review` has no soft-delete column and none was added; a deleted row is just gone, with `settings.coupon_deleted`-style precedent for the audit trail being the record instead. New `rating.delete` permission (declared in the `Permission` union/`ALL_PERMISSIONS`, deliberately never seeded to any role in `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS`) plus a new `isRatingDeleter` boolean helper (`ratings/domain.ts`, `roles.includes('SUPERADMIN')`) — same two-layer "route passes via the DB-editable permission matrix, the service's hardcoded role check is the real gate" convention as `isBookingDeleter`/`isFleetDeleter`/`isCountryRegulationWriter`/`isFinanceConfigWriter`/`requireSettingsWriter`. New `ratingsRepository.deleteReview` (genuine `tx.review.delete`, scoped through `withOrg` like every other query in this module) backs `ratingsService.deleteReview`, which re-checks `isRatingDeleter` beneath `assertCan(ctx, 'rating.delete')`, then recomputes every driver/guide aggregate the deleted review had contributed to (via its own pre-delete `subjectRatings` snapshot) plus the org-wide aggregate — reusing the exact same `recomputeDriverAggregate`/`recomputeGuideAggregate`/`recomputeOrganizationAggregate` calls `submitRating` already uses, so a deleted review's score never lingers in an average — and audits `rating.review_deleted`. New `DELETE /api/v1/ratings/[reviewId]` route (`withAuth('rating.delete', ...)`) and a matching `deleteReviewAction` Server Action power a per-row Delete button on the staff page, gated client-side on `ctx.roles.includes('SUPERADMIN')` and using the standard `SubmitButton`/`confirmMessage` destructive-action pattern (DR-086/109). Explicitly confirmed via the schema, not guessed: `Review.ratingCodeId` cascades *from* `Review` to `RatingCode`, not the reverse, so the review's own `RatingCode` row survives deletion, still marked used (`usedAt` unchanged) — a deleted review does not free up a fresh rating attempt on that booking. New `tests/api/ratings.delete.test.ts` covers the role gate (a plain `TOUR_OPERATOR`, who holds `rating.read`/`rating.issue` but not `rating.delete`, is forbidden), cross-tenant anti-BOLA (a different org's `SUPERADMIN` gets 404, not 403, on org A's review id — the existing "404 not 403" convention), and the real delete + cascade + aggregate-recompute path, including the surviving-`RatingCode` assertion above. No schema/module-dependency change. | V1, V6, V8 |

| DR-149 | 2026-08-18 | **User-reported gap**: a vehicle/driver/guide could stay marked BOOKED after its departure no longer had any active booking, in two cases the DR-082 fleet-availability sync hook never actually covered. (1) `bookingService.deleteBooking` (DR-058, SUPERADMIN-only hard delete) never called `syncFleetAvailabilityForDeparture` at all, unlike confirm/cancel/refund — fixed at both call sites (the staff `deleteBookingAction` in `staff/(dashboard)/bookings/[bookingId]/actions.ts` and `DELETE /api/v1/bookings/[bookingId]/route.ts`), each now reading the booking's `departureId`/`organizationId` via `bookingService.getById` *before* deleting (the row is invisible to every other read path in the module immediately after `deleteBooking` runs) and syncing afterward. (2) A real latent bug meant `bookingRepository.hasActiveBookingForDeparture` — the query the sync hook itself uses to decide "is this departure currently booked" — filtered only on `status: { in: ['CONFIRMED', 'IN_PROGRESS'] }` with no `deletedAt: null` guard; since `bookingRepository.softDelete` only ever sets `deletedAt` and never touches `status`, a soft-deleted CONFIRMED/IN_PROGRESS booking still matched as "active" even on a call site that DID already sync (e.g. if a booking were deleted via some other path). Fixed by adding `deletedAt: null` to that query. `COMPLETED`/`CANCELLED`/`REFUNDED` transitions (cancel/refund actions, and the lifecycle sweep's own COMPLETED transition, DR-094) were never affected by either bug — both were already excluded by the existing status filter, and both already had a working sync call site — so this closes exactly the one missing call site (delete) and the one latent correctness gap (soft-delete visibility), not a new mechanism. New one-off `scripts/backfill-fleet-availability.ts` (`npm run fleet-availability:backfill`) iterates every organization and re-runs `syncFleetAvailabilityForDeparture` for every departure that has ever had an `Assignment`, to correct any vehicle/driver/guide already left stale by these two gaps on pre-existing bookings — safe to re-run any number of times, since the sync helper always recomputes fresh from current state rather than applying a delta. New test case in `tests/fleet-availability-sync.test.ts` (soft-delete a CONFIRMED booking mid-BOOKED, confirm the resync frees it back to AVAILABLE). No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6 |
| DR-150 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request**: the staff Map tab (DR-089) now shows the WHOLE tour's circuit — every day's stops plotted on one map, each day in its own color — and downloads as a single PDF for the entire itinerary, replacing the old one-day-at-a-time map + per-day PDF entirely. New shared, framework-free `src/lib/circuit-colors.ts` (`circuitColorForDayIndex`, cycling a 12-color qualitative palette) is the one source of truth for a day's color, consumed identically by the client Google Maps JS widget (CSS hex) and the server Static Maps image + PDF (Google's `0xRRGGBB` syntax) so the interactive view, the downloaded PDF's map image, and its stop-list color swatches always agree. `itinerary/gateway.ts`'s `StaticMapsGateway.renderMap` (one color, one day) is replaced by `renderCircuitMap` (`RenderCircuitMapRequest.days: CircuitMapDay[]`), emitting one Static Maps `markers=color:0x...|...`/`path=color:0x...|weight:3|...` pair per day — Google's Static Maps API already natively supports this, so it's a wider query string, not a different endpoint. Flagged and handled, not previously documented anywhere in this codebase: Google's ~8192-character URL limit on a Static Maps request, realistically reachable by a long circuit (many days × several stops/day) once markers and paths are both emitted per day — `renderCircuitMap` degrades in stages (charter rule 8): drop per-stop markers first (the colored path alone still conveys the circuit), then thin each day's path points (keeping first/last, roughly halving per pass, capped at 5 passes) if still too long, rather than ever sending a URL `fetch()` would reject. `itinerary/map-pdf.tsx`'s `renderDayMapPdf` (one day, one image, one stop list) is replaced by `renderItineraryMapPdf` (one combined circuit image, then every day's stop list with a color-swatch heading), mirroring `itinerary-summary-pdf.tsx`'s (DR-137) single-`Document`/day-block/`wrap={false}` shape rather than one page per day. `itineraryService.streamDayMapPdf` is replaced by `streamItineraryMapPdf` (resolves the whole itinerary + every day via the existing `getOwnedItinerary`/`buildDayStops`, both already shared with `resolveMapOverview` — no new query logic needed there), audited as `itinerary_map.downloaded` (replacing `itinerary_day_map.downloaded`); the now-single-caller `getOwnedDay` anti-BOLA helper is deleted as dead code. Deliberately ungated by `Itinerary.status`, unlike `streamItinerarySummaryPdf`'s APPROVED-only gate — this stays staff's own working tool for checking geocoding/circuit sanity while an itinerary is still being built, not a client-facing final document (an explicit choice, not an oversight). Route `GET /api/v1/itineraries/{itineraryId}/days/{dayId}/map-pdf` is replaced by `GET /api/v1/itineraries/{itineraryId}/map-pdf` (same thin-pass-through convention). `src/components/ui/ItineraryDayMap.tsx` is replaced by `ItineraryCircuitMap.tsx`: takes every day's stops grouped with a color instead of one day's flat list, draws one colored `Polyline` + colored circle `Marker`s (a `SymbolPath.CIRCLE` icon with `fillColor`, not Google's fixed named-color pin images, since it needs to accept any hex from the shared palette) per day, and fits the viewport to every geocoded point across every day (`LatLngBounds`/`fitBounds`) instead of centering on day 1's first stop — the old per-day version could get away with a fixed center, a multi-day circuit spanning real distance can't. The hand-written `google-maps-types.ts` shim gains `LatLngBounds`/`fitBounds`/`Marker.icon`/`SymbolPath.CIRCLE`, and `Map`'s constructor `center`/`zoom` become optional (fitBounds sets the viewport instead) — additive, doesn't affect `MapLocationPicker.tsx`'s existing always-supplies-both usage. `/staff/map`'s page collapses its per-day map+download-button loop into one `ItineraryCircuitMap` + one whole-itinerary "Download PDF" button + a color-keyed day legend below the map. `tests/api/itinerary.security.test.ts`'s day-map-pdf anti-BOLA block is updated to the itinerary-level route (same 409/404/403 coverage: no geocoded stops anywhere, cross-itinerary 404-not-403, TOURIST 403). No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6, V8 |

| DR-151 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request**: SUPERADMIN can now genuinely (hard) delete an individual visa application from `/staff/visa-queue`'s main table, and deleting a booking now automatically removes any visa application(s) belonging to its travelers, closing the same regression class DR-059 already fixed for Itinerary (a `VisaApplication` left pointing at a soft-deleted `Booking`'s `Traveler` would keep showing on the queue as if nothing had happened, since the visa module had no concept of "booking deleted"). `VisaApplication` has no soft-delete column, so this is a genuine `tx.visaApplication.delete`, same "no column exists, a deleted row is just gone, the audit log is the record" precedent as DR-148's `Review` delete.
