New `visaRepository.deleteById`/`deleteManyByTravelerIds` (genuine hard deletes, scoped through `withOrg`, returning the raw pre-delete row(s) for the audit snapshot) back two new `visaService` methods: `deleteApplication` (the manual staff delete button, gated `assertCan(ctx, 'visa.delete')` + `isVisaDeleter`, audits `visa.application_deleted`) and `deleteForBooking` (the cascade, gated `visa.process` since it only ever runs as an internal cleanup step riding along on an actor who has already passed `booking.delete`'s own SUPERADMIN-only gate — not a second independently-reachable delete surface). `deleteForBooking` is deliberately NOT called from `bookingService.deleteBooking` itself — the visa module already depends on booking (`findTraveler`/`contactTraveler` already import `bookingService`), so booking calling back into visa would be circular — instead it's orchestrated by the caller, mirroring `itineraryService.deleteForBooking`'s own DR-059 precedent exactly: added to both the staff `deleteBookingAction` Server Action and the `DELETE /api/v1/bookings/[bookingId]` route (the itinerary cleanup only lives in the former, a pre-existing inconsistency; the new visa cleanup was deliberately added to both, so the same booking-delete path can't leave a stale visa application depending on which of the two ways it was deleted). New `DELETE /api/v1/visa/[applicationId]` route (`withAuth('visa.delete', ...)`) and a matching `deleteApplicationAction` Server Action power a per-row Delete button on the staff page, gated client-side on `ctx.roles.includes('SUPERADMIN')`, using the standard `SubmitButton`/`confirmMessage` destructive-action pattern (DR-086/109). No blob-deletion attempted for a visa application's referenced decision `Document` — same accepted "orphaned blob" tradeoff every delete path in this app already carries (no blob-deletion capability exists anywhere yet). No schema/module-dependency change. | V1, V6, V8 |
| DR-152 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request**: the staff package-detail "download summary PDF" feature (DR-135) now produces two distinct documents instead of one — a staff version (unchanged: full cost breakdown, per-day accommodation rates) and a new client-facing version (same day-by-day itinerary table, but no internal cost buckets at all, only the participant count and the one total a guest is actually charged) — plus both downloads' filenames now carry the package's own name and reference instead of a fixed literal. `finance/package-summary-pdf.tsx`'s header/itinerary-table/footer chrome is factored into three shared components (`DocumentHeader`/`ItineraryTable`/`DocumentFooter`) so the two documents can never visually drift apart outside the pricing section, which is where they differ; a new `ClientPackageSummaryPdfInput` (locale/currency/title/packageReference/referenceGroupSize/priceMinor/days — no cost-bucket fields at all) backs a new `renderClientPackageSummaryPdf`. New `financeService.generateClientPackageSummaryPdf` (same `catalog.write` gate as the existing `generatePackageSummaryPdf` — still staff-triggered, there's no guest-facing download route; this produces a document meant to be forwarded to a guest, not fetched by one) is deliberately more permissive than the staff version's precondition: it only requires `pkg.priceMinor` and the breakdown's `referenceGroupSize`, not every `computed*Minor` bucket, and skips the per-day hotel-rate resolution entirely (the client document never shows a rate) — so a package whose breakdown predates the current pricing model can still produce a client-facing document as long as it has an actual price. New route `GET /api/v1/catalog/packages/[packageId]/client-summary-pdf` mirrors the existing `summary-pdf` route's thin pass-through shape; the package detail page gains a second EN/FR download row labeled distinctly ("staff summary, with costs" vs. "client summary, itinerary only, no costs"). Filenames: this is the first dynamic `Content-Disposition` filename anywhere in this app (every PDF route before this, including this same one, shipped a fixed literal like `"package-summary.pdf"`) — both routes now build one from `slugify(title)` (DR-118's existing helper, already ASCII-only/URL-safe) plus `packageReference` (the human "PKG-00034" id shown everywhere else in the staff UI — chosen over the raw UUID, which would make an uglier, less meaningful filename) plus a `staff`/`client` tag and the locale, e.g. `sossusvlei-explorer-PKG-00034-staff-summary-en.pdf` — entirely ASCII by construction, so no RFC 5987 `filename*=` fallback form was needed. `financeService.generatePackageSummaryPdf`'s return shape gains a `filename` field (previously just `{ body, contentType }`); its own audit action name (`finance.package_summary_pdf_downloaded`) is unchanged for continuity with existing audit history, the new client variant gets its own distinct `finance.package_client_summary_pdf_downloaded`. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6, V9 |
| DR-153 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request**: removed the generic "Back" browser-history button (`back-button.tsx`, rendered on every staff dashboard page via `layout.tsx`) — with a page-specific "back to X" `BackLink` already present on many pages, the two were redundant on those pages, and the generic one's plain `router.back()` target (whatever page staff happened to arrive from) was less predictable than an explicit destination. `back-button.tsx` and its `NO_BACK_ROUTES` suppression list (added in a prior, undocumented commit to hide the button on top-level hub pages — a process gap this entry also closes) are deleted outright — the hub-page problem it solved no longer exists once the generic button itself is gone. Auditing every staff dashboard page first: 10 pages already had their own `BackLink` and needed no change; 10 more sit inside the Settings `SidebarShell` (`admin/clients`, `admin/permissions`, `admin/users`, `content`, `country-regulations`, `insights`, `profile`, `sites`, `sites/new`, `sites/[siteId]`) whose persistent sub-nav already gives equivalent (in fact broader) navigation, so removing the generic button leaves them with no functional gap. The remaining 9 leaf pages had no back-navigation at all other than the generic button and each got a real `BackLink` added to preserve it: `admin/users/[userId]` → `/staff/admin/users` (`StaffEditUser.backToUsers`), `country-regulations/new` and `country-regulations/[country]` (both branches) → `/staff/country-regulations` (`StaffCountryRegulations.backToCountryRegulations`), `hotels/[hotelId]`/`hotels/new` → `/staff/hotels` (`StaffHotels.backToHotels`), `restaurants/[restaurantId]`/`restaurants/new` → `/staff/restaurants` (`StaffRestaurants.backToRestaurants`), `itineraries/[itineraryId]` → `/staff/itineraries` (`StaffItineraryDetail.backToItineraries`), and `departures/[departureId]` → `/staff/packages/{departure.tourPackageId}` (`StaffDepartureDetail.backToPackage`, reusing the exact key/text already established by the package cost-breakdown page) — conditionally rendered, since a bespoke (`TAILOR_MADE`) departure has no `tourPackageId` and is instead reached from the booking/itinerary detail page, which already has its own navigation. All 6 new EN/FR message keys added in both `src/messages/en.json` and `src/messages/fr.json`. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V6 |
| DR-154 | 2026-08-18 | **Explicit user request** (walked through several rounds of clarifying questions on scope, confirmed at each fork rather than assumed): makes the visa workflow fully operational end-to-end from the guest side, and retires a duplicate/disconnected internal cost-plus bucket for visa handling. (1) **Retired `ImmigrationCostRate` entirely** — the 4-fee staff-only cost-plus bucket (`requiresVisa`/`immigrationCostRateId` on both `PackageCostBreakdown`/`BookingCostBreakdown`) had nothing to do with whether any guest actually bought the guest-facing "Visa Assistance" `AddonService` (priced via `AddonRate`, DR-128, already additive to the invoice total) — confirmed with the user this was true double-tracking, same fix DR-147 already applied to Photographer/Videographer. `ImmigrationCostRate` model, its Operational Rates card, its section on both cost-breakdown staff forms, its REST routes (`/api/v1/finance/rates/immigration-cost` + `[id]`), and every `requiresVisa`/`immigrationCostRateId`/`visaMinor` reference across `finance/{domain,repository,service,index}.ts` are removed outright; `computeCostBuckets`/`computeBaseCostMinor` now sum seven buckets, not eight. The one test relying on a differently-currencied `ImmigrationCostRate` to exercise the "resolved rates disagree in currency" 409 case (`tests/api/booking-cost-breakdown.api.test.ts`) now uses a second, EUR-priced `TransportRate` fixture for the same purpose. **Schema change (drops the `immigration_cost_rates` table + `requiresVisa`/`immigrationCostRateId` columns on both cost-breakdown tables) applied to the shared Neon DB** — verified via `psql` first that zero `PackageCostBreakdown`/`BookingCostBreakdown` rows had `requiresVisa = true` (the 2 `immigration_cost_rates` rows themselves were unused config, confirmed safe to lose), then applied via scoped raw SQL (`ALTER TABLE ... DROP COLUMN` × 2, `DROP TABLE`) inside one transaction rather than a blanket `db push` — a second, uncommitted, unrelated feature (a `WizardProgressEvent`/analytics table + `staff_roster.read` permission, also self-labeled "DR-154") was mid-flight in the same working tree at the time, so `db push`'s file-on-disk schema diff would have picked up its changes too. **DR number collision, not yet resolved**: that other feature must renumber to DR-155 (or later) once committed, since this entry landed on `main` first. While verifying this, also found and corrected two stale "not yet applied" claims elsewhere in this log/CLAUDE.md: DR-147's and DR-126's schema changes were, in fact, already live (see DR-147's row above, corrected in place; CLAUDE.md corrected for both). (2) **Guest self-service visa workflow** — previously a guest's only visibility into their own `VisaApplication` was a bare read-only status badge on the anonymous `/find-booking` lookup, with no way to see a rejection reason, fix and resubmit, or download an approved visa document from their own authenticated booking. New minimized `GuestVisaApplicationView` (`visa/domain.ts`, same BR-10-style minimization precedent as `FacilitatorVisaView`) backs three new guest-safe `visaService` methods — `getApplicationForGuest`, `resubmitApplicationForGuest`, `streamDocumentForGuest` — all deliberately without the staff `visa.process`/`documents.read` permission assertions (TOURIST holds neither), relying instead on the existing `findTraveler` → `bookingService.listTravelers` ownership check (`assertOwnedBooking`: 404 unless `booking.touristUserId === ctx.userId`) for anti-BOLA, the same "caller-context already gates it" convention `autoSubmitOnPassportUpload`/`getBookingForTraveler` already established. Confirmed with the user: the booking's tour lead (the only session that can ever reach these travelers at all, since a `Traveler` isn't its own `User` account) may act on behalf of *any* traveler on the booking, not just themselves — same precedent as the initial per-traveler passport-upload wizard. `documentsService` gains a matching `streamDocumentForOwner` (same body as `streamDocument`, minus the `documents.read` assertion). New guest page `/booking/[bookingId]/visa` (one row per traveler, status badge, rejection reason + re-upload-and-resubmit form when `REJECTED`, download link when `APPROVED`), a new Server Action `resubmitVisaAction` (same upload shape as the existing `uploadPassportAction`), and a new guest-only download route `GET /api/v1/bookings/[bookingId]/travelers/[travelerId]/visa/document/guest` (hand-written, not `withAuth`, since that forces a staff permission — resolves the session directly and delegates ownership + streaming to `streamDocumentForGuest`; the existing staff-only route at the parent path is untouched). The main booking status page (`/booking/[bookingId]`) gains a "Visa application" summary section linking to the new page, shown whenever `booking.requiresPassportUpload`. (3) **Notifications on decision** — new `VISA_APPROVED`/`VISA_REJECTED` `NotificationEvent`s (two distinct events per outcome, matching this module's existing `BOOKING_CONFIRMED`/`BOOKING_CANCELLED`-style convention rather than one event with an outcome flag), EN/FR templates mirroring `VISA_CONTACT_TRAVELER`/`VISA_MISSING_DOCUMENTS`; `visaService.decideApplication` now resolves the booking's tour lead via `bookingService.getBookingForTraveler` and calls `notificationsService.notify` right after persisting the decision, same shape/placement as `contactTraveler`'s own call. A guest-initiated resubmit does not itself notify staff — the status flip back to `SUBMITTED` already surfaces live in the facilitator queue's own counts, and the audit log is the record, same as every other action in this module. (4) **Staff decide/upload UI** — closes the gap the visa-queue page's own header comment used to document ("decide/resubmit/upload stay API-only, not a new decision-making UI"): new `decideApplicationAction`/`uploadVisaDocumentAction` Server Actions (mirroring the page's existing `contactTravelerAction` pattern exactly) back a new outcome-select-plus-reason form (shown only while `SUBMITTED`) and a document-upload form in the existing actions cell of `/staff/visa-queue`'s main table — a facilitator can now approve/reject an application and upload its granted document without a raw API call. New EN/FR message keys across `StaffVisaQueue`, `BookingHome`, and `BookingVisaPage`; removed keys from `StaffCostBreakdown`/`StaffFinanceRates`/`StaffPackageCostBreakdown`. No new permission — every new capability rides on an existing permission (`visa.process`) or an existing ownership check, not a new grant. | V1, V6, V8, V9 |

discovering a concurrent session had already committed a real DR-154 (the visa-workflow entry directly above) to `main` mid-session — see that row's own note flagging this exact collision. **Live mechanism**: client polling every 30s against `GET /api/v1/insights` (now accepting an optional `?from=&to=` `DateRange`, unbounded/all-time when both are omitted, matching the prior snapshot behavior), backed by a 30s Upstash Redis cache (new `src/lib/cache.ts`, same env-gated graceful-degradation shape as `rate-limit.ts`) keyed on `(organizationId, range)` so concurrent viewers cost one recompute per window, not one each — chosen over a real push channel (SSE/WebSocket) specifically to avoid new infrastructure this Vercel-serverless stack doesn't hold well. **Access restricted** beyond the existing DB-editable `insights.read` to SUPERADMIN/TOUR_OPERATOR/PLATFORM_ADMIN via a new hardcoded `isInsightsViewer` check (`insights/domain.ts`), same layering as `isBookingDeleter`/`isFleetDeleter`/`isVisaDeleter` — the page redirects to `/staff/forbidden` on failure (matching `admin/permissions/page.tsx`'s own convention), the API route 403s via the same check inside the service. **New Staff-stats section** (headcount by role, active/deactivated/dormant, fleet availability) via a new `authService.getStaffRosterSummary`, gated by a brand-new `staff_roster.read` permission (seeded to PLATFORM_ADMIN + TOUR_OPERATOR) rather than the much broader `admin.all` — confirmed with the user via a clarifying question first, since TOUR_OPERATOR doesn't hold `admin.all` and granting it just for a headcount stat would also unlock user CRUD + the permissions matrix; `insightsService` degrades this section to zeros rather than failing the whole dashboard if the permission isn't yet seeded live in a given environment. **New Guest-stats section**: new-vs-returning guests (classified by each tourist's earliest-ever booking, checked against the full unfiltered history so it's correct even when that earliest booking falls outside the selected range), PREDEFINED_PACKAGE-vs-TAILOR_MADE origin split, a TAILOR_MADE-only booking-stage funnel (Awaiting Quotation → Quotation Sent → Confirmed-or-later, an honest current-pipeline distribution rather than a fabricated historical funnel, since no booking-status-transition-history table exists) + cancellation/refund rate, and a guest-geography breakdown that surfaces rather than papers over a real data gap: `Booking.countryOfResidence` only exists for TAILOR_MADE bookings, so PREDEFINED_PACKAGE bookings bucket under a new `GUEST_GEOGRAPHY_NOT_COLLECTED` constant. **New Finance-stats additions** (average booking value, tax/platform-fee collected, deposit-vs-full-paid split derived from each invoice's `payments[]` `PaymentKind` since `Invoice` has no such flag itself, total discount given + coupon-redemption count) all derive from `InvoiceView`/`PaymentView` fields `invoicingService.listAllForOrg` already returns to insights — no new `insights -> settings` dependency needed. **New infrastructure — plan-my-trip wizard-step-abandonment tracking**, confirmed via a further round of clarifying questions on session-identification mechanism, what to record, granularity, and retention: previously nothing was persisted until the wizard's final submit (steps 0-7 were pure client state). New `analytics` module (no table owned by `insights` itself, `auth`-shaped: domain/repository/service/index) owns a new RLS'd tenant table, `WizardProgressEvent`, storing only the highest step reached per session (never actual field values, never decreased by a "Back" click — a parameterized `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT ... DO UPDATE SET "highestStep" = GREATEST(...)` keeps this race-free), identified by a lightweight opaque cookie set client-side the moment the wizard loads rather than a real better-auth anonymous session/User row (a heavier privacy footprint explicitly rejected in favor of the lighter option). `analyticsService.recordWizardStep` is a public, no-`AuthContext` write mirroring `ratingsService.submitRating`'s exact shape (`getPrimaryOrgId()`, never a caller-supplied org), throttled by a new general-purpose `assertWriteNotRateLimited` (`src/lib/rate-limit.ts`, extending the existing failure-gated `assertLookupNotRateLimited` with a plain per-call-volume limit, since a tracking write has no pass/fail concept) and wrapped so any failure is silently swallowed, never affecting the wizard. Rows purge after 30 days via a new QStash job, `/api/jobs/purge-wizard-progress` — coded, added to `scripts/register-qstash-schedule.ts`'s list, and registered against the live deployment (confirmed via the script's own console output, which also caught DR-107's `sweep-fleet-cooldowns` still needing the same registration run). New **`insights -> analytics` module dependency** (confirmed acyclic — `analytics` has no module dependencies of its own). **Trend charts** (revenue per-currency, bookings, visa-application volume, new-guest signups) bucket already-fetched timestamped rows by day/week/month, granularity chosen from the selected range's span — no new queries or stored time-series table. **Visual design**, per explicit follow-up request ("very fancy... charts, circles, pies"): built via the `dataviz` skill's form-then-color procedure against this app's own "Horizon" tokens (not a generic palette) — circular ring meters for utilization/conversion percentages, donut charts for part-to-whole breakdowns with enough categories to earn one, split-meter bars (not 2-slice pies, per the skill's own guidance) for new-vs-returning/origin splits, horizontal funnel bars for both funnels, and line/area trend charts with hover crosshair+tooltip — all hand-rolled inline SVG/CSS, no new charting dependency (`@visx` stays scoped to the homepage map, unchanged). The categorical palette was run through the skill's `scripts/validate_palette.js` together with the user (`--mode light --surface "#F6EFE4"`) — the raw named Horizon tokens (including mist as a 5th slot) FAILED (gold too light/low-contrast; forest, navy.line, and mist all read as gray, below the chroma floor); fixed by stepping three of the four hue families to a passing chroma/lightness (forest → `#1B8F5A`, gold → `#B8860B`, navy/dusk-plum → `#8B3F82`, amber unchanged) and dropping mist entirely — `ALL CHECKS PASS` (two WARNs, both legal only because every chart already ships direct labels + a legend). This also surfaced a real bug: `DonutChart`'s fold-into-"Other" threshold was hardcoded at 6 (a leftover from the original 5-hue set), so a 5th/6th real category would have silently repeated a color instead of folding — fixed by deriving it from `CHART_CATEGORICAL.length`. This app has no dark theme anywhere; the charts don't attempt one. New EN/FR keys throughout `StaffInsights`. **Schema change (one new table, `wizard_progress_events`, purely additive, plus its RLS policy) applied by hand to the shared Neon DB, verified via `psql`** (RLS enabled+forced, `tenant_isolation` policy present); `db:seed` re-run afterward, confirmed via `psql` that `staff_roster.read` is now granted to both `PLATFORM_ADMIN` and `TOUR_OPERATOR`. **Same-DR follow-up**: explicit user request — "stats should start fresh with only current figures" — added `DASHBOARD_EPOCH` (the day this shipped) as a fixed floor no computation reaches earlier than, even under "All time" (`clampRangeToEpoch` + a one-time epoch filter on the three fetched event arrays in `insightsService`); a display floor for this one dashboard only, never touching the underlying rows elsewhere in the app. | V1, V5, V6, V8, V9 |
| DR-158 | 2026-08-19 | **Explicit user request**: guest `PackageCard` (`src/app/(guest)/package-card.tsx`, shared by the homepage and `/packages`) rendered a different height per package depending on how long its title/description happened to be, and whether it had any tags -- uneven card grids. `line-clamp-2`/`line-clamp-3` now cap the title/description from growing past 2/3 lines, paired with a `min-h` on each (a clamp alone only stops a long text from growing further -- a short one still collapses smaller without an explicit floor), and a fixed "Read more" line always follows the description (true of every package regardless of whether its own text was actually truncated, since the detail page always has more than the card) rather than depending on the reader to notice a cut sentence. The tags row stays conditional (a package may have none), but price/CTA is now pinned to the same bottom edge on every card via `mt-auto` inside a `flex h-full flex-col` layout, so a card with no tags doesn't end up shorter than one with several. No schema/permission/module-dependency change -- presentation only. | V1 |
| DR-157 | 2026-08-19 | **User-reported bug**: `/staff/insights`' revenue/finance figures ("outstanding," "average booking value," tax/platform-fee collected, deposit-vs-full-paid split, total discount given, revenue-by-country/-package, the revenue trend chart) kept counting an invoice's amounts forever, even after its parent `Booking` was deleted. Root cause: `invoicingService.listAllForOrg` (`src/modules/invoicing/repository.ts`) does a bare `tx.invoice.findMany` with no join back to `Booking` -- `Invoice` carries no `deletedAt` of its own, and a `Booking`'s soft-delete (DR-058) never cascades to its `Invoice` row -- so `insightsService.computeDashboardSummary`'s revenue-accumulation loop (`src/modules/insights/service.ts`) added every invoice's `succeededMinor` to the running totals unconditionally, before it ever looked up the invoice's booking; `computeFinanceExtras` received the same unfiltered `invoiceRows` array. The same dangling-reference-after-delete class of bug as DR-133/DR-149. Fixed by fetching `bookingService.list` (already `deletedAt: null`-scoped) unfiltered by `DASHBOARD_EPOCH` into a new `allBookings`, building an `activeBookingIds` set from it, and filtering `invoiceRows` against that set at fetch time, alongside the existing epoch filter -- every downstream consumer (`revenue`/`outstanding`/`revenueByCountry`/`revenueByPackage`, `computeFinanceExtras`, the revenue trend's `paidInvoicesInRange`) inherits the fix with no further change, matching this file's own "filter once, immediately after fetching" convention. `bookingById` (used for revenue-by-country/-package attribution) is now built from `allBookings` rather than the epoch-filtered `bookings`, so a booking that predates `DASHBOARD_EPOCH` but is still live is correctly distinguished from one that's genuinely deleted. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V8 |
| DR-156 | 2026-08-19 | **Explicit user request**: replaced the three site-wide `next/font/google` typefaces — the user found Fraunces/IBM Plex Sans/IBM Plex Mono "too generic" (a warm-editorial serif and the exact dev-tool sans/mono pairing a dozen other products already use). Reviewed via two rounds of live rendered specimens (a published Artifact per role, real app copy/colors, current fonts shown alongside each candidate as a control) rather than picked from names alone; the first pass (Piazzolla/Frank Ruhl Libre, Hanken Grotesk/Schibsted Grotesk, Space Mono/Fragment Mono) was rejected outright as still too warm-editorial, and a second pass pulled toward a rugged/expedition register instead. User picked: **Big Shoulders Stencil** (headlines/hero figures — a die-cut crate/signage stencil, weight 700/900 only), **Archivo** (body/UI, replacing IBM Plex Sans — an institutional grotesque built for wayfinding signage), and **Special Elite** (eyebrow labels + booking/rating reference codes, replacing IBM Plex Mono — a distressed field-dispatch typewriter face, one weight only). Since the headline face is now a display stencil sans, not a serif, the Tailwind `fontFamily.serif` key and `--font-serif` CSS variable are renamed to `display`/`--font-display` (`tailwind.config.ts`, `src/app/layout.tsx`) rather than kept under a now-inaccurate name; the 4 call sites using the `font-serif` utility (`globals.css`'s `h1`/`h2`/`h3` rule, the guest homepage's step-marker numerals, `TrustSummary`'s rating figure) are updated to `font-display`. No schema/permission/module-dependency change — typography only. **Follow-up, same DR-156** (a real live-production incident, user-reported: after two rounds of deploy fixes, headlines still rendered as a plain bold sans with no visible die-cut gaps at all): "Big Shoulders Stencil" and "Big Shoulders Stencil Display" turned out to be two *separate* published Google Fonts families, not one variable family exposing different names via an optical-size axis — `next/font/google`'s bundled `Big_Shoulders_Stencil` function only ever wraps the base (non-Display) family, which has no `Big_Shoulders_Stencil_Display` counterpart export at all. Two earlier fix attempts (requesting `axes: ['opsz']` with `weight: 'variable'`, then reverting to pinned `weight: ['700', '900']`) both still resolved to that same wrong base family — its file declares a `wght` axis of 100-900 (so neither attempt errored) but has zero named instances beyond its default and visibly renders with no stencil cuts, confirmed both by inspecting the actual served file with `fontTools` (no Bold/Black named instances, versus the real Display family's file, fetched directly from Google's own CSS2 endpoint for comparison, which has genuine Thin-through-Black named instances) and by a live screenshot showing an ordinary bold sans. Fixed by vendoring that real Display family's file (`src/app/fonts/big-shoulders-stencil-display.woff2`, Latin subset, OFL-licensed) and self-hosting it via `next/font/local` instead of `next/font/google` — the only reliable way to get this specific family, since it isn't in `next/font/google`'s bundled list at all. Archivo and Special Elite were unaffected throughout (both real `next/font/google` bundled families) — this was a single-font, single-file problem the whole time. **Second follow-up, same DR-156, the real root cause**: the vendored-Display-family fix above was still, itself, invisible in production — user-reported after three browsers/devices (desktop, Shields-disabled, mobile Chrome) all showed zero change. Root-caused with Playwright driven directly against the live production URL (no more guessing from screenshots): `getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue('--font-display')` (and `--font-sans`/`--font-mono`) returned the plain system fallback string, not the real font, on every property, and `document.fonts` showed the real "bigShouldersStencilDisplay" @font-face present but `status: "unloaded"` -- nothing had ever asked the browser to use it. Cause: `globals.css`'s own `:root { --font-sans: ...; --font-mono: ...; --font-display: ...; }` block (a pre-hydration fallback, present since long before this DR -- likely since the original DR-068 identity work) ties `next/font`'s generated `.__variable_xxx{--font-sans:"Archivo",...}` class rule on CSS specificity (both single-class-level, 0-1-0); the tie breaks toward whichever rule lands later in the compiled stylesheet, which was this `:root` block (`globals.css` is imported last in `layout.tsx`) -- so *every* element referencing `var(--font-sans)`/`var(--font-display)`/`var(--font-mono)`, on every page of the entire app, silently rendered the plain system fallback instead of whichever font was actually configured, almost certainly since before this DR entirely (Fraunces/IBM Plex Sans/Mono were likely never actually visible either -- just harder to notice than a stencil font's absence). Fixed by deleting the `:root` block outright: Next.js SSR means the real class is already in the very first byte of HTML, never added late by client JS, so the "pre-hydration" scenario it was guarding against doesn't exist, and Tailwind's own `fontFamily` config (`tailwind.config.ts`) already appends a generic fallback after each `var()` reference for the one scenario that's real (the font *file* failing to load). Re-verified against the live deployment with the same Playwright script: all three custom properties now resolve to their real font names, and every font-face's `document.fonts` status moves to `loading`/`loaded`. | V1 |
| DR-159 | 2026-08-19 | **Explicit user request, walked through the permission review tab-by-tab with the user before any code, per their own "ask questions, don't assume" instruction** — reverses DR-035's runtime, DB-backed permission-matrix editor. What each role grants is a hardcoded, in-code map again (`src/lib/rbac.ts`'s `ROLE_PERMISSIONS`, replacing `DEFAULT_PERMISSIONS`/the DB-backed `RolePermission` table) — `/staff/admin/permissions` (page + route + `authService.getPermissionMatrix`/`setRolePermission` + `authRepository`'s four `RolePermission`-backed methods) is deleted outright, along with `GET`/`PATCH /api/v1/permissions` and `SetRolePermissionInput` (openapi.yaml updated to match — the path and schema are removed, not just left stale). SUPERADMIN is unchanged: still the one hardcoded, unconditional wildcard, never consulting the map. Per the user's own explicit per-item decisions: PLATFORM_ADMIN is narrowed off `catalog.write`, `itinerary.approve`, `visa.process`, `documents.read`/`.write`, `invoice.read`, `payment.initiate`/`.resolve`, and `booking.cancel` (TOUR_OPERATOR is now the operational role for all of these); both PLATFORM_ADMIN and TOUR_OPERATOR lose `finance_config.*`/`platform_settings.*` entirely (Finance Settings — Tax Rates/Platform Rate/Coupons/Operational Rates — is now SUPERADMIN-only, view and write); TOUR_GUIDE loses `documents.read` (staff-side passport/visa-document viewing is now TOUR_OPERATOR/VISA_FACILITATOR-only); the Clients directory (`isClientDirectoryViewer`, `auth/domain.ts`) gains PLATFORM_ADMIN alongside SUPERADMIN/TOUR_OPERATOR. **Two menu items are gated by a brand-new mechanism, `STAFF_PAGE_ACCESS` (`rbac.ts`) plus `requireStaffRole`/`withRole` (`staff-guard.ts`/`route-guard.ts`, siblings of `requireStaffContext`/`withAuth`)** — a plain hardcoded role list, deliberately decoupled from any `Permission`, for a menu item whose old gating permission is also load-bearing for an unrelated internal composition that can't itself be narrowed: the general **Bookings** tab/list/detail pages (`booking.read` stays broadly granted — VISA_FACILITATOR's `findTraveler` and TOUR_GUIDE/DRIVER's own-assignment-scoped My Schedule view both still need it — but the general list/detail pages narrow to PLATFORM_ADMIN/TOUR_OPERATOR, plus VISA_FACILITATOR on the detail page specifically, since `/staff/visa-queue` links directly into it for the application they're processing) and the **Packages** tab/list/detail-view pages (`catalog.read` stays broadly granted for guest checkout and other roles' internal lookups; the tab itself narrows to PLATFORM_ADMIN/TOUR_OPERATOR). A third, narrower case: the "Confirm" booking action itself is TOUR_OPERATOR-only even though PLATFORM_ADMIN keeps the broader `booking.confirm` permission (which also gates Refund/Send Quotation/Convert-to-Itinerary/link Customized Package/the cost-breakdown editor, all kept for PLATFORM_ADMIN) — a new `isBookingConfirmer` (`booking/domain.ts`), same "hardcoded role check beneath the permission gate" layering as `isBookingDeleter`. Every new/changed denial redirects to `/staff/forbidden` or returns a structured `problem+json` 403 (never a raw 404 or crash) — confirmed as an explicit requirement mid-session; the Confirm action specifically also gets a same-page `?error=notAuthorized` Alert (new EN/FR `StaffBookingDetail.notAuthorized` key) since its button is additionally hidden from anyone but SUPERADMIN/TOUR_OPERATOR. **Schema change (drops the `role_permissions` table) made in `prisma/schema.prisma` and `prisma/seed.ts` (seeding removed)**, initially left NOT YET applied to the shared Neon DB pending explicit confirmation, same "explicit confirmation before a destructive change" convention as every other one in this project's history. **DR number note**: this entry is DR-159, not DR-156 — DR-156/157/158 (directly above) were already committed by concurrent sessions under those numbers by the time this work was ready to log, the same class of collision DR-154/155 hit before. No new module dependency; `RolePermission`'s removal is the only schema change. **Same-DR follow-up**: explicit user request ("do it") — the `role_permissions` table (108 pre-existing rows, no FK dependents) was dropped by hand against the shared Neon DB via `psql` using the `neondb_owner` credential, verified afterward (`to_regclass('public.role_permissions')` returns null). | V4, V5, V8 |
| DR-160 | 2026-08-20 | **User-reported bug**: the guest `/weather/[town]` 7-day forecast only ever rendered 5 days. Root cause: Google's `forecast.days:lookup` (DR-113) paginates independently of the `days` query param — `days` only bounds the total window (1-10) that exists to fetch, while `pageSize` (defaulting to 5 server-side when omitted) governs how many of those days come back in one response, with a `nextPageToken` required to fetch the rest. `GoogleWeatherGateway.getDailyForecast` (`src/modules/weather/gateway.ts`) set `days` but never set `pageSize` or followed `nextPageToken`, so it silently received page 1 (5 days) and stopped — a valid response shape, not an error, so nothing here or in `weatherService`'s existing gateway-failure degradation (`WeatherGatewayError` → `null`) ever caught it. Fixed by passing `pageSize: String(days)` alongside `days` on the same single request — since the app's own `FORECAST_DAYS` (`src/modules/weather/service.ts`) is comfortably under Google's 10-day/10-pageSize cap, one request is sufficient and no real pagination loop is needed. Batched in the same session: `FORECAST_DAYS` was briefly raised 7 → 9 (explicit user request, "add 2 more days"), then reverted back to 7 on explicit follow-up correction — the pageSize fix is the only lasting behavior change; the window itself stays at 7 days. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V4, V6, V8 |
| DR-161 | 2026-08-20 | **Explicit user request**: every downloadable PDF in the app (`finance/package-summary-pdf.tsx`, `itinerary/itinerary-summary-pdf.tsx`, `itinerary/map-pdf.tsx`) now embeds the same two body/label type roles the rest of the app uses — Archivo (role 2, body copy) and Special Elite (role 3, the `.eyebrow`/reference-code face) — instead of silently falling back to `@react-pdf/renderer`'s built-in Helvetica. Deliberately excludes role 1 (Big Shoulders Stencil Display): the user named only roles 2 and 3, and that headline stencil face is reserved for on-screen `h1`/`h2`/`h3`. New shared `src/lib/pdf-fonts.ts` (`registerPdfFonts`, `PDF_FONT_BODY`/`PDF_FONT_CODE`) is called once per render from all three files; each file's `page` style now sets `fontFamily: PDF_FONT_BODY`, and the one booking/package-reference line in each gets `PDF_FONT_CODE`. **A real fontkit bug found and worked around while building this**: Google's own CSS2-served Archivo file for a modern client is identical across every requested static weight (confirmed: same URL for wght 400/600/700) — i.e. still carrying composite/variation-derived glyph structure from Archivo's upstream conversion to a variable font — and embedding that file with the app's actual French cost-summary label set (not a short smoke-test string) crashed `@react-pdf`'s fontkit-based subsetter with `RangeError: Offset is outside the bounds of the DataView` in `TTFSubset._addGlyph`. fontkit is already pinned at its latest release (2.0.4); no newer version fixes it. Fixed by building genuinely static font files ourselves instead of trusting Google's per-request serving: fetched Archivo's real upstream variable source (`github.com/google/fonts`, `Archivo[wdth,wght].ttf`), pinned to true static wght=400/700 instances via `fonttools`' `varLib.instancer`, then subsetted both (plus Special Elite's own already-static upstream file — Apache-2.0, not OFL like Archivo) to the Latin-1 + common-punctuation range every PDF label/reference string in this app actually needs via `fonttools.subset` — re-verified against the real French label set with no crash. Embedded as base64 `data:` URLs baked directly into `pdf-fonts.ts`, not a filesystem path: `@react-pdf/font`'s `FontSource._load()` does its own internal `fontkit.open(path)` for a plain path string, a dynamic `fs` call buried in `node_modules` that Next's Vercel output-file-tracer can't discover, so the asset would silently go missing from the serverless bundle in production while still working locally — a `data:` URL is decoded in-memory instead, the same self-hosting precedent as `src/app/fonts/big-shoulders-stencil-display.woff2` (DR-156). **Batched, same session, explicit follow-up request**: the staff dashboard (`/staff/(dashboard)/*`, plus the pre-auth `/staff/login`, `/staff/forbidden`, `/staff/change-password` pages) no longer uses role 1 on-screen either — a new `.staff-shell` wrapper class (added to each of those roots) opts their own `h1`/`h2`/`h3` back to Archivo (`globals.css`, higher specificity than the global `h1,h2,h3 { @apply font-display }` rule), leaving the guest site as the only surface still using the headline stencil face. No schema/permission/module-dependency change — typography only. | V1 |
| DR-162 | 2026-08-20 | **Explicit user request, walked through with several rounds of clarifying questions before any code** ("ask questions, don't assume"): the `content` module is being turned into a real CMS covering staff-editable text *and* media (image/video) across the whole guest site, not just About/FAQ. Scope is too large for one session, so it's split into phases — **this is Phase 1: the structural rename plus the new unified data model, no UI/upload wiring yet.** Confirmed with the user: full rename (module/model/route, not just a label), eventual coverage (Home hero, About+FAQ, Gallery, Package images, "all pages on the guest site"), image compression via `sharp` server-side (deferred to a later phase — not added as a dependency yet), video stored as-is with size/type validation only, no transcoding (also deferred), write access stays SUPERADMIN-only (no rbac change beyond the rename itself). **Rename**: `src/modules/content` → `src/modules/cms` (`contentService`/`contentRepository` → `cmsService`/`cmsRepository`), `/staff/content` → `/staff/cms`, `content.read`/`content.write` → `cms.read`/`cms.write` (`rbac.ts`), `StaffContent` i18n namespace → `StaffCms`, nav label `siteContent`/"Site Content" → `cms`/"CMS" (`settings-items.ts`, `en.json`/`fr.json`). Prisma models `SiteContent`/`FaqEntry` renamed to `CmsTextBlock`/`CmsFaqEntry` — deliberately a **TS-facing/Prisma-Client rename only**: `@@map("site_content")`/`@@map("faq_entries")` are left unchanged, since both tables hold live, guest-facing production content (the About page text and every published FAQ entry) and a real table rename was unnecessary risk for a pure naming change. **New model, additive**: `CmsMediaItem` (`@@map("cms_media_items")`) — the generic image/video building block later phases will use for Home hero slides, Gallery, etc. (`page`, `slotKey` unique pair, `mediaType` a plain zod-validated string rather than a Postgres enum, `url`/`caption`/`sortOrder`), created now with no repository/service method or UI yet since nothing calls it until a specific page is actually wired to it in a later phase — a `tests/cms-media-item.repository.test.ts` round-trip test exercises it directly via the raw Prisma client instead. No RLS policy needed for the new table, same "platform-wide, no `organizationId`" precedent as the two renamed tables (`prisma/rls.sql` comment updated). Explicitly out of scope for this phase, confirmed with the user: `sharp`/webp pipeline, video upload validation, any `CmsMediaItem` upload UI/route, and wiring Home hero/Gallery/Package images/Contact/Terms to the new model — `TourPackage.imageUrl` stays owned by `catalog` (DR-114's `uploadPackageImage`) rather than moving into this module, preserving the module-boundary rule. **DR number note**: assigned DR-162, not DR-161 — DR-161 (directly above, same date) was already written by a concurrent session's uncommitted work by the time this entry was ready to log, the same class of collision DR-154/155/159 hit before. | V1, V4, V5, V8 |
| DR-163 | 2026-08-20 | **Explicit user request, scoped via AskUserQuestion before any code**: Phase 2 of the CMS expansion (DR-162) — the homepage hero carousel (`src/components/HeroCarousel.tsx`, rendered by `(guest)/page.tsx`) is now fully staff-editable from `/staff/cms`, replacing the hardcoded 3-slide `HERO_SLIDES` array (i18n text, local PNGs, hand-written gradients). User confirmed: **dynamic add/remove/reorder** of slides (not fixed at 3), the per-slide **CSS gradient overlay becomes staff-editable** too (explicit trade-off: flexible over design-review-guarded), and the **full image/video pipeline ships now** rather than a placeholder (both deferred in DR-162). **New dependency**: `sharp` `0.34.5` (was already an undeclared transitive dependency at this exact resolved version — pinned explicitly per the charter's exact-version rule rather than left implicit) — `src/lib/public-image-blob.ts`'s `uploadPublicImage`, the one shared primitive every public image upload already goes through (cms's `uploadImage`, catalog's `uploadPackageImage`, and this new hero flow), now always compresses to webp (max edge 2560px, quality 80) before storing; a malformed input throws a new `PublicImageCompressionError`, mapped to a validation error by both existing callers. Catalog's package-image upload gets compressed webp output too as a direct, deliberate side effect of the fix living at this shared level rather than being duplicated per caller — not a separately-scoped change. **Video is a genuinely different upload path**: capped at 25MB (mp4/webm), which exceeds Vercel serverless functions' ~4.5MB request-body limit regardless of the cap chosen, so it uploads directly from the browser to Vercel Blob via `@vercel/blob/client`'s `upload()`/`handleUpload` (already part of the installed `@vercel/blob` `2.6.1`, no new dependency) rather than proxying through a Server Action. New route `src/app/api/v1/cms/media-upload/route.ts` — this module's first REST route, and deliberately **not** wrapped in `withAuth` like every other `/api/v1` route: it's hit by two different callers with two different trust models (the staff browser requesting a token, gated by `cms.write` + a hardcoded SUPERADMIN check; and Vercel's own backend reporting upload completion, authenticated by `handleUpload`'s internal request-signature check, not a staff session cookie — wrapping the whole handler in `withAuth` would 403 that second caller before it ever reached that check). `onUploadCompleted` is a deliberate no-op; the actual `CmsMediaItem` write happens via the browser's own follow-up call to `setHeroSlideMediaAction` once `upload()` resolves, avoiding any race between the two. **Schema** (additive, nullable, both confirmed safe before writing since `cms_media_items` held no rows yet in production and `site_content`'s existing rows all default to null): `CmsTextBlock.eyebrow String?` (a hero slide's 3rd text field, beyond title/body); `CmsMediaItem.overlayGradient String?` (the per-slide gradient, stored with the media since it's a visual property applying to either an image or a video) — and, discovered as a real design gap while building the dynamic add/remove flow, `CmsMediaItem.mediaType`/`url` were corrected from required to nullable (`String?`), since a freshly-added slide can have its text filled in with no media chosen yet, which the original non-nullable columns couldn't represent without a placeholder-URL hack. **Module changes**: `cms/domain.ts` gains `CmsMediaItemView`/`CreateCmsMediaItemInput`/`UpdateCmsMediaItemInput` plus `eyebrow`; `cms/repository.ts` gains `listMediaItems`/`createMediaItem`/`updateMediaItem`/`deleteMediaItem`/`deleteTextBlocksByKey` (`CmsMediaItem`'s first real methods, replacing the placeholder-only state DR-162 left it in); `cms/service.ts` gains the matching RBAC-gated CRUD (`requireCmsWriter`, same layering as FAQ) plus a public `listPublicMediaItems` — a media item's paired per-locale text (if any) lives in `CmsTextBlock` under key `` `${page}.${slotKey}` ``, and `deleteMediaItem` also removes every locale's text row sharing that key so a removed slide doesn't leave orphaned text behind. **Guest rendering**: `(guest)/page.tsx` composes `cmsService.listPublicMediaItems('home-hero')` + `getPublicTextBlock` per slide, falling back per-field to today's hardcoded EN/FR text/local image/gradient **only for the original 3 slots** (fixed slotKeys `sossusvlei`/`virunga`/`victoria-falls`, one-time-seeded in `prisma/seed.ts` with today's exact values so nothing visually changes on ship and staff has real rows to edit from) — a CMS read failure degrades to those same 3 hardcoded defaults rather than a 500, matching the existing `featured` packages section's degradation convention on the same page. `HeroSlide` (`HeroCarousel.tsx`) gains an optional `video`; both `image`/`video` are optional at the type level since a slide has one or the other, never both (`CmsMediaItem` stores one media url+type per slide) — a video slide autoplays muted/looping/`playsInline` unless `prefers-reduced-motion`, in which case it renders paused on its first frame instead (no separate poster asset required). No RLS/organizationId change (same platform-wide precedent as DR-162's tables); `cms.read`/`cms.write` are reused as-is, no new permission. | V1, V4, V5, V8 |

UI: both booking-detail pages (guest + staff) show two plain `<a>` download links (EN/FR) next to the existing invoice card, shown only when `canDownloadInvoicePdf(invoice.status)`, same convention as the staff package-detail page's existing summary-PDF links. | DR-172 | 2026-08-23 | **Explicit user request**: a `TourPackage` can now carry up to 3 images (not just 1), shown as an autoplay slideshow within the guest package card. **Schema**: `TourPackage.imageUrl String?` renamed/retyped to `imageUrls String[] @default([])`. CLAUDE.md's OI-12 note ("no package has a real photo") turned out to be **stale** — the live DB actually had one real staff-uploaded value (`PKG-00040`, "The Nomad Loop"), so this was NOT run as a plain `prisma db push` rename (which would have silently dropped it as a column-type change). Migrated by hand instead: `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN "imageUrls"` (additive) -> `UPDATE ... SET "imageUrls" = ARRAY["imageUrl"] WHERE "imageUrl" IS NOT NULL` (backfill, preserving that one real photo as the new array's first/only element) -> an in-transaction `DO $$ ... RAISE EXCEPTION` row-count sanity check comparing old-non-null-count to new-array-length-1-count before allowing anything destructive -> `ALTER TABLE ... DROP COLUMN "imageUrl"` only after that check passed, all in one transaction. `prisma db push` afterward confirmed "already in sync" (zero further diff), verifying the hand-written SQL produced exactly the column Prisma expects. The cap is enforced in `catalog/domain.ts`'s `CreatePackageInput`/`UpdatePackageInput` zod schemas (`z.array(z.string().max(500)).max(3)`), not a DB constraint. This remains a destructive-shaped change for deploy-timing purposes — per the existing Gotchas entry on destructive schema changes (the DR-088 incident), the shared Neon DB's manual push and this commit's Vercel deploy needed to land close together, in either order, to avoid a window where live code and live schema disagree on the column name; CI is unaffected (its own fresh Postgres runs `db:push` from this same schema.prisma). **New** `src/components/ui/PackageCardSlideshow.tsx` (client component): autoplay crossfade (4s, `framer-motion`, disabled under `prefers-reduced-motion` same as `HeroCarousel`) through `imageUrls` with no nav dots/arrows (unlike `HeroCarousel`) since it's nested inside the card's own `<Link>` to the detail page; collapses to the existing `PackageImage` single-image/gradient rendering whenever there are 0 or 1 images, so an unphotographed package (the common case today) renders exactly as before. Every other single-image call site (`packages/[packageId]/page.tsx`'s detail hero, `opengraph-image.tsx`, `find-booking/result/page.tsx`'s `DepartureTripSummaryView.imageUrl`, which stays intentionally singular) now reads `imageUrls[0] ?? null` as its "cover" image — the slideshow is card-only, not the detail-page hero or a static social preview. **Staff UI**: both `/staff/packages/new` and the package edit page's file input gained `multiple`; the edit page also lists each existing image as a thumbnail with its own "remove" checkbox. The Server Action computes the final list server-side (kept existing + newly uploaded, capped at 3, checked BEFORE uploading anything so a too-many-files mistake never burns a wasted Blob upload) rather than trusting any client-side count. No RLS/permission change (`catalog.write`/`catalog.read`, already held by the same roles, gate this exactly as before) — `catalogService.uploadPackageImage` itself (the single-file upload primitive, DR-114) is unchanged, just called once per file from the action layer now instead of once per submit. | V1, V5 |

The 0-or-1-image fallback path is unchanged (still renders `PackageImage`, now forwarding `children` too so the hero's scrim/title still appears for an unphotographed package). Every other single-image call site DR-172 named (`opengraph-image.tsx`, `find-booking/result/page.tsx`'s `DepartureTripSummaryView.imageUrl`) is intentionally unaffected — those stay static covers, not interactive pages a guest lingers on. | DR-174 | 2026-08-23 | **Real production incident**: staff reported package creation/edit crashing (a raw Next.js "server-side exception" page with a Digest, not a clean error message) after DR-172 shipped. Root-caused to a real, pre-existing gap in both `packages/new/actions.ts` and `packages/[packageId]/actions.ts`: their catch blocks only handled `ApiError` (from `catalogService.uploadPackageImage`/`createPackage`/`updatePackage`), never `ZodError` from `CreatePackageInput.parse`/`UpdatePackageInput.parse` -- any zod validation failure fell through to an unhandled `throw err`, crashing instead of redirecting with `?error=&detail=` like every other expected failure here already does. Fixed by catching `ZodError` too, converting it via `Errors.validation(err.message)` -- the exact same ZodError-to-`validation-failed` mapping `route-guard.ts`'s `withAuth` already applies for JSON API routes, just missing at the Server Action layer. Verification: isolated `catalogService.createPackage`/`updatePackage`/`getPackage` calls (bypassing the Blob upload step, which can't be exercised locally -- OI-15, the public-image store isn't connected for local Development) all succeeded cleanly against the live migrated DB, confirming the domain/repository layer itself is correct; `sharp` JPEG/PNG-to-webp compression also verified working standalone. The exact zod rule that was actually failing in production couldn't be confirmed without Vercel log access (not available in this session) -- this closes the whole class of "any validation failure here crashes instead of showing a message," which is the right fix regardless of which specific rule was tripping it. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V5, V8 |

A find-booking visitor has no session tying their browser to the booking (they may be looking it up from a different device entirely), so ownership can't come from a cookie the way the authenticated route gets it -- instead the new `GET /api/v1/find-booking/invoice-pdf` route (query params `bookingReference`/`lastName`/`locale`, deliberately NOT wrapped in `withAuth`, same "public, no-ctx, does its own trust check" shape as the cms media-upload route) re-runs the exact same two-factor `bookingService.lookupByBookingReference` check the `/find-booking/result` page itself already ran to even show the download link -- complete with that method's own rate-limiting and "never reveal which part was wrong" 404-on-mismatch behavior, rather than trusting a bare `bookingId` in the URL. New `invoicingService.streamInvoicePdfForBookingLookup(organizationId, bookingId, bookingReference, locale)` mirrors `streamInvoicePdf`'s render-payload construction but returns `null` (not throw) when there's nothing downloadable yet, same no-ctx convention as `getBillingSummaryForBookingLookup` -- deliberately duplicated rather than sharing a helper with `streamInvoicePdf`, since the two have different error semantics and the ~15 duplicated lines are simpler than a shared helper with two different "nothing to download" behaviors layered on top. The whole-tour link flattens every day's stops in day order into one `waypoints` list -- Google's own UI silently caps how many stops it will actually route through on a very long circuit, acceptable for "get the driver moving," not a promise every stop renders. | DR-180 | 2026-08-24 | **Explicit user request, scoped via discussion first**: staff now curate exactly which add-ons a `TourPackage` offers on the guest site, instead of every org-active, country-matched `AddonService` showing up uniformly for every package regardless of which one was booked. **Schema**: new `PackageAddonService` join table (`package_addon_services`) -- `TourPackage`/`AddonService` both live in the `catalog` module, so this is a plain intra-module relation, not a cross-module-dependency change. RLS: a standard `organizationId`-scoped `tenant_isolation` policy (mirrors `booking_addons`), plus a new `rls.cross-tenant.package-addon-services.test.ts`. **Backfill decision** (discussed with the user first): `scripts/backfill-package-addons.ts` linked every pre-existing package to every then-active add-on in its own org, one time, immediately after the schema/RLS push -- preserving the exact prior guest-facing behavior on deploy rather than every package suddenly showing zero add-ons until staff manually curated it. **Tailor-made fallback decision** (also discussed first): a `TAILOR_MADE` booking has no resolvable `TourPackage` until `Booking.customizedPackageId` is set post-quote (DR-108) -- the guest add-ons step (`(guest)/booking/[bookingId]/addons/page.tsx`) falls back to the pre-DR-180 org-wide list in that case via a new `bookingService.getBookingPackageId` (mirrors the existing `getBookingCountry`/`resolveBookingCountry` shape), returning `null` pre-quote. `catalogService` gains `setPackageAddons` (replace-all, `catalog.write`, silently drops any addonServiceId that doesn't belong to the caller's own org rather than throwing -- anti-BOLA for a curation step, not a critical write), `getPackageAddonServiceIds` (staff-form prefill), and `listAddonServicesForPackage` (the guest-facing read, `active: true` filtered same as `listActiveAddonServices`, so a since-deactivated selection silently drops off the guest list without needing to be removed from the package). `createPackage`/`updatePackage` both take an additional `addonServiceIds` parameter -- deliberately NOT added to `CreatePackageInput`/`UpdatePackageInput`'s zod schemas, since both are spread directly into the `tourPackage` Prisma call and an unknown field would throw; handled as a separate service parameter instead, applied via a shared `applyPackageAddons` helper. Staff UI: both the package create and edit forms gain an add-ons checkbox checklist (same `SelectableCard` multi-select convention as `tags`/`additionalCountries`) -- leaving every box unchecked means the package offers no add-ons at all, not "show everything" (an empty submitted array is treated as an intentional full replacement on edit, not "leave unchanged," since the checklist always submits its complete current state). New i18n keys (`addons`/`noAddonsAvailable`) added to both `StaffPackages` and `StaffPackageDetail` namespaces in `en.json`/`fr.json`. `POST /api/v1/catalog/packages` and `PATCH /api/v1/catalog/packages/{packageId}` (`docs/openapi.yaml`) gain the same `addonServiceIds` capability for API parity with the staff UI, read from the raw request body rather than the zod schema for the same "spread directly into Prisma" reason; `openapi.yaml`'s `CreatePackageInput` documents the new field, though this schema was already drifted from the real, richer zod shape (missing `tags`/`imageUrls`/`countries`, still listing a removed `priceMinor` requirement) well before this change -- reconciling that pre-existing drift is out of scope here and called out to the user rather than silently expanded. **Follow-up fix (same day, caught by CI)**: `e2e/guest-checkout.spec.ts` timed out waiting for its Visa Assistance checkbox on the guest add-ons step -- its fixture (`e2e/helpers/catalog-fixture.ts`'s `seedPublicDeparture`) created the package and add-on via raw, independent Prisma calls with no `PackageAddonService` link row, so the newly-package-scoped guest page correctly showed nothing. Fixed by having the fixture also create that link row, modeling the real staff workflow rather than loosening the guest page's new curation behavior. The staff-side `/staff/bookings/{id}/addons` page (a different route, used when staff manage a booking's add-ons directly) still calls `listActiveAddonServices` unfiltered by package -- deliberately unchanged, since DR-180's request was scoped to the guest site; noted here as a known asymmetry, not a bug. | V1, V5 |
| DR-182 | 2026-08-24 | **Real production incident, second recurrence of DR-174**: staff still hit a raw server-side crash uploading a package image after DR-174's ZodError fix -- confirmed to be uploading from a phone's Chrome browser, i.e. a real camera photo (routinely 2-8MB), not a pre-resized desktop file. Root cause: this app had **no `experimental.serverActions.bodySizeLimit` configured at all** in `next.config.mjs`, so Next.js's own 1MB default applied -- a request over that size is rejected in Next's own request-parsing layer, *before* `updatePackageAction`/`createPackageAction` ever starts running, so no amount of try/catch inside those actions (DR-174's fix included) could ever have caught it. Raised to `'4mb'`, just under Vercel's own ~4.5MB hard platform ceiling for a serverless function's request body (the same ceiling DR-163's tech-stack note already cites as the reason hero video upload bypasses this entirely via a direct-to-Blob client upload) -- that platform ceiling itself can't be configured away, so a single photo pushing 4-5MB, or 3 photos combined, can still fail; a durable full fix would move package images to the same direct-to-Blob client-upload pattern DR-163 already uses for video, not attempted here. Also added a true catch-all in both package Server Actions' error handling (previously ApiError + DR-174's ZodError only) -- any other exception (a raw Prisma error, or anything unforeseen) now logs server-side with a traceId (surfaced in the redirect's `?detail=` so a future incident is actually diagnosable, unlike this one and DR-174, where no Vercel log access was available to confirm the exact trigger) and redirects with the existing generic "something went wrong" message instead of an unhandled crash -- charter rule 8 ("third-party integrations must not crash the system") applied to an unexpected *internal* error, not just a third-party one. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V5, V9 |

Guest-facing output is unchanged by design (the seeded text is byte-identical to the fallback each page already rendered) -- this only changes what staff sees in the editor. `packages` never had a body fallback at all (the page only rendered `eyebrow`/`title` with no `?? t(...)` for body, since the schema requires a body but the guest page never needed one) -- newly authored for this seed, same voice as the rest: "Browse every package we currently offer — filter by country or search by name." / "Parcourez tous les circuits que nous proposons actuellement — filtrez par pays ou recherchez par nom." `contact.office.{namibia,drc}` never had a single fallback *string* either (the page renders 3 separate translated lines -- address/email/phone "on its way" -- when `body` is null, via a `<dl>`) -- seeded as the closest single-body equivalent (newline-joined, rendered `whitespace-pre-line`) so staff have a real block to overwrite with the actual address once available (OI-02/03), rather than 3 separate hardcoded strings with no edit surface at all. French `plan-my-trip`'s existing "envoyra" typo (should be "enverra") was preserved verbatim rather than silently corrected -- staff can now fix it themselves via the editor, and silently changing wording during a "prefill with current text" pass would blur the line between prefilling and unrequested copy-editing. Run once by hand against the live DB via a throwaway copy of just this loop (not `main()` as a whole, to avoid touching this session's other seed steps) -- confirmed 20/20 rows created, 0 skipped, verifying none of these keys had a prior row (matching the reported "blank in the CMS editor" symptom exactly) -- the `seed.ts` change itself remains for any future fresh `db:setup`/`db:seed` run. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V4 |
| DR-183 | 2026-08-24 | **Real production incident, explicit user report**: sharing a package link (WhatsApp/iMessage/Slack, etc.) showed no picture in the preview card. Root-caused by fetching the live `og:image` route directly for two real packages: a pre-DR-163 PNG-covered package (`PKG-00040`) rendered its real photo correctly, but a post-DR-163 webp-covered package (`PKG-00041`) returned a 200 OK, valid-looking PNG response that was actually a **blank white 1200x630 plate** -- confirmed by rendering both downloaded images directly. Since DR-163, *every* public image upload (package photos included) is compressed to webp; `next/og`'s renderer (Satori) silently fails to decode a webp `<img src>` rather than throwing, so `opengraph-image.tsx`'s real-photo branch (added DR-118/DR-172) has been broken for every package image uploaded since DR-163 shipped, not just this one report -- the fallback-gradient branch was unaffected (never exercised for a package that actually has a photo, so this went unnoticed until a package with a real, recent photo was shared). Fixed by fetching the image server-side and re-encoding it through `sharp` (already a pinned dependency, DR-163) to PNG -- a format Satori is confirmed to handle -- before handing it to `ImageResponse`, wrapped in its own try/catch that falls through to the existing gradient plate on any fetch/decode failure (never crash the social-preview route). Requires `export const runtime = 'nodejs'` on this file (added) since `sharp` needs Node's native bindings, unavailable on the Edge runtime this file defaulted to -- same convention as `cms/media-upload/route.ts`, this repo's other `sharp` consumer. No schema/permission/module-dependency change. | V1, V5 |

| DR-187 | 2026-08-24 | **Explicit user request, extensively scoped via discussion first**: `VisaApplication` now snapshots and tracks the destination country's own government/immigration fee -- distinct from the guest-facing `VISA_ASSISTANCE` add-on's own price (`AddonRate`, DR-128), which was and remains the only thing actually charged through the booking checkout. **Schema**: 5 new columns on `visa_applications` (`governmentFeeMinor Int?`, `governmentFeeCurrency Currency?`, `feePaymentStatus VisaFeePaymentStatus @default(NOT_REQUESTED)`, `feeRequestedAt DateTime?`, `feePaidAt DateTime?`) plus a new 3-value enum `VisaFeePaymentStatus` (`NOT_REQUESTED`/`REQUESTED`/`PAID`) -- additive columns on an existing tenant table, no new RLS policy needed (the table's existing `tenant_isolation` policy is column-agnostic), applied via `db push` directly to the shared dev/production Neon database using the `neondb_owner` credential ephemerally (never written to `.env`), with explicit user confirmation first, same precedent as DR-025/DR-024. **New cross-module dependency, `visa` -> `immigration`** (previously zero inbound dependencies on `immigration` from any module): `immigrationService.getRegulation` requires a staff `ctx` + `country_regulation.read`, unusable from `visa`'s guest-facing/automatic code paths, so a new no-ctx public read, `immigrationService.getPublicFee(country)` (returns just `governmentFeeMinor`/`feeCurrency`, never the regulation's other text/embassy fields -- same data-minimization instinct as the guest visa views), was added mirroring `cms`'s/`weather`'s existing public-read convention. Confirmed acyclic: `immigration` still imports nothing from `visa`. **Snapshot rule** (explicit user decisions): captured at (re)submission time, not at approval, via a shared `resolveGovernmentFee` helper called from `submitApplication`, `autoSubmitOnPassportUpload`, `resubmitApplication`, and `resubmitApplicationForGuest`; frozen once set -- editing a country's fee on `/staff/country-regulations` never retroactively touches an existing application, and there is deliberately no reapply sweep (unlike DR-136/146's rate tables). A resubmission DOES re-snapshot the fee from the country's current value, but **only while `feePaymentStatus` is still `NOT_REQUESTED`** -- a nuance resolved without re-asking the user: once staff has requested or collected the fee, a resubmission must not silently change a number the guest may already have been told or paid. **Collection model** (explicit user decisions): entirely out-of-band -- no `Payment`/`Invoice` row is ever created for this fee (today's `Payment` is strictly 1:1 with a booking's single `Invoice`, with no `VISA_FEE`-shaped `PaymentKind`; extending that was explicitly declined as unwarranted scope), and no push notification fires on a status change (`notify` was explicitly declined too) -- the guest finds out only by checking `/find-booking` or their own booking pages. Two new `visaService` methods, both `visa.process`-gated (no new permission): `requestFeePayment` (`NOT_REQUESTED` -> `REQUESTED`, only) and `markFeePaid` (`REQUESTED` -> `PAID`, only) -- new pure domain predicates `canRequestFeePayment`/`canMarkFeePaid` mirror `canDecide`/`canResubmit`'s shape. If a country has no fee on file, staff can still request/mark paid (explicit user choice) -- shown as "Unspecified" on `/staff/visa-queue`, staff communicate the real amount out-of-band via the existing `contactTraveler` action. No manual per-traveler fee override exists -- always exactly whatever `CountryRegulation` said at the snapshot point (explicit user choice). **New REST routes** (`docs/openapi.yaml` updated): `POST .../visa/fee/request` and `POST .../visa/fee/paid`, same `visa.process` + `findTraveler` anti-BOLA shape as every sibling visa route. **UI**: `/staff/visa-queue` gains a fee column (amount via a new `formatOrPending` call + a `VISA_FEE_PAYMENT_STATUS_TONE` badge) and the two new row actions. Three guest-facing surfaces, per explicit user scoping, all showing the same "this is separate, not charged today, you'll be notified, paying doesn't guarantee a visa" disclaimer copy: the add-ons step (`booking/[bookingId]/addons`, next to the `VISA_ASSISTANCE` option, sourced via `bookingService.getBookingCountry` + `getPublicFee`, reusing the existing country-resolution call already made there for `AddonRate` pricing rather than duplicating `visa`'s own), the booking confirmation/"what's next" page (`booking/[bookingId]`, both the in-progress-setup and fully-set-up branches), and the dedicated `/booking/[bookingId]/visa` page (per-traveler fee amount + status badge, via the now-extended `GuestVisaApplicationView`). **`/find-booking` extension** (explicit user request -- "reflect status on find my booking"): `getStatusForBookingLookup`'s return type widens from a bare `VisaStatus` to a new `BookingLookupVisaView { status, feePaymentStatus }` (one existing call site, `find-booking/result/page.tsx`, updated) -- the fee badge only renders once `feePaymentStatus` is `REQUESTED`/`PAID` (skipping the non-actionable `NOT_REQUESTED` default, explicit user scoping, same "never a bare country-regulation dump" posture DR-064 already established for the status badge itself). Full EN/FR parity added for every new label/disclaimer (`VisaFeePaymentStatusLabel`, plus new keys in `StaffVisaQueue`/`AddonsPage`/`BookingHome`/`BookingVisaPage`/`FindBookingResult`). New tests: `visa.domain.test.ts` (the two new predicates), `visa-auto-submit.test.ts` (a read-only-against-`CountryRegulation` snapshot assertion, deliberately never writing to that platform-wide unscoped table since this suite runs against the one shared dev/production DB and `NA` is a real seeded country, not a disposable fixture), and four new cases in `tests/api/visa.api.test.ts` covering the request/paid transitions, invalid-transition 409s, and TOURIST 403s -- three pre-existing/adjacent tests in this file needed a `40_000` ms timeout bump (up from the 20s default) purely for this sandbox's real-Neon/real-notification-gateway latency, unrelated to correctness. | V1, V4, V6, V7, V8 |

(2) DR-186's PDF letterhead badge bump (`20x20`->`32x32`) was still too small per explicit follow-up -- all four files (invoice, package summary, itinerary summary, map) bumped again to `48x48`; the two files with a fixed-width `companyBlock` column next to the logo (invoice, package summary) also widened that column `190`->`220` to keep the company address block's line-wrap roughly unchanged now that a bigger logo eats more of it. | DR-193 | 2026-08-24 | **Explicit user request**: an "Export PDF" action on the staff Insights dashboard (`/staff/insights`), letting SUPERADMIN/TOUR_OPERATOR/PLATFORM_ADMIN download the currently-viewed executive summary as a PDF rather than only viewing it live. New `insights/insights-pdf.tsx` (`@react-pdf/renderer`, EN/FR, mirrors `finance/package-summary-pdf.tsx`'s shape -- brand logo + company footer via the existing `pdf-fonts.ts`/`brand-logo.ts` primitives, no charts since the live dashboard's rings/donuts/funnels have no server-renderable equivalent here, tables/stat rows instead) + `insightsService.generateDashboardPdf(ctx, range, sections, locale)`, gated by the same two-layer `insights.read` + hardcoded `isInsightsViewer` check as `getDashboardSummary`, which it calls directly so the exported figures can never disagree with what's on screen. New `domain.ts` export `DASHBOARD_SECTION_KEYS`/`DashboardSectionKey`/`isDashboardSectionKey` -- one key per visual section on the live dashboard, so staff can opt out sections they don't want in the download; an invalid/empty `sections` selection falls back to every section rather than an empty report. New thin route `GET /api/v1/insights/pdf` (`?from=&to=&sections=&locale=`, same query shape as the existing polling route plus the two new params), and a new client `ExportPdfButton` (checkbox panel, reusing the page's current date-range/locale) wired into `InsightsDashboardClient.tsx` next to the existing date-range controls. Real CI failure caught and fixed same day: `noDataText`'s `fontStyle: 'italic'` had no registered italic Archivo face in `pdf-fonts.ts` (regular/bold only), so react-pdf threw the instant any section actually had no rows -- local runs never exercised this path (the fixture's own Neon-transaction flakiness silently prevented the new tests from running at all), confirmed fixed via a standalone `renderInsightsPdf` call forcing an empty trend array through the exact broken branch. | V1, V6, V8 |

New `tests/booking-contact-email.test.ts` covers all three cases (TAILOR_MADE resolves via `Booking.contactEmail`, `PREDEFINED_PACKAGE` resolves via the tour lead `Traveler.email`, and a tourist with neither stays unresolved so the caller's own placeholder fallback still applies). No schema/permission/module-dependency change (`Traveler` was already a same-module table). | V1, V6 |
