Root cause: `Booking.customCountry` is `@db.VarChar(2)` (ISO-3166 alpha-2 only), but this fixture passed the file's shared `country` constant (`` `SETUP${Date.now()}`.slice(0, 10) ``, 10 chars) straight through as `customCountry` -- every *other* `customCountry:` fixture in this same file already truncates it (`country.slice(0, 2)`), this one call site (added by DR-222) was simply missed. Fixed by truncating it the same way. That surfaced a second, latent bug the first one had been masking: `getEffectiveEsimRate`'s lookup is keyed off `resolveBookingCountry` -> `booking.customCountry` (2 chars), but the fixture's matching `EsimDataPlanRate` row was seeded under the full untruncated `country` (10 chars) -- once the booking's own value was truncated, the ESIM rate lookup would have started missing (409 instead of 200) had this not also been fixed; the `EsimDataPlanRate` create/cleanup now both use `country.slice(0, 2)` too, matching the booking. Verified in this session's own sandbox (Neon-pooler connectivity was intermittent from here too, consistent with this repo's documented gotcha, but eventually connected): the original `P2000` no longer reproduces, and none of the DR-222 FLIGHT_TICKET/ESIM tests appear among the failures in a full run of the file -- the remaining failures in that run are unrelated sandbox DB-connectivity/transaction-pool timeouts (worsened by a second concurrent session also hitting the same Neon pooler during this investigation), not this bug. No schema/product-code change -- test fixture only. | V6 |
| DR-228 | 2026-09-03 | **User request: French translation of the `/staff/cms` Emails tab, and "French is assumed when the browsing/booking was done in FR."** Two parts. (1) The Emails tab's *content* was already fully bilingual before this DR -- every `EMAIL_TEMPLATE_DEFAULTS` key has both an `EN` and `FR` coded default, `withEmailFallback` already prefilled the editable fields from the right one per the page's own `?locale=` toggle, and `StaffCmsEmail`'s own UI chrome was already translated in `fr.json` -- but one real bug was found and fixed: each accordion `<summary>` label (and the `sectionTitle` passed into `PageTextEditor`) was hardcoded to `EMAIL_TEMPLATE_DEFAULTS[templateKey]?.EN.eyebrow`, so every template's disclosure label stayed in English even while viewing `?locale=fr` -- now reads `.FR.eyebrow` when `locale === 'fr'`, matching `withEmailFallback`'s own locale selection. (2) **Real, more consequential bug found**: nothing in the guest checkout flow ever wrote `User.preferredLocale` -- the field `notify()`/`notifyEmail()` (`notifications/service.ts`) read to decide which language an automated booking/payment/visa email renders in -- so it stayed at the Prisma schema default (`EN`) for every guest booking regardless of what language they actually browsed/booked the site in, silently defeating the FR translations DR-205/217 already built. Fixed: new `src/lib/guest-locale.ts` (`resolveGuestPreferredLocale`) maps the guest-facing `locale` cookie (`en`/`fr`, seeded by `middleware.ts`/`set-locale-action.ts`, the same cookie `src/i18n/request.ts` reads for page chrome) to the Prisma `Locale` enum; all three guest booking-creation Server Actions ((guest)/book/[departureId]/actions.ts, (guest)/book-package/[packageId]/actions.ts, (guest)/plan-my-trip/actions.ts) now pass it into their existing `authService.updateProfile(ctx, {...})` call alongside `name`/`phone` -- the same call already guarded by `!isStaffRole(ctx.roles)` (DR-139), now decoupled from `name` being non-blank so `preferredLocale` is captured even on that edge case (Prisma drops `undefined` data fields, so an unrecognized/missing cookie safely leaves any existing value untouched, same convention `phone`'s ternary already relied on). Snapshotted once at booking-creation time -- since a `User` row persists across a booking's whole lifecycle, every later notification (BOOKING_CONFIRMED, PAYMENT_SUCCEEDED, etc., sent well after this initial write) now correctly renders in whichever language the guest was actually browsing/booking in at that moment. New regression tests in `tests/guest-actions-staff-guard.test.ts` (DR-228 describe block) cover FR/EN/no-cookie cases across all three actions. No schema change (the column and its default already existed, DR-013), no new permission, no module-dependency change. | V1, V6, V8 |
| DR-229 | 2026-09-03 | **Explicit user request, after three same-day production incidents on the same call chain (DR-221, DR-224, DR-226) each patched with a bigger retry budget rather than a real fix: "let's redesign/re-engineer it."** Root cause, restated precisely: `authService.createUser` performed `auth.api.signUpEmail` (better-auth's own atomic INSERT, its own connection) immediately followed by a **separate** `withOrg` transaction's `tx.user.update` (`finalizeAdminCreatedUser`) to set `role`/`phone`/`organizationId`/`emailVerified`/`mustChangePassword` -- two independent writes to the same row, the second racing the first's visibility across a different pooled Neon connection. Every prior DR papered over this by giving the race a bigger window to resolve in; none removed it. **Structural fix**: those fields now land in the *same* INSERT `signUpEmail` already performs, via a new `AsyncLocalStorage`-based "trusted signal" (`src/lib/trusted-user-create.ts`, a plain Node standard-library primitive, not a better-auth internal) that only `authService.createUser` and `scripts/create-staff-user.ts` populate -- read by the existing `databaseHooks.user.create.before` hook (`src/lib/auth.ts`), whose returned `data` is merged into that one atomic INSERT (verified against the actual pinned `better-auth@1.6.23` source, not assumed: `createWithHooks` merges the hook's `data` into `actualData` *before* the single `adapter.create()` call, and this merge happens *after* the route's own hardcoded `emailVerified: false`, so `true` from the hook wins). `finalizeAdminCreatedUser` no longer touches the `User` row at all -- it now only writes `Membership` rows (a related table that can't be folded into the `User` INSERT), and its retry budget reverts from DR-224/226's emergency 10-attempt/~7s bump back to `withTransientRetry`'s shared default (4 attempts/~1.5s) -- the severity that justified the bigger budget (a corrupted/incomplete `User` row) no longer exists once role/org/verification are atomic. **Security reasoning, the actual hard part of this redesign**: `/api/auth/sign-up/email` is a live public route (guest checkout shares the route family) -- the "obvious" fix of registering `role`/`mustChangePassword`/`phone` as `additionalFields` with `input: true` so `signUpEmail`'s own `body` could carry them was rejected as a genuine privilege-escalation hole (any anonymous POST could self-assign `role: 'SUPERADMIN'`). Registered instead with `input: false` (same contract `organizationId` already has) -- this blocks a truthy value from `body` for *every* caller including our own server-side call (verified in better-auth's `parseInputData`: throws `FIELD_NOT_ALLOWED` on a truthy `input:false` field regardless of caller), so the only channel that can set real values is the `AsyncLocalStorage` signal, which a raw HTTP POST body can never reach. Reading the hook's raw `context.request`/`context.body` to distinguish a trusted server call from a public one was investigated and explicitly rejected too -- it works (`context.request` is genuinely `undefined` for a direct `auth.api.*` call vs. a real incoming HTTP `Request` for the mounted route), but it's undocumented internal wiring with no stated contract, an unacceptable foundation for a privilege-escalation boundary; the `AsyncLocalStorage` approach depends on nothing but Node's own standard library. Also investigated and explicitly rejected: bypassing `signUpEmail` entirely via `auth.$context.internalAdapter.createUser` (true full atomicity including `Membership`, but relies on wholly undocumented better-auth internals for the *entire* creation mechanism, not just a config knob -- too much version-bump risk for the marginal gain over the chosen design). **Files changed**: new `src/lib/trusted-user-create.ts`; `src/lib/auth.ts` (three new `additionalFields`, rewritten `databaseHooks.user.create.before`); `src/modules/auth/service.ts` (`createUser` wraps `signUpEmail` in `withTrustedUserCreate`); `src/modules/auth/repository.ts` (`finalizeAdminCreatedUser` narrows to Membership-only, drops `phone` from its input type); `scripts/create-staff-user.ts` (brought into scope per explicit confirmation -- same underlying pattern, now uses the same helper with `mustChangePassword: false` since its password is operator-chosen, not temporary). **What did NOT change**: no Prisma schema migration (`role`/`mustChangePassword`/`phone`/`organizationId` are pre-existing columns; this is a better-auth config-level registration only, same as `organizationId` already was); `prisma/seed.ts` untouched (verified it never goes through `signUpEmail`, only plain `prisma.user.create`/`upsert`); no credential/session row touched. **Explicit user constraint verified held**: `cyberpolco@gmail.com` remains SUPERADMIN with today's exact credentials -- this only changes the code path for *new* account creation going forward. **Verification**: `tests/api/users-admin.api.test.ts` gains a new atomicity-proving test (direct `prisma.user.findUnique`, bypassing `resolveRoles`'s `Membership` dependency, asserting `role`/`organizationId`/`mustChangePassword`/`emailVerified` are already correct the moment `createUser` returns); new `tests/trusted-user-create.test.ts` proves the `AsyncLocalStorage` signal is isolated per-async-context, including under concurrent calls with different signals, and doesn't leak outside its own callback. Every create-user test (multi-role, three-role, VEHICLE_OWNER auto-provisioning, email-conflict, empty-roles, non-admin-403) passed cleanly across two consecutive full runs, alongside the pre-existing, unrelated Neon transaction-timeout flakiness this sandbox has shown all session (`GET /users` 500, two edit-flow `PATCH` timeouts -- confirmed present in run-to-run comparisons *before* this redesign too, on the edit flow this change never touches) -- not a new regression. Guest/anonymous checkout regression-checked via `tests/api/bookings-v2.api.test.ts` (17/18 pass; the one failure is an unrelated booking-refund-lifecycle timeout, same sandbox flakiness class, nothing to do with signup). **Residual, explicitly not fully closed**: `Membership` row creation is still a genuinely separate write (kept idempotent via `skipDuplicates`); the `findUserById` retry-on-null loop at the end of `createUser` is kept, not removed -- the User-row-completeness race it partly covered is gone, but whether a User-row *read* can still independently race the now-atomic User-row *write* (a Neon pooler topology question, e.g. whether a read replica is involved) could not be fully verified by reading code alone in this session; flagged for empirical confirmation by watching Vercel logs post-deploy rather than further speculation. No new `Permission`, no module-dependency change. | V1, V5, V6, V8 |
| DR-230 | 2026-09-03 | **DR-229 follow-up: its own first production deploy failed the build outright** (`vercel logs` on the failed deployment showed `UnhandledSchemeError: Reading from "node:async_hooks" is not handled by plugins (Unhandled scheme)`, import trace `src/lib/trusted-user-create.ts` -> `service.ts` -> `auth/index.ts` -> `role-checkbox-group.tsx`/`edit-user-form.tsx`). Production was never actually down -- Vercel kept serving the prior working deployment throughout, since a failed build never replaces a live one -- but DR-229 itself hadn't actually shipped. **Root cause**: the exact same class of problem DR-163 already hit and fixed for `sharp` -- `auth/index.ts`'s barrel exports `authService` as one big object literal alongside pure domain utilities (`findIncompatibleRolePair`/`ASSIGNABLE_ROLES`), and webpack's module-resolution stage must successfully resolve a barrel's *entire* re-exported graph before any dead-code elimination can drop an unused export -- so a `'use client'` file (`role-checkbox-group.tsx`, `edit-user-form.tsx`) importing only the domain utilities from `@modules/auth` still forced webpack to resolve `service.ts`'s new `trusted-user-create.ts` import, whose `node:async_hooks` specifier has no built-in webpack fallback at all (unlike a bare Node builtin, which webpack's client compiler silently stubs by default) and hard-fails resolution outright. **Fix, same DR-163 pattern exactly**: `src/lib/trusted-user-create.ts`'s import changed from `'node:async_hooks'` to the bare `'async_hooks'` specifier (avoids the "Unhandled scheme" class of error entirely, since bare specifiers go through normal resolution where a fallback/alias can apply); `next.config.mjs`'s existing client-only `webpack()` override (which already aliased `sharp` to `false`) now also aliases `async_hooks` to `false` for the client compiler pass -- belt-and-suspenders, since nothing in a client bundle ever actually calls `AsyncLocalStorage`-dependent code. **Verification, not another guess**: pushed to a throwaway branch (`fix/dr229-webpack-build`) first and confirmed its Vercel Preview deployment built successfully (`Build Completed`, all routes compiled, `status: Ready`) before merging to `main` -- deliberately not a second blind push to `main`, and not a local `npm run build` either (this sandbox has previously strained the user's machine running that command). Production's own post-merge deployment then also completed successfully. No code-behavior change from DR-229's design -- purely a build/bundling fix. | V6 |
| DR-231 | 2026-09-03 | **DR-229 follow-up: the very first real production create-user attempt after DR-230's fix deployed still failed** (`vercel logs`: `POST /staff/admin/users` -> `PrismaClientKnownRequestError`, `Foreign key constraint violated: organization_members_userId_fkey`, code `P2003`, traced to `finalizeAdminCreatedUser` -> `tx.membership.createMany`, digest `2359851205`) -- twice, ~24s apart, both raw uncaught crashes (the generic Next.js error boundary page, not even the app's own inline form error). **Root cause**: this is the exact residual risk DR-229's own decision-log entry already flagged as not fully closed -- `finalizeAdminCreatedUser`'s `Membership.createMany` is a genuinely separate write from `signUpEmail`'s now-atomic `User` INSERT, and its `userId` foreign key can race that INSERT's visibility across the same pooled Neon connection every DR in this chain (221/224/226) has hit -- just surfacing as `P2003` here (an INSERT+FK-check) instead of `P2025` (an UPDATE), since this is a `createMany` referencing a `userId`, not an update targeting one. **Compounding gap**: `isTransientDbError` (`src/lib/db.ts`) only recognizes `P2025`/`P2028`, so `P2003` wasn't retried at all (failed on the very first attempt, zero backoff) and wasn't reclassified as `Errors.internal()` either -- the raw `PrismaClientKnownRequestError` propagated all the way to Next.js's generic error boundary. **Fix**: `withTransientRetry` (`src/lib/db.ts`) gains an optional third parameter, a custom `isRetryable` predicate defaulting to `isTransientDbError` -- deliberately not broadening the shared default itself, since blindly retrying every `P2003` for every future caller could mask a genuine referential-integrity bug elsewhere; only 2 callers of this helper exist today (this one, and `createUser`'s `audit()` call, unaffected). `finalizeAdminCreatedUser` passes its own scoped predicate (`isTransientDbError(e) || code === 'P2003'`), justified narrowly: the `userId` in question was returned by a `signUpEmail` call one line above in the same function call, so a `P2003` against that exact id can only mean "not visible yet," never "doesn't exist" -- and reuses the same predicate in its `catch` block so an exhausted retry still surfaces as a clean `Errors.internal()`, not a raw Prisma error. Retry budget bumped from the shared 4-attempt/~1.5s default straight to DR-224/226's 10-attempt/~7s budget rather than starting conservative -- two consecutive real production failures ~24s apart the same day is evidence this specific window needs real patience right now, not a rare edge case worth a smaller budget. Verified: all 7 create-user tests in `tests/api/users-admin.api.test.ts` (multi-role, three-role, atomicity, VEHICLE_OWNER, email-conflict, empty-roles, non-admin-403) pass cleanly; the DB queried directly for any orphaned `User` row left by the two failed attempts (a real concern, since `signUpEmail`'s insert should be durable independent of the later Membership failure) -- none found under any real (non-test) email in the lookback window, itself a data point worth more scrutiny if this recurs, not fully explained in this session. No schema change, no new permission, no module-dependency change. | V6, V8 |
| DR-232 | 2026-09-03 | **DR-230 follow-up: reported as a full-page crash just loading `/staff/admin/users` (no create-user submission involved), with no matching server-side error in `vercel logs`** -- the absence of a server log was itself the clue this was a client-side (browser) crash, not another backend race. **Root cause**: DR-230's fix (`config.resolve.alias.async_hooks = false` for the client webpack build, so the bundle reachable from `auth/index.ts`'s barrel compiles instead of hard-failing on the `node:` URI scheme) makes the build succeed, but `src/lib/trusted-user-create.ts` constructed `new AsyncLocalStorage()` at **module top level** -- that statement runs the instant the client bundle *evaluates* the module (defining `withTrustedUserCreate`/`getTrustedUserCreateSignal`), regardless of whether either function is ever called, and in that bundle `AsyncLocalStorage` is `undefined` (webpack's alias-to-`false` stub has no named exports), so `new undefined()` throws immediately -- crashing the instant `/staff/admin/users` (which renders `create-user-form.tsx` -> `role-checkbox-group.tsx`) loads in the browser, with nothing for the server to log since the exception never reaches it. This is the one real difference from DR-163's `sharp` precedent this whole chain has been modeling itself on: `sharp` is only ever invoked *inside a function body* a client component never calls, so an `undefined` stub is harmless until called; `trusted-user-create.ts`'s constructor ran unconditionally at import time, which no prior DR in this chain had reasoned through. **Fix**: the `AsyncLocalStorage` instance is now constructed lazily, on first real call inside `getStorage()`, not at module scope -- so client-bundle module evaluation (defining the functions) never touches the stubbed-out constructor, since neither function is ever actually invoked from client code. **Verification, escalating beyond a Preview build this time** (a successful build doesn't prove a bundle doesn't crash at runtime in a browser, which is exactly what DR-230's Preview check missed): pushed to a throwaway branch (`fix/dr232-async-storage-lazy-init`) and confirmed its own Preview build succeeded, **then** added `tests/trusted-user-create-client-bundle.test.ts`, which `vi.mock`s `async_hooks` to export `{ AsyncLocalStorage: undefined }` -- the exact condition the client webpack alias produces -- and proves (1) merely importing the module no longer throws under that condition (the actual bug, now closed) and (2) calling `withTrustedUserCreate` under that same condition still throws, but only on actual invocation, not on import (confirming the fix's precise mechanism, not just its symptom). A live-browser Playwright check against the Preview URL was attempted first but blocked by Vercel's own account-level deployment-protection SSO wall (a project setting, not something to change unilaterally to get a test to run) -- the `vi.mock`-based test above is the verification that replaced it, and is now a permanent regression test for this exact class of bug, not just a one-off check. No schema change, no new permission, no module-dependency change; also no code-behavior change from DR-229's actual design, same as DR-230 -- purely a client-bundle-safety fix. | V6 |
| DR-233 | 2026-09-04 | **DR-229 follow-up: real production repro of the exact residual risk that DR-229's own entry flagged as "investigated but not fully confirmed" -- a User-row *read* racing the now-atomic User-row *write* across Neon's connection pooler.** User-reported symptom: creating a staff account with roles reliably returned a generic "Something went wrong" error on submit, with no console error and no server crash. Traced to `authService.createUser`'s final read-back loop (`src/modules/auth/service.ts`): after `signUpEmail` and `finalizeAdminCreatedUser` (Membership rows) both commit successfully, the function re-reads the new user via `authRepository.findUserById` to return it to the caller, retrying on a null result -- but the loop's budget was only 4 attempts / ~900ms total, too short for this pooler's real visibility lag under production load. When it exhausted, `createUser` threw `Errors.internal()` ("Something went wrong") even though the account (with every selected role) had already committed successfully one line above -- the staff member saw a failure, but the account silently existed with an unknown temporary password (generated in-memory, lost on this throw before the one-time display/email step). A retry through the UI would then hit the pre-existing-email conflict check instead, compounding the confusion. **Fix**: bumped the retry loop from 4 attempts/~900ms to DR-224/226's already-proven 10-attempt/~7s budget -- the same Neon-pooler-read-lag class of problem this exact call chain has now needed a bigger window for three separate times. No schema change, no new permission, no module-dependency change, no test assertion depended on the old attempt count. **Residual**: a true "always correct" fix would return success information available at the write step itself rather than depending on a follow-up read succeeding at all -- not pursued here, since the existing accounts created during this bug's window needed identifying/cleaning up first (see the Gotchas entry on the same date for the unrelated but co-discovered test-data-in-production issue) and a full redesign wasn't requested. | V6, V8 |
| DR-235 | 2026-09-04 | **Real production incident, discovered while cleaning up guest-site test packages: 432 leftover test-fixture `Organization` rows had accumulated in the shared Neon DB over roughly a year** (`tests/api/*.test.ts` naming convention, e.g. `FLEET-API-TEST-1785580375687`), found by a manual `withOrg`-scoped audit and hand-deleted as a one-time cleanup (432/432 succeeded, 0 failures; a further 8 dangling `User` rows -- `User.organizationId` has no FK/cascade to `Organization` -- turned out to already be gone, apparently cleaned up by their own test's teardown before this pass reached them). Distinct from DR-233's co-discovered `@example.test`-user/`TEST-*`-package cleanup in the real Lam org -- this one is about throwaway `Organization` rows accumulating outside Lam entirely. **Root cause**: ~97 `tests/api/*.test.ts` files create their own throwaway `Organization` (and clean it up in their own `afterAll`) with zero retry against the transient Neon connectivity blips this repo's own Gotchas section already documents as expected ("Prisma's query engine intermittently can't reach the Neon pooler... treat as transient and retry") -- a blip during `afterAll` throws before `organization.delete()` runs, orphaning the org permanently. A second, worse bug compounds this in several `*.security.test.ts` files: they create two orgs via `Promise.all([...])` and only assign both id variables *after* the whole promise resolves, so if either create call fails, the successfully-created org's id is never captured in a JS variable at all -- `afterAll`'s own "don't wipe production if setup half-failed" guard (added after the real `users`-table-wipe incidents this file's Gotchas section already documents) then intentionally skips cleanup entirely, permanently orphaning that org with no reference left to clean it up. `vitest.config.ts`'s `hookTimeout: 20000` is a third, independent leak vector (a slow-but-working multi-`deleteMany` teardown can be killed mid-cleanup). No CI step or scheduled job existed to catch any of this. **Fix, scoped to a safety net rather than retrofitting ~97 test files** (explicit user choice): a new `src/lib/test-org-purge.ts` (`purgeStaleTestOrganizations`) finds every non-primary `Organization` whose name matches the exact synthetic test-fixture naming shape (`^[A-Z0-9]+(?:-[A-Z0-9]+)*-\d{13}$` -- uppercase segments ending in a `Date.now()` millisecond suffix) and is older than **1 hour** (explicit user choice over an initially-proposed 24h), then deletes it inside a `withTransientRetry`-wrapped, `app.org_id`-scoped transaction so Postgres `ON DELETE CASCADE` cleans up every RLS-scoped table underneath it, same mechanism the manual cleanup used. Deliberately **not** "any non-primary org" -- DR-005 keeps multi-tenancy live specifically so a second real operator can onboard with no migration; a real operator's name is vanishingly unlikely to collide with this exact synthetic shape, but a bare `isPrimary` check would delete a genuine new tenant a day after onboarding. New scheduled job `/api/jobs/sweep-test-orgs` (QStash-signature-gated, same shape as every other `sweep-*`/`purge-*` job), registered hourly (`polco-sweep-test-orgs`, `0 * * * *`) in `scripts/register-qstash-schedule.ts` -- not yet actually registered against the live QStash account (that's a manual `npm run qstash:register-schedule` step, same as every prior schedule addition). Tests: `tests/api/jobs-sweep-test-orgs.api.test.ts` (route-level, mocked, mirrors `jobs-sweep-bookings.api.test.ts`) and `tests/test-org-purge.test.ts` (a real integration test against the shared DB -- unlike `authRepository.markDormantUsers`, deliberately left untested there per that file's own trailing comment, this function is safe to test for real since it can only ever match the synthetic naming pattern + primary-org exclusion, confirmed by creating one backdated stale test-named org, one fresh test-named org, and one backdated real-looking-named org, and asserting only the first is gone). No schema change, no new permission, no module-dependency change (`Organization` has no owning module, same "shared, no owning module" precedent `averageRating`'s own schema comment already documents). Numbered DR-235 (skipping DR-234, reserved by a concurrent session's in-progress create-user diagnostic-logging commit) to avoid a decision-log collision -- coordinated live via a cross-session message during this same investigation. | V4 |
| DR-234 | 2026-09-04 | **DR-233 follow-up: the retry-budget fix did not resolve the user's actual reported symptom** -- production logs (`vercel logs`) showed `finalizeAdminCreatedUser`'s `Membership.createMany` failing with `P2003` on **every one of its 10 retry attempts**, not intermittently, meaning `signUpEmail`'s User-row write was never actually visible at all, not merely slow to become visible. Temporary diagnostic logging (`src/lib/auth.ts`'s hook, `src/modules/auth/service.ts`'s `createUser`) was added, deployed, and used live (with the user reproducing the failure on request) to capture exactly what `signUpEmail` returned: `resultUserOrgId`/`resultUserRole` both `null`, and a direct same-connection re-read of the returned id (`immediateCheckFound`) came back `false` -- ruling out cross-connection pooler visibility lag entirely (proven with the *same* Prisma client, not a different one) and pointing at the write itself never landing. This diagnostic work is what led directly to DR-236's actual root cause. The temporary logging was removed once root-caused, per its own inline comment; no lasting code change from this entry itself. | V6 |
| DR-236 | 2026-09-04 | **DR-234's actual root cause, found once the user reported the failure was 100% reproducible for one specific email (`latunakou11@gmail.com`) and immediately succeeded with any other**: that email already had a `User` row -- a `TOUR_GUIDE` staff account permanently deleted 2026-08-18 (`deletedPermanently: true`). `User.email`'s database unique constraint applies regardless of `deletedAt`/`deletedPermanently`, but `authService.createUser`'s pre-check used `findUserByEmail`, which deliberately treats any `deletedAt`-set row as "not found" (correct for its *other* callers, e.g. `resolveSession`) -- so the pre-check reported the email as available, `signUpEmail`'s real INSERT then failed on the actual unique index, and better-auth returned an optimistic success result with a freshly generated id that was never persisted, surfacing minutes later as the confusing `finalizeAdminCreatedUser` foreign-key crash this whole DR-229/233/234 chain had been chasing. **Fix, two parts**: (1) `createUser` now calls a new `authRepository.findUserByEmailIncludingDeleted` (mirrors the existing `findUserByIdIncludingDeleted` precedent) so a conflicting email of *any* deletion state is caught immediately with a clear, specific message (active: "already exists"; merely deactivated: "reactivate it instead"; permanently deleted: "cannot be reused") instead of a crash three steps later. (2) **Explicit user direction, not just a clearer error**: a permanently deleted account must not tie up its email forever -- `authRepository.permanentlyDeleteUser` now also rewrites the row's own `email` to a synthetic `deleted-<id>@deleted.invalid` value in the same update, freeing the original for a genuinely new, unrelated account going forward. The original email is preserved in the `auth.user_deleted` audit log's metadata rather than lost. Deliberately **not** applied to `softDeleteUser` (mere Deactivation) -- that account can still be reactivated, and must keep resolving by its real email to do so; only `deleteUser`'s permanent path is affected, and `deleteClient` (DR-036/085) uses `softDeleteUser`, so client/tourist records are untouched by this change. **One-time backfill**: 24 already-permanently-deleted rows in the real Lam org were still holding their original email at the time this shipped (found via a direct query, not guessed) -- `driver1-10@polcotours.com`, `guide1-10@polcotours.com`, `visa@polcotours.com`, `latunakou11@gmail.com`, `josephatunakou@gmail.com`, `faustin@mufasasafaris.com` -- all rewritten the same way, immediately unblocking any future account creation under those emails (this fix would otherwise only apply going forward, to deletions performed after this shipped). New regression test in `tests/api/users-admin.api.test.ts` covers the full cycle: permanently delete a fixture account, confirm its own email no longer matches the original, then confirm a brand new account using that exact original email succeeds (201) with a distinct user id. No schema change (no new column; `email` already existed and is a plain `String @unique`, not nullable), no new permission, no module-dependency change. | V6, V8 |
| DR-237 | 2026-09-04 | **Explicit user direction, follow-up to DR-236**: an email already belonging to a bare `TOURIST` record (DR-036 -- a login-less contact row auto-created from a guest booking or a staff-entered client email, never a real account) was, before this DR, lumped into the same generic "A user with this email already exists" conflict as any other duplicate -- misleading, since there's no actual login/account to conflict with, just a customer contact record. `authService.createUser`'s (and, for the same reason, `updateUser`'s email-change path's) conflict check now gives this case its own specific message ("This email belongs to an existing guest/tourist record (from a booking), not a staff account.") rather than the generic one, using the already-fetched `existing.role === 'TOURIST'` check -- explicit user choice of **blocking with a clear message**, the same shape as the deactivated/permanently-deleted branches DR-236 already established, over the two alternatives considered (silently allow-and-audit-log, or upgrade the existing tourist record in place to hold the new staff role) -- kept deliberately simple rather than guessing at a more involved behavior change. `updateUser`'s own email-conflict check is also upgraded from the plain (deletedAt-excluding) `findUserByEmail` to `findUserByEmailIncludingDeleted` in the same pass, for the identical DR-236 reasoning -- it had the same latent bug, just not yet reported. New regression test: creating a staff account with an existing TOURIST's email 409s with a message matching `/guest\/tourist record/i`. No schema change, no new permission, no module-dependency change. | V6, V8 |
| DR-238 | 2026-09-04 | **Explicit user request: FLIGHT_TICKET/ESIM (DR-222) needed to actually reach staff package setup and the guest plan-my-trip wizard, not just the already-working guest package-booking add-ons step.** Investigated before changing anything, per explicit instruction not to assume: DR-222's guest add-ons step (`(guest)/booking/[bookingId]/addons`, `bookingService.setAddons`) was already fully wired end to end -- pickers, variant fields, pricing, backend validation, untouched by this DR. Two real gaps found instead. **(1)** No `AddonService` catalog row of code `FLIGHT_TICKET` or `ESIM` existed anywhere -- `prisma/seed.ts`'s add-on list only ever seeded the original 4 codes (PHOTOGRAPHY/VIDEOGRAPHY/TRANSLATOR/VISA_ASSISTANCE), and no admin CRUD exists to create an `AddonService` row through the UI (confirmed: no route, no page). Staff package setup's add-on checkboxes (`packages/new`, `packages/[packageId]`) already list any *active* `AddonService` row with zero code-based filtering -- so this alone, not a UI gap, was why flight/e-sim never appeared there: there was nothing to list. Fixed by adding both codes to `prisma/seed.ts`'s seeded add-on array, `priceMinor: 0` (a required-but-unused placeholder for these two -- their real price always comes from `FlightFareRate`/`EsimDataPlanRate` via a guest-picked variant, resolved live in `bookingService.setAddons`, same as documented in that method's own DR-222 comment). **(2)** plan-my-trip's "interested in?" add-on checklist -- guest-facing (`(guest)/plan-my-trip/plan-my-trip-form.tsx`) and its explicit staff mirror (`staff/(dashboard)/bookings/new/staff-plan-my-trip-form.tsx`, kept deliberately identical to the guest wizard per its own file comment) -- each hand-syncs a local `ADDONS`/`ADDON_CODES` vocabulary (documented as a deliberate hand-copy in both files' own comments, since `catalog`/`booking` don't export a shared zod-validating `AddonCode` list) that was never updated when DR-222 added the two new codes to the real `AddonCode` enum; `booking/domain.ts`'s own `ADDON_CODES` copy (backing `CreateTailorMadeInput`'s zod schema) had the identical gap, so the server would have rejected either value via zod even if a client had somehow sent one. Fixed by adding `FLIGHT_TICKET`/`ESIM` to all three copies. Scope decision (asked, not assumed): plan-my-trip's add-on step is explicitly a lightweight "interested in?" checkbox for every code, with no price/date/variant capture at all -- explicit user confirmation that this same lightweight treatment is correct for flight/e-sim too, not a full priced picker, since plan-my-trip (TAILOR_MADE) has no fixed package/route/dates locked in yet for a route or data-plan lookup to resolve against; the full priced picker already exists once staff quotes the trip and it becomes a real booking, via the untouched DR-222 flow. `en.json`/`fr.json`'s `TripAddons.FLIGHT_TICKET`/`ESIM` keys already existed (added ahead of the constant-list fix, apparently in anticipation) -- no i18n change needed. New test: `tests/booking.domain.test.ts` asserts `CreateTailorMadeInput` now accepts both codes in `preferredAddons`. Operational-costs/cost-breakdown pages (package-level and booking-level) were checked and confirmed to show no add-on of any code, old or new -- a pre-existing scope boundary, not something this DR changed or needed to change. Seeded the two new `AddonService` rows into the shared Neon DB via `npm run db:seed` with explicit user go-ahead (idempotent upsert-by-code pattern already used by the existing 4 rows); any throwaway test bookings/data created to verify the flow end-to-end were deleted afterward per explicit user instruction to always clean up test data post-verification, going forward. No schema change, no new permission, no module-dependency change. Numbered DR-238, after three live renumberings during this same session as concurrent sessions kept claiming lower numbers first (233/234 by already-committed create-user-investigation commits, 235 by a concurrent test-org-sweep commit, then 236 and 237 both landed as genuine create-user-related fixes mid-session) -- coordinated live via cross-session messages throughout. | V4, V8 |
| DR-239 | 2026-09-04 | **User-reported: Resend flagged the domain's own "Needs attention" deliverability check with two specific findings against `src/modules/notifications/email-template.ts`'s shared branded HTML shell (DR-205), which every one of the 26 `NotificationEvent` templates renders through.** (1) The brand logo `<img>` used `BRAND_LOGO_DATA_URI` (`src/lib/brand-logo.ts`) -- a base64 `data:` URI, the same primitive every downloadable PDF and the package-page `opengraph-image.tsx` fallback already share, chosen there specifically because Vercel's output-file-tracer can't reliably discover a runtime `fs` read against a `public/` path from inside a serverless function bundle. That constraint doesn't apply to a plain HTML email (no server-side binary rendering step to trace), and Resend/Gmail flag an inline `data:` image in email HTML as a deliverability risk -- fixed by pointing the `<img src>` at a real hosted URL instead, `https://mufasasafaris.com/images/brand/mufasa-logo.png` (the same `public/images/brand/mufasa-logo.png` file `Logo.tsx` already serves), matching the same "hardcode an absolute mufasasafaris.com URL" convention `domain.ts`'s own `FIND_BOOKING_URL` already uses for its CTA links. (2) The footer's "Powered by Cyber PolCo" credit line (`POWERED_BY`, explicit user request per its own comment, same "a Cyber PolCo Product" attribution as the guest site's own footer legal line, DR-204/214) linked to `https://www.cyberpolco.com` -- a different domain than the sending domain, which Resend also flagged directly. **Explicit user choice, asked rather than assumed** (three options presented: drop the link but keep the text, leave it live and accept the risk, or remove the credit line entirely): keep the attribution text, drop the hyperlink -- `POWERED_BY` is now a plain styled `<span>`, not an `<a>`. Verified the flagged domain in a separate bounce report the same day (`temp@xi7q1oinav4culzsjxy7kzcba1zurrtm.com`, `550 4.4.7 Message expired... 421 Unable to connect to remote host`) has no MX/A/NS records at all -- unrelated to either finding above, almost certainly a guest-entered disposable/fake email address, not a sender-side or DNS-config problem. No schema change, no new permission, no module-dependency change; `src/lib/brand-logo.ts` itself is untouched and still correctly used by every PDF/opengraph-image caller. | V6 |

## How to add a decision

1. Append a `DR-nnn` row above with today's date and the affected volumes.
2. Update the relevant volume section(s) in the master design package.
3. Reference the `DR-nnn` id in your PR description (the template has a checkbox).
